## Phase 6c: sub-agent run fixes (Go only, 2026-11-02)

Audit: `audit_findings/p6c_merged.md` (batch (i): P6CM-04, 07, 02, 08).

- **A restored run's answer is kept whole, whoever reads it first (P6CM-07).** `loadText` read the transcript once but cut the answer to the first caller's limit and set `cut`, and `Detail` passed the async status limit (4096), so one `async status` after a restart left 4 KiB of a 21 KB answer for the life of the process and told every later reader it was cut. A reader that arrived during the first read also saw the flag set and got an empty text. The read is now a `sync.Once` (a second reader waits for the first), stores the whole text and leaves `cut` as the manifest had it; `Detail` already cuts what it returns. No schema or text change. Test: `TestSubagentRestoredAnswerIsNotPinnedToTheFirstReadersLimit`.
- **A panic after the launcher call fails the run instead of ending the server (P6CM-04).** `runLauncher` was the phase's only `recover`; the process sweep, `finish` and its bookkeeping in `execute` ran outside it, so a panic there ended the process and left the handle running. `execute` now defers `recoverRun`: it logs the value and stack (`slog.Error`, as 6b's `waitForProcess` and the async run do) and finishes the run as `failed` with the reason `internal error`; if `finish` had already stamped the end, it only closes `done` so no waiter hangs (a half-run `finish` may leave a counter or the manifest behind; the process stays up). The launcher's recovered value is logged with its stack too, and **model-visible text changes**: the failed run's reason was `sub-agent launcher panicked: <value>` and is now `sub-agent launcher panicked`, because the value can hold paths and goes into the manifest and the remote API. Tests: `TestSubagentPanicAfterTheLauncherFailsTheRunInsteadOfEndingTheServer`, `TestSubagentLauncherPanicTextStaysOutOfTheRunReason`.
- **Cancelling an inline remote sub-agent call returns at once (P6CM-02).** The inline branch of `startRemote` called `run.Stop` and then waited on `run.Done()`, but `Stop` only fires a best-effort HTTP stop; `Done` closes when the follower sees a terminal state, `ErrLost` (60 s by default) or the local timeout, so a user stop or reset against a slow or partitioned remote hung the turn for up to that long. It now returns `ctx.Err()` right after `Stop`, as `profile_message`'s `runInline` does; the follower goroutine still ends on its own and records the outcome. No text or schema change. Test: `TestRemoteSubagentInlineCancelDoesNotWaitForAnUnreachableRemote`.
- **A remote start the hub refuses stops the run it just started (P6CM-08).** `startRemoteRun` started the run on the remote and only then registered its follower; when the hub had closed in between, `directory.Go` refused and the helper returned "the server is shutting down" with nothing left that could stop the remote run (a sub-agent that can change files kept going, unreported). It now sends one `StopRun` bounded by `stopCallTimeout` (3 s, inline, so a shutdown start waits at most that long) before returning. The helper serves both remote sub-agents and remote profile messages, so both are covered. **Model-visible text changes:** the error message is now "the server is shutting down; the remote run was asked to stop" (code `remote_unavailable` unchanged). Test: `TestRemoteSubagentStartRefusedByAClosedHubStopsTheRemoteRun`; the fake peer gained an `onSubagentStart` hook to close the hub inside the window.
- **A nested run belongs to its session; its handle to the run that started it (P6CM-03).** `subagentRun.owner` held the handle owner, so a child's run had the parent run id as its owner: the session's `list`, `CancelOwner`, the state context and the transcript gate (`RunTranscript`, `transcript_path.go`) all missed it, and a restart lost the session because the manifest had no `session`. `owner` is the session key now for every run, and a new `handleOwner` keeps the async handle under the parent run id (the 2026-10-22 rule, unchanged). The manifest gains `session` and `root` (`omitempty`, version still 1): a manifest without them loads with both empty, and its owner is the only key, as before. **Model-visible text is unchanged.** Tests: `TestNestedRunBelongsToTheSessionThatStartedTheTree`, `TestSubagentManifestKeepsTheSessionAndRootOfANestedRun`.
- **A remote sub-agent start honours `max_depth` and the run caps (P6CM-01, reversed 2026-11-01).** `startRemote` checked nothing of the run scope, so a child at the configured depth could start remote agents that a local start at that depth refuses. A nested remote start now passes the same gate a local nested start does: `subagentTool.depthExceeded` first, then `SubagentManager.admitNestedLocked` (the depth, then `max_runs_per_root` and `max_runs_per_session`), which `start` also calls. A remote start past the depth returns `subagent_depth_exceeded`; one past a cap returns `subagent_limit_reached`. **What is counted:** a remote run takes a place in the root and session counts (`reserveRemote`) for its lifetime, with no local run record, slot or queue place; the place is released once when the remote run ends, on every path (completion, failure, stop, timeout, cancel) and on a start that fails or panics (`releaseRemote`, guarded by `sync.Once`). **Scope, by decision:** the caps count this host's own resources only: a remote run counts against the caps of the host that runs it, and the other host counts its own. **Not visible:** `list`, `async` and `stop` do not show a reserved place. **Restart:** the counts are in memory only and are not in the manifest, so a restart drops the remote places with the work they belong to. **Previous decision, kept for the reason it was made:** the caps had counted local runs only, on the ground that a remote run is not a local slot. That reason is overridden by the ruling above, which asks this host's caps to bound what this host runs. Tests: `TestRemoteSubagentStartHonoursMaxDepth`, `TestRemoteSubagentStartIsRefusedAtTheSessionRunCap`, `TestRemoteSubagentStartIsRefusedAtTheRootRunCap`, `TestRemoteRunReleasesItsCapSlotOnEveryEndPath`, `TestRemoteRunReleasesItsCapSlotOnCancel`, `TestReleaseRemoteTwiceDoesNotFreeAnotherRunsPlace`.
- **The changed-file list is bounded in bytes, and the async summary stays within the run's limit (P6CM-06, P6CM-24).** `written_files` had a count cap (`changed_files_max`) and no byte cap, so 100 long paths were a 21 KB tool result, re-sent every round. A file is now kept only while the list it would make stays within `tools.native.subagent.result_max_bytes` (the same setting the answer uses; no new key), and every file that does not fit is counted in `written_files_omitted`. The list is cut when it is recorded, so a restored run keeps the bounded list it was saved with; a manifest written before this change keeps its unbounded list. **Budget choice, not the 200-byte helper:** 200 bytes would have kept almost none of a writer's list; the list has its own budget, so an answer and a list can each reach the setting (at most twice it per result). **Async summary (P6CM-24):** the files line still comes first, and the answer is cut to what the run limit leaves after it, so the handle's summary no longer goes 200 bytes past `result_max_bytes`; the manager's `summary_max_bytes` cut still applies after. **Model-visible:** the `written_files` value and `written_files_omitted` change only when the list is over the limit; a shorter list renders as before. No golden file contains either text (checked by grep of `internal/**`). Tests: `TestSubagentChangedFilesListIsCutToTheResultLimit`, `TestSubagentChangedFilesByteCountIsTheLengthOfTheList`, `TestSubagentAsyncSummaryIsNotPushedPastTheResultLimit`.
- **Closing a sub-agent manager waits 2 s for its children, then logs and returns (P6CM-05).** `SubagentManager.Close` waited on the child goroutines with no bound, so a child that ignored its cancellation kept shutdown from finishing. It now waits up to the package constant `closeGrace` (2 s, the bash kill-deadline default), then logs the ids still going with `slog.Warn` and returns; those children are left behind. The bound is a fixed constant, not a setting, and it does not read `tools.native.process.kill_grace_ms`, which is absent when the process tool is not configured. No model-visible text changes.
- **A finished sub-agent run drops its manifest after the manager lock is released (P6CM-13).** `finish` and the startup prune called `subagentStore.remove` while holding `SubagentManager.mu`, and `remove` waits for the store lock, which a concurrent save holds across its fsync. Every other caller of the manager (`start`, `list`, `status`, `Cancel*`) then waited for that fsync. The manifests are now removed after `mu` is released, with the transcript folders, the way the folders already were. The removal goes through `SubagentManager.removeManifest`, which a memory-only manager binds to a nil store (a no-op). No model-visible text changes; the only observable change is that a finish no longer delays other callers of the manager.
- **A sub-agent run id is drawn until no held run has it (P6CM-09).** The run id is 32 random bits and `start` installed it in the run map without a check, so a repeat overwrote a live run: the older run's finish and prune then acted on the newer record. `start` now draws the id under the manager lock and redraws (up to 8 attempts) while a run of the manager holds it; the attempt bound is a safety net, not a setting. An id that cannot be drawn fails the start with an error. No model-visible text changes. The remote (`rs_`) and profile-message (`pm_`) handle ids are not changed: `asyncmgr.Register` already refuses a duplicate handle id.
- **A user stop of a sub-agent run that is not a handle keeps its reason (P6CM-16).** `CancelUnmanagedOwner` and `CancelUnmanagedOwnersWithPrefix` cancelled the run without a reason, so a stopped run that blocked its call said "was stopped" with no `: user_stop`. The resolver now passes the reason the async manager gets: `user_stop` for a stop, `session_reset` for a reset and `session_delete` for a delete. The reason is kept the way a handle's stop keeps it: the first stop wins, and a run already being stopped by the model keeps no reason of ours. **Model-visible:** the stopped result of such a run gains `: <reason>` after "was stopped"; no other text changes. Test: `TestSubagentCancelUnmanagedOwnerKeepsTheStopReason`. The three unmanaged cancels now share one helper, `cancelActive`, so the reason cannot be dropped from one of them again. `CancelOwner`, the handle path, already passed its reason and is unchanged.
- **A sub-agent cancel counts every run it stops, including one that ends during the call (flake).** The unmanaged and handle cancels checked each run's state inside the loop that cancelled the runs before it. A cancelled parent ends its nested child through its context, so a child that ended during the call was no longer active when the loop reached it and was not counted. `TestNestedRunBelongsToTheSessionThatStartedTheTree` failed on that count under load (1 of 4 parallel `-race` runs). The runs are now chosen before any is cancelled, in `cancelActive`. **Visible:** `CancelOwner`'s return value is the number of runs it asked to stop, which now includes a nested run that ended during the call; no model-visible text changes. Test: `TestSubagentCancelOwnerCountsARunThatEndsDuringTheCall`.
- **A failed sub-agent run stores a bounded reason (P6CM-11).** `execute` stored the launcher's error text whole in the run's reason, which the manifest keeps and a remote peer receives through `ExternalRun.Outcome`: a provider's error body could go to a peer unbounded, while the model's text was already cut to 400 bytes. The reason is now bounded at the write with the same `boundedReason` helper the model's text uses, so the stored value, the manifest and the remote answer carry the same bound. **Stored data:** a manifest written before this change keeps its unbounded reason; a restored run reads it as it was saved, and the remote API still cuts it to 1024 bytes on the way out. **Model-visible:** none; the model text was already bounded to the same 400 bytes. Test: `TestSubagentFailedRunStoresABoundedReason`.
- **Session allow is a glob and profile deny is exact: kept (P6CM-25, decision).** `deny_profiles` matches a profile id exactly, and the session allow (`sessions`) matches globs. A deny of every profile of a session has no spelling, and that is kept: the deny list is a list of profile ids, as the schema's `deny_profiles` description says ("Profile ids (category/name)"). No glob was added to `deny_profiles` and no code changed. A glob deny would be a new grammar for the setting, which is a user-facing configuration change and not hygiene.

## Phase 7a: fetch storage and per-agent paths (Go only, 2026-11-03)

- **The fetch store has no automatic reclaim (P7A-02, decision: keep the cap).** `tools.native.fetch.max_storage_bytes` (default 64 MiB) is a hard cap on the stored documents of one `storage_dir`. The store does not evict: an eviction would delete documents that a model may still page, and the store is shared by every session with the same `storage_dir`, so one session could remove another's documents. When the cap is reached, `Create` refuses the new document with `fetch_storage_error` and a message that names `storage_dir` and `max_storage_bytes`. The recovery is manual and documented: deleting a document's `.md` and `.json` files in `storage_dir` frees its space and ends its cursors, and raising `max_storage_bytes` also works. A document larger than the whole cap is refused with its own message. Test: `TestFetchToolFullStorageErrorNamesTheLimitAndTheRecovery`. **Model-visible:** the message text changed; the code did not.
- **Per-agent `paths` narrows write and edit only (P7A-16, decision: keep).** An agent's `paths` limits where its `write` and `edit` may change files. It does not limit reads: `read`, `grep`, `glob` and `fetch` ignore it, so a child can read whatever the session can read. This is the recorded design (the Per-agent `paths` entry in the write-capable children chapter above). The schema description of `paths` in `sample/schemes/agent.schema.json` says so. A read restriction would change behaviour for existing agents and is a separate decision. Not built: a read `paths` key.
- **The public-address check refuses five more IANA special-purpose ranges (P7A-03, decision: block the listed five).** `isPublicFetchAddress`, the check that `fetch` applies to every resolved address, now refuses 198.51.100.0/24 (TEST-NET-2), 203.0.113.0/24 (TEST-NET-3), 192.88.99.0/24 (deprecated 6to4 relay anycast), 2001:2::/48 (benchmarking) and 2001:10::/28 (deprecated ORCHID). It keeps 2001:20::/28 (ORCHIDv2) and 2001::/32 (Teredo) open: the registry marks both globally reachable, and blocking them is a separate decision. The rules are added to the existing IPv4 switch and IPv6 byte checks, with no second list. Source: IANA IPv4 Special-Purpose Address Registry and IPv6 Special-Purpose Address Registry, both last updated 2025-10-09. The rows: `198.51.100.0/24, Documentation (TEST-NET-2), RFC 5737, Destination False, Globally Reachable False`; `203.0.113.0/24, Documentation (TEST-NET-3), RFC 5737, Destination False, Globally Reachable False`; `192.88.99.0/24, Deprecated (6to4 Relay Anycast), RFC 7526, terminated 2015-03, Source/Destination/Forwardable/Globally Reachable empty`; `2001:2::/48, Benchmarking, RFC 5180, Globally Reachable False`; `2001:10::/28, Deprecated (previously ORCHID), RFC 4843, terminated 2014-03, flags empty`; kept: `2001:20::/28, ORCHIDv2, RFC 7343, Globally Reachable True` and `2001::/32, TEREDO, RFC 4380, Globally Reachable True`. **Trade-off:** a lab or private network that numbers hosts in TEST-NET space, or in these prefixes, can no longer fetch them, and a public host that resolves into one of these ranges is refused where it was accepted before. No config key was added; the check has no setting. Tests: `TestFetchURLPolicyAndPublicAddressRanges` (one blocked and one open address per range, and the two kept ranges).

## Transcript tools are not hook-wrapped (Go only, 2026-11-04)

Audit: `audit_findings/p7b_merged.md` (MRG-30, from R1-12).

- **`transcript` and `transcripts` are not wrapped by `tool_event` hooks (decision, not a defect).** `buildNativeTools` wraps every native tool in the `tool_event` hooks (`WithToolEventHooksAll`), but the two transcript tools are appended after that wrapping, so a `pre_tool` hook does not see a transcript read while it sees every other native tool. `request_draft` is appended after it for the same reason (it is a question, not an action). The two transcript tools are read-only and only read a transcript the caller names or its own, so a hook would add an event for a read the user has no reason to gate. A hook that must see them is not served by this, and wrapping them is a separate decision.

## Another session's reasoning is hidden by default (Go only, 2026-11-04)

Audit: `audit_findings/p7b_merged.md` (MRG-07, from R3-07). Decided by the user.

- **`tools.native.transcript.allow_other_reasoning`, default `false` (new key).** Reasoning is private planning. Until now a session that could read another session's transcript (`allow_other_sessions` true, the default) could also read its reasoning steps with `step` and `part: reasoning`. The new key sits in the family of `allow_other_sessions`, `allow_other_profiles` and `allow_children`, and gates one thing: `part: reasoning` on a transcript that belongs to another session. Other profiles of the caller's own session, the caller's own transcript and sub-agent runs of the caller's session keep their reasoning, unchanged. The key is not in `transcripts`, which lists and has no reasoning part.
- **Denial.** A refused read returns status `forbidden` with the code `reasoning_denied` and the text "Another session's reasoning is not shown: tools.native.transcript.allow_other_reasoning is false". The check runs before the file is opened. The code is new and visible to the model; no existing code list or error table needed it, because the tool's error codes are not enumerated outside the code that returns them.
- **Model-visible change.** With the default, a cross-session `part: reasoning` read returns the `reasoning_denied` result instead of the reasoning text. Every other read of another session is unchanged: the list, `part: both`, `part: call` and `part: result`.
- **Enabling.** Set `allow_other_reasoning` to `true` in the `transcript` block of `tools.native`.

## Low batches: constructor ladder, glob copies and hash error text kept (Go only, 2026-11-05)

Audit: `audit_findings/p6c_merged.md` (P6CM-19), `audit_findings/p1_merged.md` (N20), `audit_findings/p8_merged.md` (P8-M-10). Decided by the user as "do recommended".

- **The sub-agent constructor ladder stays (P6CM-19, style, no change).** `subagent.go:189-313` has seven layers (the six wrappers at `:189-249`, and `newSubagentToolRemotes`, the body, at `:254-313`): `NewSubagentTool`, `NewSubagentToolWithMCP`, `NewSubagentToolWithManager`, `NewSubagentToolWithProcesses`, `newSubagentTool`, `newSubagentToolAsync` and `newSubagentToolRemotes`. Each layer adds one parameter and has a doc comment saying why. Only `newSubagentToolRemotes` has a production caller (`native/catalog.go:209`); the public wrappers are used by 32 test call sites in nine `native` test files. Collapsing the ladder would change every test constructor for no behaviour change, so the layers stay as a deliberate pattern. A new parameter should still go on the bottom layer only.
- **The three glob copies stay apart (N20, no change).** `remote/glob.go:6-16` (`matchGlob`, `MatchGlobs`), `remote/entry.go:291-300` (`Entry.Allows`) and `remoteapi/tokens.go:150-162` (`matchAny`) differ on purpose. `remoteapi` refuses a target with an empty, `.` or `..` segment before it matches (`tokens.go:151-155`); the other two do not, and `remote` has no such check. Merging them would change which targets a token or a client allow-list reaches. `remote.MatchGlobs` (`glob.go:9`) has no caller, so it is dead code and may be removed in a cleanup commit of its own. Each copy keeps its rule.
- **The hash error text reaches the model, so `appendFileIfAbsent` keeps its own hash (P8-M-10 follow-up, no change).

## JAD_SESSIONS_DIR is refused (Go only, 2026-11-06)

Audit: `audit_findings/p2b1_merged.md` (P2B1-10). Decided by the user: refuse outright.

- **Deliberate tightening.** `JAD_SESSIONS_DIR` used to name a single session root with no catalog layout, so such a root could not serve a session that needed one. It now fails the load with "JAD_SESSIONS_DIR is no longer read; name the session folder in session_roots of the root setting". The `LoadOptions.SessionsDir` option, which the embedders and tests use, is unchanged.
- **Test.** `TestLoadRootSettingAliasesDeduplicatesPathsAndHonorsSessionsDir` fails on the previous commit (`Load(JAD_SESSIONS_DIR) error = <nil>`) and passes after. `TestCheckCommandFailsWithoutTheCatalogLayout` no longer uses the variable; it runs with no root setting, which is the no-layout case.

## Sessionfs review decisions (Go only, 2026-11-06)

Audit: `audit_findings/p2b1_merged.md`, `audit_findings/p2b2_merged.md`. Each row below was re-read at HEAD before the decision.

- **Symlinked config files are supported (P2B1-07, closed, no code change).** `readRegularFile` (`native_config_file.go:31`) opens with `os.OpenFile`, so a final symlink is followed, and the type is checked on the opened handle. That is the intended behaviour: a config kept outside the folder still loads. `TestReadConfigTextFileReadsASymlinkedConfigFile` documents it. `O_NOFOLLOW` is not added.
- **A future mtime is treated as racy (P2B1-05, closed, no code change).** `fileStamp.racy` (`settings_stamp.go:51`) has no lower bound on `taken - modTime`, so a file whose mtime is in the future is never current and the settings cache stays off until the clock passes it. This is the conservative choice: a stamp cannot show a further change to a file it cannot date, so the cache is not used.
- **The `persistence.durability` default is `turn` (P2B1-06, closed, no code change).** `normalizePersistence` (`session.go:727`) defaults to `turn`. The only fsync is for `strict` (`layout_settings.go:120`, `writeFileIfUnchanged`). The policy: the default trades a sync per write for speed, and a process or power loss may lose the last turn of a model-default write. An operator who needs the write on disk sets `strict`.
- **Closures are not a defect (P2B2-24, closed, no code change).** The four bounded closures (`checkpoint_settings.go:85`, `hooks_settings.go:165`, `image_resize_settings.go:88`, `provider_retry_settings.go:48`) each read the same way and name their own key in the error. The merge did not name a concrete wrong value.
- **Model reads per load are deferred to the performance list (P2B1-04, P2B1-12, deferred, no code change).** `checkModelEffort` and `layout_load.go:102-107` read the model file again per load (up to three reads). The cost is measured in the performance list (`audit_findings/final_perf.md`), not fixed here. P2B1-04 also records a narrow staleness: a `model_default` that is not the selected model is not stamped, so a change to it is seen on the next load.
- **`max_entries: 0` is kept (P2B2-17, closed by decision).** `sessionFileInteger` (`file_settings.go:264`) accepts 0. A zero limit lists nothing and reports the listing as truncated when a folder has entries. Nothing is persisted, so no state is corrupted. Refusing 0 would refuse a config that loads today, which is not wanted.
- **The delete check is a documented race (P2B2-13, closed by decision).** `CheckSessionDelete` (`session_files.go:263`) runs the If-Match check without the session-file lock, then `DeleteSessionFile` removes the file. A re-check under that lock was rejected: `DeleteSessionConfig` (`session_catalog_files.go:74`) already holds `settingsMu`, and `acquireSettingLock` (`resolver.go:378`) takes the same file lock, so a lock inside the check would nest under the process mutex and make the check endpoint wait behind a running session. The window (another writer changes the session between the check and the remove) is recorded here, not fixed.
- **Symlinks in the native read path (P2B2-06, closed for the file side).** `CollectSessionFiles` skips every symlink entry (`files.go:84-86`), and a directory is entered only when it resolves inside the workspace (`resolveContainedDirectory`, `files.go:167`). So a symlink never reaches the deny check in the listing. The read and write tools check the resolved target: `inspectMutateTarget` (`tools/native/mutate_path.go`) calls `resolvedPathAllowed` for a symlink target, and `pathPolicyError` (`tools/native/path.go:77-80`) matches deny rules against the target it is given. **Open:** the rule-side resolution (a deny rule path is compared as written, not resolved through symlinks) is a separate decision and is not changed here.
- **Open item: the shared `configSafeInteger` (P2B2-19).** Only the three tool-limit readers (`max_tool_rounds`, `max_run_tokens`, `max_parallel_tool_calls`) refuse exponent notation. The other callers of `configSafeInteger` (`session.go` cursor and checkpoint keys, `roots.go`, `remote_api_settings.go`, `checkpoint_settings.go`, `hooks_settings.go`, `image_resize_settings.go`, `provider_retry_settings.go`, `openai_oauth_settings.go`, `provider_stream_settings.go`, `native_memory_settings.go`) still accept `1e3`. Narrowing them is a separate decision, since each one is a config change.
- **Deferred to the performance list (P2B1-04, P2B1-12).** See the model-read item above.** `jsonl.go:474` (`hash rendered transcript line: %w`) is a whole-file loop that returns a `[32]byte` digest used only as the duplicate key (`jsonl.go:476-496`). Its error is wrapped as `append response line: %w` (`turn.go:398`), then `commit completed Responses event: %w` (`session/runner_response.go:128`), returned from `runResponse` (`:142`) and `Run` (`runner.go:502`). A sub-agent run that fails this way reaches the model as `subagent_failed`, `Agent <name> failed: <reason>` (`tools/native/subagent_call.go:370-371`), with the reason bounded to 400 bytes by `boundedReason` (`subagent.go:797`, `subagent.go:795`). Routing the copy through `hashReader` would change that text and the digest shape, so the copy stays. `hashReader` (`recovery.go:283`) is shared by `hashFile` (`recovery.go:124`, startup recovery) and `hashTranscriptFile` (`maintenance.go:140`, compaction). Their error text was not traced to model output in this pass; that is open, and it does not change this entry, which concerns only the copy in `jsonl.go`.

## Sub-agent batch: stop policy, remote 503, failure text cap (Go only, 2026-11-07)

Audit: `audit_findings/p2b2_merged.md` (P2B2-25, P2B2-18), `audit_findings/p4_merged.md` (M24, M28), `audit_findings/p2b2_merged.md` (repair order).

- **The tool batch now honours its stop policy (P2B2-25, part c, behaviour-neutral).** `executeToolRound` passes `shouldStop` to `runToolBatch` as `ctx.Err() != nil` (`runner_tools.go:252-274`). It was `nil`, so the batch never checked the documented contract. The change has no observable effect: `runner_tools.go:254` already returns `ctx.Err()` before `ExecuteFunctionCall`, so a cancelled round runs no tool with or without it, and the calls the batch no longer claims get the `tool_execution_cancelled` placeholder (`runner.go:616-617`). The batch-level stop test covers the claim count. No new test: none can fail at HEAD.
- **Test-only exports, kept (P2B2-25).** `BuildRequest`, `BuildRequestWithTools` and `PersistInput` have no reference outside the `session` package. `BuildRequest` and `BuildRequestWithTools` are used by `runner.go` and by in-package tests; `PersistInput` is used by `runner_tools_test.go` and `input_accepted_test.go`. None is dead code, so none is deleted.
- **Open refactor, not done: split `Runner.Run` (P2B2-25).** `runner.go` `Run` is about 247 lines (`:392-639`) and mixes input, checkpoint, request, stream, compaction, token cap, tools and persistence. Splitting it is a structural change, so it is a separate item.
- **Open refactor, not done: replace the sleeps in the batch tests (P2B2-25).** `tool_batch_test.go` orders four tests with `time.Sleep`. Replacing them with synchronisation is a test-quality change, so it is a separate item.
- **The process abort reason is passed through the process summary limit (P2B2-18, explicit cap, no current output change).** `processAsyncHandle.Abort` (`process_async.go:50`) now cuts the cause to `AsyncHost.Config().SummaryMaxBytes` (`asyncmgr/config.go:69`, default 512) before it is stored. Correction to the first draft of this entry: the stored reason was already capped by `asyncmgr` at `reasonMaxBytes = 128` (`manager.go:401`, `types.go:52`), so the path was not uncapped and no default changed. The explicit cap is a guard that keeps the process path independent of the manager constant. The only cause on this path is the fixed `errProcessStorage` (`process_manager.go:248`), so no current output changes. Test: `TestProcessAbortCapsTheStoredReasonAtTheSummaryLimit` passes before and after the change; it guards the bound and does not fail on the previous commit. The sub-agent failure text keeps its own key (`tools.native.subagent.failure_text_max_bytes`); the two caps are separate settings.
- **A broken token table answers 503, not 401 (M24, status change).** When the table file cannot be read, `TokenTable.Authenticate` returns `errTableBroken` (`tokens.go:283`), and the server answers `503` with `code: "token_table_unusable"` and no `WWW-Authenticate` (`server.go:222-226`). Before, it answered `401 unauthorized` with the challenge, which is wrong: the caller was not refused for its token. The client treats a status under 500 as permanent (`remote/run.go:152-154`), so a followed run ended on the old 401; a 503 is retried with backoff (`:161-164`). Unknown, revoked and expired tokens keep `401` and the challenge. Test: `TestBrokenTokenTableAnswers503AndAnUnknownTokenStays401` (fails on the previous commit with `status 401`).
- **The sub-agent failure text was already bounded on both paths (P2B2-18, closed by reading, no config key).** Sync path: `boundedReason` caps the provider-error reason at `subagentReasonBytes = 400` (`subagent.go:797`, `subagent.go:795`). Async abort path: `subagentAsyncHandle.abort` stores `cause.Error()` through `asyncmgr.Manager.Complete` (`subagent_async.go:38`), which caps every stored reason at `reasonMaxBytes = 128` (`manager.go:401`, `types.go:52`). So the finding's sub-agent half is not a defect. A `tools.native.subagent.failure_text_max_bytes` key was considered and rejected: it would add config surface without fixing anything. The constants stay. The process half is fixed in `6e794cc` (see above).
- **The panic text is generic and model-visible (P2B2-18).** A recovered tool-batch panic now reads `tool call panicked`. The panic value and stack are logged server-side only, through `toolBatchPanic.LogValue` (`session/tool_batch.go:18-22`). The previous text, `tool batch call panicked: <value>`, reached the model through a sub-agent's failure reason. Test: `TestRunToolBatchTurnsAPanicIntoTheCallsError` (fails on the previous text, passes now).
- **M28 closed by decision (design note, no code change).** `checkpoint.CallChangesWorkspace` (`checkpoint/run.go:12-39`) and `catalog.CallMayChangeFiles` (`tools/catalog.go:256-286`) are two mechanisms for "this call can change files", ORed at `session/runner_tools.go:176`. Every currently registered write-capable native tool is covered by one of them, so the split is latent. Unifying them (a flag on every tool) would touch every tool registration and is a design change, so it is recorded here, not fixed. The guard against drift is the current coverage: if a new write tool is added, it must set its hook or be added to `CallChangesWorkspace`.
- **Repair-order change accepted (P2B2-11, commit `07678f5`).** The session path validates input items (`transcript.ValidateInputItem`) before it externalizes their images, so a rejected batch writes no image. Before, the images were written first and the batch was refused afterwards, which spent the per-session image quota (`input_accepted_test.go`, `TestPersistInputWithImagesWritesNoImageForARejectedBatch`, fails on `be71753` with 1 file). Refuse before writing is the correct order: a rejected batch leaves nothing to clean up.

## Phase 3 batch: owner entry and profile listing (Go only, 2026-11-08)

Audit: `audit_findings/p4_merged.md` (M21, M27), `audit_findings/low_unverified_verdicts.md`.

- **An owner entry goes once its last dropped count is read (M21).** `Wait` reports `dropped` once and clears it (`takeDroppedLocked`, `wait.go`). If the owner has no rows left, its entry is removed in the same step, so an owner whose rows were deleted no longer keeps a map entry. Before, `forgetLocked` kept the entry while `dropped > 0`, and nothing removed it after the count was read. Test: `TestOwnerEntryGoesWhenItsLastDroppedCountIsRead` (fails with the removal disabled).
- **Status change: a session the token cannot see answers 404 `no_session` (M27).** `GET /profiles?session=` returns `404 no_session` when the token sees none of the session's profiles, the same answer as a missing session. Before, that case answered `200` with an empty list, so a valid token could tell which sessions exist. A token that sees at least one profile still gets `200` with its filtered list. The 403 for a token without the listing actions is unchanged. This also covers a session that has no profiles at all, which now answers 404 for every token; no in-repo test or client relied on the empty 200 (the `/profiles` callers in `app/remote_api_test.go` use a token that sees profiles). Test: `TestProfilesListIsFilteredByTheToken` (fails with the new branch disabled).

## Maintenance gate order (P3-12)

Audit: `audit_findings/p3_merged.md` (P3-12).

- **Fix: reset, front compact and state context clear their mutation gate before they give the process lock back (P3-12).** Each handler now releases the gate in the same deferred closure as the locks, first, as compaction does, so a start queued behind the action does not find the gate still set. Before, the gate was deferred first and so ran last. The early refusals still clear the gate. Test: `TestMaintenanceClearsItsMutationGateBeforeReleasingTheProcessLock` (fails on all three at HEAD; a swapped order is CAUGHT). Checkpoint restore had the same order and is fixed in its own change (see the checkpoint note below).

## Content cache fallback reads only the cap (P3-16)

- **Fix: the memory fallback reads at most `fallbackMax` bytes of the partial file (P3-16).** `switchToFallbackLocked` used to read the whole partial file, then cap it. It now reads only the prefix the cap keeps (`readStablePrefixLocked`). The kept bytes are the same. Test: `TestFallbackSwitchReadsNoMoreThanTheCap` (measured allocation: 8.4 MB at HEAD for a 100-byte cap; CAUGHT when the bounded read is reverted).

## Checkpoint restore clears its gate first (P3-12, restore)

- **Fix: checkpoint restore clears its mutation gate and restoring mark before the process lock (P3-12).** The restoring mark (`markRestoring`) still covers the whole restore: it is cleared in the same deferred closure, after `RestoreCheckpoint` returns, first among the releases. A dry run does not set it. Test: `TestCheckpointRestoreClearsItsMutationGateBeforeReleasingTheProcessLock` (fails at HEAD, passes now; a swapped release order is CAUGHT).

## Phase 3 batch: library error text and stale or open rows (P3-09, P3-15, P3-07, P3-08)

- **Status text change: `/api/library` no longer names the absolute directory (P3-09).** A prompt, template or skill directory that is not a directory is refused with `a library directory for <kind> is not a directory` (`contentlibrary/library.go`) instead of the resolved path. The status (422) is unchanged. The text is client-visible only, not model-visible. Test: `TestLibraryErrorDoesNotNameTheAbsoluteDirectory` (fails at HEAD, which named the path).

- **P3-15 stale.** A run retired by the failed-start guard is finished with `failureEvents(...)` (`handler.go`), not `nil`, so a late attacher replays `done`. No change.
- **P3-07 accepted limitation.** Finished runs are kept by time only. Accepted by the decider: no count or byte cap and no config key. Finished runs are kept by time only (`maxRuns` counts running runs). Adding a count or byte cap changes retention and needs a configured limit; the audit accepted it at low.
- **P3-08 accepted limitation.** A finished run keeps its content cache and `liveImages` until retirement. Accepted by the decider: no config key and no release change, since the live image route needs `liveImages` for a finished run. `finish` does not release the content cache or `liveImages`. The live image route (`handleLiveRunImage`) serves a finished run's images from `liveImages` until retirement, so releasing them at finish would 404 images that are not yet in the transcript. Releasing only the content cache at finish needs a check of the late-snapshot path first.

## Session listing: an unreadable file has no etag (P3-11)

Audit: `audit_findings/p3_merged.md` (P3-11).

- **Status: a session file the listing could not read is listed without an `etag` (P3-11, option a).** The `etag` field on a catalog listing row is omitted when empty (`CatalogFileInfo.ETag`, `catalog_files_handlers.go`). Before, the unreadable row carried `"etag": ""`. The frontend accepts a row with no `etag` (`fetchCatalogFiles`, `api.ts`), keeps it, and disables its delete, since a delete needs a version. Opening a file still needs an etag, and a row with none cannot be opened. Tests: `TestSessionListLeavesOutTheEtagOfAnUnreadableFile` (backend, fails with `omitempty` removed) and `fetchCatalogFiles accepts a session row that carries an error and no etag` (frontend, fails before the validator change).

## Phase 3 batch: remaining rows classified (P3-06, P3-14, P3-17, M16, M17, M20, M30)

- **P3-06 open, performance only.** `beginSubscribe` builds the async_state and provider_retry frames under `r.mu`. This delays other `r.mu` users but is not a correctness defect; moving the encoding out of the lock needs a snapshot-consistency argument, not a one-line change.
- **P3-14 refuted as a defect.** `pushLocked` deliberately does not charge an oversized snapshot to the budget; the code comment says the frames after it are bounded on their own. Retention is one frame per subscriber, and a stalled subscriber is still removed by the ordinary path.
- **P3-17 accepted, unreachable in production (decider option c).** `acquireRun` spins only for a resolver with the try form of the process lock and not the blocking form; `app.Resolver` has both. No code change and no test. `acquireRun` spins only when the resolver has the try form of the process lock and not the blocking form. `app.Resolver` implements both (`resolver.go`). A fix would either refuse a queued start with a new status or add a backoff constant; both need a decision. The fail-first test (551,849 attempts in 100 ms) was not committed.
- **M16 fixed.** Checkpoint `begin`, `Finish`, `Restore` and the gc used to hold the staging mutex across git subprocesses. Each staging directory now has a gate (`Service.gate`) that serializes all git work and `checkpoints.json` of its services, so no two operations on one directory interleave their git commands. `Service.mu` guards only `gcPending` and is never held across git. The on-disk format is unchanged. Tests: `lock_scope_unix_test.go` (serialization, no interleaving, state lock free and `Restore` gated during a blocked `Finish`).
- **M17 refuted as a defect (bounded).** The `locks` and `repoSizes` maps have one entry per staging directory. The key is `config.StagingDir` (server configuration), never a session or request value, so the set is fixed for a process.
- **M20 refuted.** The `evictFinishedLocked` precedence picks the token's own oldest finished run first, then the oldest overall; the case analysis gives the intended order. Readability only.
- **M30 fixed (decider option a).** `begin` ignores the `rand.Read` error, so a failed read mints `rr_0000000000000000`. The fix refuses the start with the existing `unavailable` refusal (503), through a new test-only seam `Options.Random` (default `crypto/rand.Read`). Test: `TestRunIDIsRefusedWhenTheRandomSourceFails` (at HEAD the run started with `rr_0000000000000000` and answered 202; the reverted line is CAUGHT).

## Wait timeout checks the context before it takes signals (M26)

- **Fix: `Wait` checks `ctx` before it takes the ready signals on timeout (M26).** Before, a context cancelled in the window after the timeout fired still took the ready signals as read and then returned the error, so those signals were lost to the caller. The check now comes first, so a cancelled wait consumes nothing, as its doc comment states. **No deterministic fail-first test:** the window is a race between the timer and the cancel, and there is no hook to force it. The fix is by reading of `wait.go`; the existing `TestWaitCancelledConsumesNothing` and the asyncmgr suite pass.

## Debug log: token and secret headers, and transport errors (P3-10)

Audit: `audit_findings/p3_merged.md` (P3-10).

- **Status: the debug log redacts headers named `token` or `secret`, and the URL inside a transport error (P3-10, option a).** `sensitiveHeaderPattern` (`httpdebug/transport.go`) gains `token` and `secret`, so `X-Session-Token` and `X-Client-Secret` are logged as `[REDACTED]`. A transport error is logged with its request URL replaced by the redacted URL (`redactErrorURL`), so a `key=` query value in the `*url.Error` text does not reach the file. Before, both were written in full. This changes what the debug log records; it does not change what the provider receives. Test: `TestTransportRedactsTokenHeadersAndTheURLInATransportError` (fails at HEAD on both assertions, and with the pattern narrowed back, CAUGHT).

## listen_unix_mode description names the write-bit refusal (Go only, 2026-11-09)

Audit: step-2 impact pass on `a7ad56b`. Range 384-438 is unchanged; `remote_api_settings.go` refuses a mode with a group or other write bit (`mode & 0o022`). Modes 0644 and 0600 are accepted; 0664 and 0666 are refused. `sample/schemes/root-setting.schema.json` now says so in the description. Test: `TestRemoteAPIListenUnixModeSchemaMatchesTheLoader` (fails on the previous description, and with the write-bit refusal disabled, CAUGHT).

## Profile tool and transcripts redirect (Go only)

- **Profile listing moved (redirect ships now).** `native.profile` (action `list`, path `session:<id>`) lists the profiles of a session, and `native.transcripts` answers its profile-listing paths with a one-line `profiles_moved` redirect. Its run list and history reads are unchanged. The old listing code is gone from `transcripts`; the shared listing lives in `profile`. Remove the profile-listing paths of `transcripts` in the first release cut on or after 2027-03-31.
- **Profile tool.** `native.profile` lists, shows info (context tokens of the last completed response, "no usage yet" when none), and compacts or resets a profile. Settings `tools.native.profile` mirror `transcripts` for `max_entries`, `scan_max_entries`, `allow_other_sessions`, `allow_other_profiles`, `allow_children`, `allow_remote` and `remotes`; a test keeps both sections in step with `setting.schema.json`. Compact and reset are refused to a sub-agent and to the caller's own profile (from inside its run). Path `self` names the caller's own profile (the session key gives it; with no caller, or a caller with no session or no profile, it is refused as `profile_caller_unknown`) and is mapped after the list gate, so `list` refuses it as `invalid_path`; a sub-agent reads its inherited profile with `info` only when allow_children is on. Reset is refused while a run of the target is active; compact waits for a live run to end, as on the HTTP route (see `audit_findings/design_profile_tool.md`). A remote profile takes `info` only, read through the remote API; a remote compact or reset is refused before any request is made.

## Remote resume of a sub-agent run (rules)

- **Wire form.** A remote run has an id the serving host mints, `rr_` plus 16 lowercase hex digits, and the client sees it as `remote_run: <name>/rr_…` on the inline and async results (the form in `design_remote.md`, `<name>/<run id>`). A resume names the same run as `resume: <name>/rr_…`, where `name` must be the remote the call is made on. The client checks the form before any request: a wrong name, or an id without the `rr_` prefix, is refused with the expected form, and nothing is sent. The server accepts only `rr_` ids in `resume` and refuses anything else before the backend. `resume` is for subagents only; a message that names it is refused.
- **Caller from the token.** The caller is `remote:<token id>`, set by the remote API from the authenticated token, never from the body. It rides on the call's context (`WithRemoteCaller`) and only the resume branch reads it. The run records it, and the manifest keeps it across a restart. A local run has no caller, so a remote resume never reaches one, and a local resume never reaches a remote one.
- **Owner rule.** A local resume needs the run's owner to be the caller's session and the run to have no caller. A remote resume needs `run.caller == "remote:<token id>"`. Any other caller gets `unknown_run`, the same code a local other-owner run gets, so an id cannot be probed. The remote resume also checks `MayAgent` for the source's agent before the call is made, and the request fingerprint includes the resume id, so a reused `request_id` with another source is refused as `request_id_reused`.
- **Shared core, no second copy.** `checkResumeSource`, `checkResumeTranscript` and `startRunFrom` are the one path for a local and a remote resume. The caller chooses only the identity check (`resumeMayUse`). The state, transcript, unanswered-call and agent checks are the same for both.
- **Token rotation.** Runs are owned by the token id. Rotating a token (a new id) leaves its old runs unresumable through the remote API, by design: the identity check is the token id. The old runs stay on disk and locally resumable by their own session.
- **`rr_` mapping and its lifetime.** The serving host keeps a map from each `rr_` id it minted to the local run it started (`sub_…`), in memory, in the resolver's remote backend. A run is recorded only once the local run exists: a refused or failed start records nothing. The map is not exposed to the local tool. A restarted server does not know the ids of the runs before it, so a restored manifest run is not resumable through the remote API; an old `rr_` id is refused as `unknown_run`. The local run is still in the manifest and is still resumable locally by its session. The map is bounded by `keep_finished`: when the manager drops a run, its `rr_` id is forgotten too (the manager calls the index through `SetOnDropped`), so a resume of a dropped run is `unknown_run`, and the map never grows past the runs the manager keeps. **Manager close and restart.** The index is forgotten when its manager closes (`SetOnClosed`, which removes every entry of that manager and no other), and a restarted server starts with an empty index. The reason is bounded memory: a closed manager refuses every start and resume, so its runs cannot be resumed and their entries would only hold memory. A run recorded into a closed manager is refused by `Retains`, which is false once the manager is closed.
- **Caps and depth.** A remote resume is a top-level start (depth 0) and goes through `SubagentManager.start`, the same path a local start takes, so the run is registered, has its own id, and names its source (`resumed_from`). A resume from inside a child is refused as `resume_in_child` before any identity check. The P6CM-01 rule holds: a nested remote start takes its depth and run-cap place through `reserveRemote`.
- **Leak.** A child of a remote-resumed run carries no remote caller on its own calls: the caller lives on the call's context, and a child runs on the manager's context. Test: `TestRemoteResumedRunsChildHasNoRemoteCaller`.
- **View fields.** `resumed_from` and `config_note` are additive and omitted when empty, on both the server's run JSON and the client's view. The remote `resumed_from` names the remote id of the source when this host minted one, so a client sees the id it knows.
- **Tests.** App: `TestRemoteStartedRunResumesByItsRemoteID`, `TestRemoteResumeOfAnotherTokensRunIsUnknownLikeALocalOtherOwner`, `TestRemoteResumeOfAnOldRemoteIDIsUnknownAfterARestart`, `TestRemoteRunIndexFindsARunOnlyInItsSession`. Native: `TestRemoteResumeRefusalsMatchTheLocalOnes`, `TestRemoteCallerChildCannotResume`, `TestRemoteResumeGoesThroughTheManagersStart`, `TestQueuedRunIsRefusedAsActiveWhenResumed`. Server: `TestResumeIsPartOfTheRequestFingerprint`, `TestResumeUnknownRunIsMappedToItsCode`, `TestRunViewWritesTheResumeFieldsUnderTheClientsNames`.
- **Remote caps.** A top-level remote start or resume (depth 0) is never refused by the run caps, the same as a top-level local start: the caps apply to nested starts (see P6CM-01 above). A nested remote start or resume takes its place through `reserveRemote`, as a nested local start does.

## Leaf tool budget: process-wide concurrency bound (B-01, Go only)

- **The goal measured is a concurrency bound, not a goroutine bound.** `tools.LeafBudget` is one counting semaphore per process, built by `NewService` (`app/service.go`) and handed to every run through `Resolver.leafBudget` and `session.RunOptions.LeafBudget`, so every session and every sub-agent shares it. At most `server.max_leaf_calls` (default 256) leaf tool calls run at once across all runs. Measured at N=1000 runs of 64 calls each: peak concurrent calls fell from 64,000 to 256.
- **Goroutines are not bounded; the goroutine goal is NOT met this round.** Leaf goroutines are bounded at 256 process-wide, because the budget token is taken on the dispatching goroutine before `go func` (`session/tool_batch.go`), so a leaf goroutine exists only while it holds a token. Non-leaf and wrapper goroutines (subagent, profile_message, remote, tool_smith, async, exec) and tools that did not opt in (for example MCP) are NOT bounded by the budget; they are bounded only by the per-batch slots limit. The measured peak of about 1,259 goroutines at N=1000 (from 65,003 at HEAD) is driven by per-run driver goroutines above the tool layer, not by the tool pool. The goroutine goal (bounded by worker count, not call count) is therefore not met. The worker-set redesign was considered and not built, because it does not lower the measured peak; the next target is a run-layer design for the per-run driver and wrapper goroutines.
- **Concurrency goal met: 256 concurrent leaf calls, down from 64,000 at N=1000.** This is the measured goal of this round.
- **Regression: fan-out latency at N=1000 (measured).** Fanout p50 rose from about 16 ms at HEAD (14.7 ms in a later run) to about 34–59 ms across three runs at N=1000 with the budget (the run at 59 ms is an outlier on a noisy machine); p99 stayed about 45–67 ms. Cause: a run's calls queue on the shared budget, so its 64th call starts only after other runs release tokens. This is a regression of the bound, not neutral. Round path (`executeToolRound`, the production path): saturation at N=1000 went from 526 ms at HEAD to 62–69 ms with the budget.
- **The budget is a config key, `server.max_leaf_calls` (default 256, 1 to 1024).** It was a package constant (`LeafBudgetSize`), an accepted deviation from the config rule in the first round; the follow-up moved it to a root `server` key. It is read and bounded in `sessionfs/roots.go` next to `server.max_concurrent_runs`, carried as `app.Config.MaxLeafCalls`, and `NewService` builds the budget from it. The default and bounds are `serverconfig.DefaultMaxLeafCalls` and `MaxMaxLeafCalls`. 256 is still four full batches of the largest per-response limit (64). The schema entry is in `sample/schemes/root-setting.schema.json`.
- **Only opted-in tools take a token.** `tools.Tool.LeafBudget` marks a leaf: bash, edit, write, read, grep, glob, fetch and read_image. Everything else runs without a token: subagent, profile_message, the remote calls, tool_smith, exec, async, process and every MCP tool.
- **The bypass: nested wrapper work is inline and unbounded.** A wrapper's nested work (a sub-agent's own tool calls, a profile_message target run, a tool_smith bridge call, a remote run) runs without a token and is not bounded by this budget. This is deliberate, so a wrapper never waits on a token that a leaf inside it holds. Known gap: a synchronous tool_smith script can fan out nested leaf calls with no bound. That unbounded nested fan-out is accepted for this round; bounding it needs its own design round.
- **Deadlock argument.** A token holder is a leaf, and a leaf never calls `runToolBatch`, so a token is always released when its call settles. A wrapper holds no token while it waits, so the leaves it starts can always get one.
- **Wrapper guard (structural).** `TestWrappersDoNotOptIntoTheLeafBudget` (`internal/tools/native/leaf_budget_wrappers_test.go`) builds subagent, profile_message, tool_smith, async and exec with their real constructors and fails if any sets `LeafBudget`. Each flip was caught by `mutate-check.sh` with the file restored.
- **Deadlock tests, budget of one, real batch.** Each drives the parent's batch through an HTTP run and must finish within 20 s:
  - `TestDeadlockSubagentWithHandleFinishesWithBudgetOne`, `TestDeadlockSubagentWithoutHandleFinishesWithBudgetOne` (app): the child's read is a leaf that needs the token. Opt-in on subagent is caught by mutation (timeout).
  - `TestDeadlockProfileMessageNotHandledFinishesWithBudgetOne` (app): the target profile's read is a leaf that needs the token while the parent's call waits. Opt-in on profile_message is caught by mutation (timeout).
  - `TestLazyLeafLoadedRunsThroughABatchWithBudgetOne` and the three token-lifetime tests (session).
- **Profile_message handled and remote: completion tests only; the guard covers their opt-in.** `TestDeadlockProfileMessageHandledFinishesWithBudgetOne` and `TestDeadlockRemoteProfileMessageFinishesWithBudgetOne` (app) check that the run completes at budget one. They pass whether or not the wrapper opts in, so the mutation cannot catch the opt-in for these two paths. Why: the handled call with `block_ms` 0 returns its handle at once, so the wrapper holds nothing the target needs; the remote run's leaves execute on the other server, which has its own budget. The structural guard is the check that keeps profile_message off the budget on both paths.
- **tool_smith: guard-only, with the argument recorded.** No tool_smith deadlock test exists. Building one needs a layout with tool_smith settings, the `async` tool and a bun child process, and the shared layout fixture would have to change for it. Instead the guard covers it, and the argument is structural: the bridge reaches the catalog only through `ExecuteFunctionCall` (`tool_smith_bridge.go`), which does not touch the budget (the budget is taken only in `session.runToolBatchLeaves`, via `IsLeafCall`), and no `runToolBatch` or `runToolBatchLeaves` call is reachable from the tool_smith files. The argument depends on `ExecuteFunctionCall` not touching the budget, so a change there must be caught by review.

## Goroutine spawn removals: done and deferred (B-01 follow-up, Go only)

- **Goal.** Lower goroutines per active run for the 1000-run target by removing avoidable per-run and per-call spawns. The leaf budget bounds leaf concurrency, not goroutines (see the leaf budget section above), so this round removes spawns that the budget does not touch.
- **Done (one commit each, package tests and `verify-go.sh --race` run before each commit).**
  - Site 2, `tools/native/bash_child.go`: the reader waiter goroutine is gone. The two pipe readers share an atomic counter, and the last one to exit closes `readersDone`. One goroutine less per bash call. Commit `536d113`.
  - Site 3, `app/profile_message_queue.go`: a non-queued message runs one completion goroutine, which stops the target on context end, waits for the run and then calls `finish(conclude)`. The old `watch` never called `finish`, so the order is unchanged. Queued messages keep their goroutines. Commit `83eb386`.
- **Comment fix (Go only).** `session/tool_batch.go` said non-leaf calls run on the batch goroutine. They do not: every call has its own goroutine, and only leaves hold a budget token. The comment now says so. Commit `9548409`.
- **Deferred: site 1, `httpapi/async_state.go` (`watchAsyncState`).** A time.AfterFunc throttle was designed and reviewed. It keeps first-change timing, but a busy run starts one timer goroutine per publish, so under sustained changes the goroutine count stays about the same. It is too complex for a saving that only shows on idle runs. Revisit only with a design that does not start a goroutine per publish. Precondition: prove that `stop` is never called while `asyncmgr` `m.mu` is held (`manager.go:181-183`, `:188-190`).
- **Deferred: proposal A, one async-state publisher per manager (`httpapi/async_state.go:52`, `watchAsyncState`).** Burst cost: a shared tick can align up to 1000 `read` and `setAsyncState` calls into one tick, with latency and lock contention unmeasured. Panic isolation: a panic in a shared publisher stops every run, so `recover` must be per run, which the design does not yet have. Revisit only with a load measurement at N=100/500/1000, sustained changes, publish latency and goroutine peak, showing the shared tick is no worse than the per-run loop.
- **Implemented: seam B, remote message watcher end hook (`internal/app/remote_api.go`, `httpapi/profile_run.go`, `httpapi/registry.go`).** Commit `c21d26e` adds `OnEnd func(*ProfileRun)` to `httpapi.ProfileRunRequest`. The hook runs after the transcript lock is released, once, on the completed, failed, aborted and start-failure paths. Commit `cc43f1f` replaces the remote message `watch` goroutine with a `time.AfterFunc` timeout, and computes the result in `OnEnd`. The timer is stopped on end, and a `finished` flag under `r.mu` makes a late callback a no-op. Measured at N=500 only (`TestScaleRemoteMessageHeldPeak`, `JAD_SCALE_MEASURE=1`): 6.00 goroutines per held run at c21d26e, 5.00 after (3008 to 2508 total). The c21d26e before run did not reach its assertion at N=1000, so no saving is claimed there; after, N=1000 is 3.73 per run, and the goroutines that make up that count are not known. Limits: a start failure returns `ErrBusy` synchronously, with no run and no hook (`TestRemoteMessageBusyStartLeavesNoRun`). The timer race is reasoned, not tested. The 5.5 bound is a regression bound, not a target. The scale test is gated, so there is no latency or race claim for it.
- **Deferred: site 5, `tools/native/subagent_remote.go:165`.** The release can move into `follow` after `close(r.done)`, but only if `startRemoteRun` takes an end hook. `startRemoteRun` also serves the profile_message path (`profile_message_remote.go:104`), so the change needs its own review of that path. The one goroutine saved applies only to nested remote runs that hold a cap reservation.
- **Deferred: site C, `tools/native/profile_message_async.go:190-204` (in native, not app).** The wrapper that waits on `Started()`/`Done()` for a handled message can be replaced by an end hook. A hook registered before `plan.Start` can run `drop` before `setProfileMessageRun` for a message that finishes during `Start`, which leaves a stale `profileMessages[id]` entry. Fixing that needs a post-set check, a new optional plan interface, and a split of the shared `runHandle` into local and remote paths, for a local-only saving. Revisit only with a design that removes the ordering hazard without a post-set check.
- **Blocked: site 4, `app/remote_api.go:389`.** Moving `cancel` into the goroutine at `:364` is unsafe as worded. On the handle path (`ownsRun=false`), `callSubagentTool` returns before the run ends, so `runCtx` would be cancelled while the run is still running. The cancel must stay tied to run end. Reconsider only after the return timing of `ExecuteFunctionCall` is checked for both paths.
- **Kept: site 6, `remoteapi/server.go:490`.** `Run` has no end hook. Stamping `ended` lazily would leave unobserved ended runs counted as live, so they would never be swept. Revisit only if `Run` gains an end hook.
- **Not reduced by these changes.** The per-run driver goroutine, the 256 leaf floor, nested wrapper fan-out, and net/http and provider-transport goroutines. The measured peak at N=1000 is 3 harness goroutines, plus 1,000 run goroutines, plus 256 leaves (`TestScaleToolBatchFanOut` counts only those). Wrapper goroutines are not in that number, so the test cannot show progress on the goroutine goal. A separate labelled measurement is still needed.
- **Config default unchanged.** `server.max_concurrent_runs` stays at 0 (no cap). Production peak depends on it: a non-zero default would cap the run count, but it changes behaviour for existing installs, so it is a separate decision.
- **Not measured.** None of the commits has a measured goroutine reduction. Each test shows only that the count returns to baseline. The new tests have not been mutation-checked.
- **B-01 is not met in this round.** The goal, goroutines bounded by worker count, not by call count, is not reachable with the approved changes. Measured: the a9828ca profile baseline (`TestScaleRunLayerGoroutines`), N=100/500/1000, totals 202/1002/2002, with each profile driver and async watcher equal to N. Not measured: the remote message watcher (package app), and the remote follower beyond N=32. The follower ceiling of 32 is not confirmed. The per-remote `max_in_flight` cap (default 8, max 64, `internal/remote/entry.go:27-28`) is the likely limit. Every seam change stays deferred: `OnEnd` on `ProfileRunRequest` (proposal B), a `startRemoteRun` end hook (D2, site 5, site E), the async-state publisher (proposal A, needs a load measurement first), site C, `remoteapi/server.go:490`, and the default `max_concurrent_runs`. Reopen only with a new goal that has a measured target and names the seam decisions the user accepts.
- **Deferred: seam A, one async-state publisher per manager (`httpapi/async_state.go`).** Measured with `TestScaleAsyncStateLoad` (`33caf30`) and `TestScaleAsyncPublish` (`3586902`, `JAD_SCALE_MEASURE=1`). At N=1000 the shared publisher's publish p99 is 129.6 ms, against 1.21 ms for the per-run loop. Stop condition hit, so seam A is deferred. The copied resolver read (`internal/app/resolver.go:1163-1197`) is an adapter, not the real method, and the two arms are not timed the same way (1 ms polling for per-run, publish-time stamp for shared). At N=100 the shared arm published 71 of 100 samples, so its tail is understated. The tail is not attributed to the serial drain: no drain-wait figure was recorded. No race run was done, and the load was 10 changes per session, not sustained. Revisit only with a design that publishes at most once per interval per session, a like-for-like timing method, and a publish p99 at N=1000 no worse than per-run.
- **B-01 reopened goal, not met.** Goal: at N=1000 profile runs, with no remote runs, the goroutine total measured by `TestScaleRunLayerGoroutines` is at most N+10 (1,010). Baseline: 202/1002/2002 at N=100/500/1000, with profile drivers and async watchers each equal to N, from the `a9828ca` run. No saved output for that run is kept, so the baseline is not independently re-run here. Reason: the only accepted seam that removes the per-run async watcher, seam A, is deferred on its stop condition (`3586902`). Seam B (`OnEnd` on `ProfileRunRequest`) is deferred, not accepted, pending a remote measurement that is itself blocked: the remote follower ceiling at N=32 is unconfirmed, and the remote watcher is in package `app`. Seams D2, site 5, site E, site C, `remoteapi/server.go:490`, D3 and the default `max_concurrent_runs` were not accepted for this goal. Reopen only with a new goal that names the seams accepted.
- Superseded in part: seam B is implemented in `c21d26e` and `cc43f1f`, see the seam B bullet above. The N+10 goal is still not met.

## Remote message held peak at N=1000: measured watcher saving (test-only, not a bound)

Measured with `TestScaleRemoteMessageHeldPeak/n=1000` (gated by `JAD_SCALE_MEASURE=1`), with one
`runtime.Stack(all)` dump taken at the peak. The count is the goroutine headers in that dump. Three
runs per side, all passed:

- before `c21d26e`: 4433, 4468, 4438 (4.43, 4.46, 4.43 per run).
- after `83a3581`: 3780, 3768, 3760 (3.77, 3.76, 3.75 per run).

The per-run drop is 653, 700 and 678 in total (before minus after). The named watcher closures
(`app.(*remoteMessageRun).watch`) account for 685, 692 and 686 of the before count, and go to 0 after.
The rest is small growth in net/http, poll and remoteapi.

Caveats: one test shape, a fake provider, one machine, and three runs per side. This is not a
production number. The 5.5 per-run bound is asserted at N=100, 500 and 1000, but at N=1000 both sides
pass it (4.43 before, 3.75 after), so it does not separate them there. It is a regression bound at N=500,
not a bound on the saving. The profile-only goal (`1,010` at N=1000, baseline `2,002`) is not affected
by this change.

## Run-layer baseline reproduced: 202 / 1002 / 2002 (test-only, not a reduction)

Command: `JAD_SCALE_MEASURE=1 go test -count=1 -run TestScaleRunLayerGoroutines -v ./internal/httpapi/`,
exit 0. One invocation runs N=100, 500 and 1000 in turn. Each N reports its group counts and idle=2.

- N=100: profile_driver=100, async_watcher=100, total=202.
- N=500: profile_driver=500, async_watcher=500, total=1002.
- N=1000: profile_driver=1000, async_watcher=1000, total=2002.

The six groups http_driver, remote_message_watcher, remote_subagent_start, remote_subagent_end,
attach_waiter and pump are not_measured in this test. The 1,010 goal is still open and NOT MET.
This entry records a reproduced baseline, not a reduction.

## Remote cap-slot tests: drain bound 40s (test-only, Go only)

The cleanup of the remote tests waits for the manager's remote goroutines (`remoteEnds`) through
`waitForRemoteEnds`, bounded by `remoteDrainBound` in `internal/tools/native/subagent_remote_test.go`.
On expiry the test fails with "a remote goroutine did not return within 40s; a Done is missing or a
run never ended". The bound is a test constant, not a product setting; `RemoteTimeoutMs` is unchanged.

- **Filter span:** `-run 'RemoteSubagent|RemoteRun|RemoteSync|RemoteAsync'` is an unanchored regular
  expression (`go test -run`), so the `|` separators are alternation and each part matches anywhere in a
  test name. `go test -list` selects 25 top-level tests from three files:
  `subagent_remote_test.go` (22), `profile_message_remote_test.go` (2) and `async_latest_test.go` (1).
  `RemoteRun` also matches `TestRemoteInlineAndAsyncNameTheRemoteRunTheSameWay`, because its name
  contains "RemoteRun". The resume tests in `subagent_remote_resume_test.go` are not selected.
- **Measured drain under `-race`:** `go test -race -json -count=3` with the filter above, on
  `internal/tools/native`. The 25 tests each run 3 times: 75 top-level `pass` actions, 0 `fail`, and
  24 subtest `run` actions (not counted in the 75). Worst single drain wait 20.0015s in one run and
  20.0012s in an earlier run, both in `TestRemoteSubagentNeverChangesLocalCheckpointOrLimits`, which
  waits for its run to end at `RemoteTimeoutMs` (20s). The waits were measured with a temporary print,
  not kept in the tree.
- **Bound:** 40s, twice the measured worst drain wait. It was 30s before, which left only 10s of margin.
- **Worst case per test:** a stuck drain costs one drain bound (40s), in `releaseSeamVars` for a test
  that set seams or in the fixture cleanup for one that did not. The fixture cleanup adds no drain wait
  when the test has failed.
  - Sync seam test: the body waits up to `remoteSeamBound` (10s) for its seam, then `releaseSeamVars`
    waits up to one drain bound (40s). Worst case 50s.
  - Async seam test: the body waits up to 10s for its seam, then up to 10s more in the `wait` call
    (`wait_ms` 10000), then `releaseSeamVars` waits up to one drain bound (40s). Worst case 60s.
  - The measured longest drain for a test with no seams is 20.0s, in
    `TestRemoteSubagentNeverChangesLocalCheckpointOrLimits` (no seam setup), the run that stays open to
    `RemoteTimeoutMs`. Measured in the `-race -json -count=3` run above. This is a measurement, not a
    stated worst case.
- **Failing runs:** the fixture cleanup skips its drain wait whenever `t.Failed()` is true, for any
  reason. `releaseSeamVars` does not check `t.Failed()` and always waits. In a failing run a late
  goroutine can still read a later test's seam; this can only change the result of a run that is
  already red.

## Idle session stores leave the cache; commits no longer index every line (R4, Go only, 2026-11-03)

- **`server.session_store_idle_ms` (root setting).** Default `600000` (10 minutes), 0 to 86400000, `0` keeps every store until the session is deleted or the server stops (the old behaviour). A session's transcript store (`sharedSession`: the run lock, the runners with their provider clients, the turn-start and cursor indexes) leaves `Resolver.stores` once nobody has used it for that long. The next request opens it again with the same recovery as the first open after a start (about 180 µs per session in `BenchmarkResolvedSessionsRetention`).
- **Only an unused store leaves.** Two stores of one transcript would each have their own run lock and journal lock. `sharedStore` therefore returns a release with the store, and the entry counts uses; the idle timer starts when the count reaches zero and is cancelled by the next use. `boundRunner` and `boundMutation`, which the HTTP layer keeps between `Resolve` and the call, lease the store again at the start of each operation (`hold`) and use the cached one, never the one they were resolved with. A missing release only keeps that store cached.
- **Measured** (one run in each of 200 sessions with native tools, 95 s after the runs so the provider connections had closed): 25.4 KB held per session before, 6.6 KB after.
- **Not dropped:** the MCP, process, async, sub-agent, task, memory and fetch managers (about 5 KB per session together). An MCP manager owns server connections whose state a user may rely on (a logged-in browser); a process, async or sub-agent manager owns running work and would re-run orphan recovery against it when built again. Each needs its own idle rule before it can be dropped.
- **The duplicate-line index is gone.** `Journal.index` kept one SHA-256 per transcript line (65.7 B/line) for the life of the store, to check that a committed response line was not already in the file. A live commit appends once per turn, so it only compares the line with the transcript's last line (`appendFileUnlessLast`); recovery at store open, where an earlier append may sit deeper in the file, streams the file once (`appendFileIfAbsent`) and keeps nothing.

## exec starts interpreters before the call: standby shims (Go only, 2026-11-03)

- **Why.** Every exec call started its interpreter, and that start was most of the call: through the tool, python took 28–32 ms (18 ms of it the `fn` template), node 24–30 ms, ruby 42–46 ms, against 2–3 ms for bash.
- **How.** A runner with a standby shim (`tools.native.exec.runners.<name>.standby`, or `standby_code` for any other interpreter) gets a started interpreter that waits for the next call: the template is loaded, and the shim blocks reading fd 3. A call takes a waiting one, writes its code to fd 3, and the shim answers on fd 4: `R` when it runs the code, `F` when it cannot run it exactly as the runner's argv would. A replacement starts at once. Output, input, `$JAD_RESULT`, timeout, kill, the process group and async handles are the same supervision as a fresh start (`superviseBashChild`).
- **One call per process.** A standby runs one call and ends, so no state passes between calls. A resident interpreter that runs many calls (sub-millisecond) was rejected: the working directory, environment and globals are shared, one infinite loop blocks every call, and one call cannot be killed alone.
- **Same result as a fresh start.** The pool is keyed by everything the launch is made of: interpreter, argv with the shim, resolved working directory, the whole environment, the template text. A call only takes a standby it would have started itself; a call in another directory, or after a helper folder appeared, starts its own. The built-in shims run the code as `python -c`, `node -e` and `ruby -e` do (same `__main__`, `sys.argv`, `$0`, `[eval]`, `-e`, tracebacks without the shim's frames, `at_exit` order). `TestExecStandbyRunsLikeAFreshInterpreter` compares the rendered results of fresh and standby runs over a corpus. Code a shim cannot compile (a syntax error, ES module syntax or top-level await in node) is declined, and the call starts a fresh interpreter in the same scratch directory, so its error is the interpreter's own.
- **Known differences.** node: an uncaught error's stack also lists the shim's two frames (`Script.runInThisContext`, `[eval]:N`) among node's internal ones. All: standard input is an empty pipe rather than `/dev/null` when the call has no input, and node's `process.execArgv` names the shim. A standby runs the runner's template, which the previous call already ran, before the next call exists.
- **Settings.** `standby_pool` (default 1, 0–16, 0 off) per runner and launch, `standby_idle_ms` (default 300000, at most 6 hours so the 7-hour stale-scratch sweep of a new backend never removes a waiting standby's directory) and `standby_max` (default 8, 1–256) over the whole backend. Built-in shims: python, node (`js`) and ruby; bash, sh, perl and ts start in 2–4 ms and have none. A shim that dies while it waits holds its runner back for `standby_idle_ms`.
- **Measured** (this container, median of 30 calls through the tool catalog, 200 ms between calls): python 32.2 → 9.4 ms, node 29.8 → 9.5 ms, ruby 46.2 → 3.8 ms, bash 3.2 → 3.0 ms (no shim). Calls back to back, when the replacement is still starting: python 16.4, node 14.9, ruby 29.8 ms. Waiting interpreters hold memory: python 10 MB, ruby 19 MB, node 41 MB resident each (idle `VmRSS`).
- **Not proven.** One container and interpreter version each (Python 3.13, Node 22, Ruby 3.3); nothing measured under concurrent sessions or with a real model between calls; the corpus is not every program. python's remaining 9 ms was mostly its template; see the next section.

## The python template imports only what start-up needs (Go only, 2026-11-03)

- **Start-up.** `exec_templates/python.py` loads in every python call (as `sitecustomize`), and it imported `inspect` and `re` at load: 12 ms of its 15 ms (`-X importtime`). It now tests a function with `isinstance(value, types.FunctionType)`, which is what `inspect.isfunction` does, and imports `re` the first time code reads `fn.<name>`. Load: 14.9 → 2.7 ms.
- **First `fn.<name>`.** `importlib.invalidate_caches()` imports `importlib.metadata` in Python 3.10+ (21 ms, with `zipfile`, `pathlib` and `inspect`). The template needs only the helper folder forgotten: it drops a `None` entry kept for a folder that did not exist, and otherwise resets the folder's finder, as `PathFinder.invalidate_caches` does for every path. First helper call: 24 → 4.2 ms. `TestExecPythonFnSeesAHelperInAFolderCreatedAfterStart` pins the first case and `TestExecPythonFnSeesAHelperWrittenIntoAListedFolder` the second; each fails without its branch.
- **Measured** (median of 30 calls through the tool catalog, 200 ms between calls): `print(1)` fresh 31.5 → 17.3 ms, on standby 9.3 → 5.4 ms; `print(fn.double(21))` fresh 45.6 → 22.5 ms, on standby 23.5 → 11.4 ms.
- **Not proven.** Python 3.13 only; other caches the full `invalidate_caches()` reset (other folders' finders, `importlib.metadata`'s) are no longer reset by `fn`, which only looks in its own folder.

## exec runs php, lua, deno and R; fn in perl, php, lua, R and deno; pipe standby (Go only, 2026-11-03)

- **Built-in runners.** `php` (`php -d auto_prepend_file={template} {file}`, a code file, so the code starts with `<?php`), `lua` (`lua -e dofile[[{template}]] -e {code}`), `deno` (`deno run --allow-all --preload {template} {file}`, `.ts`, a second TypeScript runtime beside bun's `ts`) and `r` (`Rscript {file}`, alias `rscript`). php takes a file, not `php -r`, so a waiting interpreter can read it (below); code without `<?php` is printed as text, as php does with any file. `perl` now loads its template with `-MJadFn`.
- **fn templates** (`exec_templates/`): perl `fn->name(...)`, `fn::call('my-help', ...)`, `fn::lib('name')`; php `$fn->name(...)`, `$fn->{'my-help'}(...)`, `$fn->name['member']`; lua `fn.name(...)`, `fn["my-help"]`, with the folder on `package.path`; R `fn$name(...)`, ``fn$`my-help`(...)``, `fn$name$member`; deno as node, with `require` made by `createRequire` and the helper's extension found by the template. A perl, php or lua helper file returns its helper (a function, or a table of them); an R helper is sourced into an environment of its own. Templates are attached where each interpreter loads code before the program: `{template}` in argv (php, lua, deno), a folder in front of `PERL5LIB` (perl, like python's `PYTHONPATH`), `R_PROFILE_USER` (r; it sources the user's `.Rprofile` first, as R would). R's `$` methods are registered (`registerS3method`): since R 3.6, S3 lookup skips attached environments. The listing prints each language's call and reads a php helper's description past `<?php`.
- **`standby: "pipe"`** (mode file only; built-in for php, deno and r). The waiting interpreter is started with the runner's own argv while its code file is a named pipe at the path a fresh start would use; the parent holds the pipe open for reading and writing, so the interpreter's open never blocks, and its read waits for the call's code. The call replaces the path with the code file (written beside it and renamed, never written into the pipe), then writes the code into the pipe and closes it. Argv, file path, environment and working directory are a fresh start's, so no shim and no decline: `TestExecPipeStandbyRunsLikeAFreshInterpreter` compares php, deno and R runs over a corpus that includes errors, exit codes, input, the result file and a program that reads its own file. Writing the code file in place instead breaks it (checked).
- **Not on standby:** lua (3 ms through the tool), perl (6 ms), ts on bun (9 ms): they start fast enough. The perl template does not `use warnings`, which cost 1.3 ms of every start.
- **Measured** (median of 20 calls through the tool catalog, 200 ms apart): php 24.0 → 6.6 ms, deno 40.3 → 10.1 ms, r 118.3 → 8.0 ms.
- **Not proven.** One version each (PHP 8.3, Lua 5.4, Deno 2.9, R 4.3); lua, deno and R were installed in the test container for this, and their tests skip where they are missing. php's `auto_prepend_file` replaces one the user set; R's template ignores an `R_PROFILE_USER` the user set (it sources `.Rprofile` from the working directory, else home).
