Taking a server for a call and the idle stop check and count under one lock, so a call never gets a server that is stopping. A server that died is started again on the next call; its error names the last 4 KiB of its stderr. `jad`, `jad run` and `jad eval` stop all servers when they end; a server also ends when jad dies and its stdin closes.
- **Files.** Before each call the file's text is sent (`didOpen` once, then `didChange` with the whole text when its SHA-256 changed), so the server sees the file as saved on disk, including edits by write, edit and bash. diagnostics returns stored diagnostics at once for an unchanged file and otherwise waits up to `diagnostics_wait_ms` for the server to publish, then says when nothing new came.
- **Permissions.** Read-only (changes no file; read-only sub-agents get it). Its own `paths` (read_style; default the workspace) decide which files may be asked about, and which result lines show their text: a location outside (for example the Go standard library) shows only path and position. Servers start with the bash environment allowlist plus `env`, in the workspace root, and are not covered by the `sandbox` setting (like MCP servers). `request_timeout_ms` 30000, `diagnostics_wait_ms` 3000, `idle_shutdown_ms` 600000, `max_results` 50, `max_file_bytes` 2 MiB by default.
- **Tests.** A fake server built into the test binary (`TestMain`, `JAD_FAKE_LSP=1`) over real pipes: it asks `workspace/configuration` before answering `initialize` and refuses a wrong answer; diagnostics with source and code, stored ones returned at once for an unchanged file, a changed file sent as version 2; `é` and `𝒳` columns converted both ways; links and plain locations, a location outside the workspace without text; references cut at `max_results`; hover, nested symbols, workspace symbols; 7 refusals; a crashing server reports its stderr and the next call starts a new one; a missing program; a silent server bounded by `diagnostics_wait_ms`; one server reused across tool instances, stopped when idle and started again. Real servers (skipped where missing): gopls v0.21 (installed into a scratch directory for this work) gives the compiler diagnostic, a cross-file definition, references, hover with documentation, symbols and workspace symbols; pyright 1.1 gives the type error, the definition and hover. `-race`, three runs; no server process left running afterwards. sessionfs: defaults, values, 18 refusals, extension lookup, 13 schema/loader cases. The sample manifest loads and equals the code's schema.
- **Not covered.** No rename, code actions, formatting or completion (they change files or need an editor). typescript-language-server and rust-analyzer were not run. A server that needs project setup (a built `node_modules`, `cargo metadata`) answers only once that is done. Workspace symbols depend on what the server indexed.

## `tools.lazy` entries count as allowed (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** A lazy tool had to be listed twice: in `tools.lazy` and again in `tools.allow`, since `checkLazyEntriesVisible` refused a lazy entry outside a non-empty allow list. A lazy profile also had to put `load_tool` in its allow list (`checkLazyLoadToolShown`). Both repeats carried no information.
- **Now.** `tools.ProfileShows`, the one rule every surface uses (the model's listing, the tools route, the lazy set, the tool_smith bridge), counts a `tools.lazy` entry as allowed, and `load_tool` as allowed while `tools.lazy` is non-empty. `deny` still wins over both: a lazy entry that deny covers, and a lazy profile whose deny names `load_tool`, are still configuration errors, and their messages now name only deny. Writing a lazy tool or `load_tool` in allow as well is still accepted, so existing profiles load unchanged and see the same tools. A whole-server lazy entry (`mcp:<server>:*`) counts as allowed only while allow names nothing of that server (`tools.lazyAllows`): with `allow: ["mcp:srv:a"]` and `lazy: ["mcp:srv:*"]` only `a` is shown, as before lazy entries counted as allowed (corrected 2026-11-05 after the impact trace: the first version showed every tool of `srv`, to the model, tool_smith and sub-agents alike). A lazy entry that names one tool is always allowed: before, it had to be in allow, so no existing profile changes. `tools` still needs `allow` or `deny`; `{"allow": [], "lazy": [...]}` shows only the lazy tools and `load_tool`.
- **Tests.** `ProfileShows` cases (lazy not in allow, an MCP wildcard, an empty allow, load_tool with and without lazy tools, deny over both, an extension named load_tool; a lazy wildcard beside an allow that names another tool of the server, this tool, the whole server, a server with a longer name, and an exact lazy entry beside it). The two checks: an allow that omits a lazy tool or load_tool now passes, deny still refuses. End to end: a profile with `allow: [mcp:fixture:lookup]` and `lazy: [read]` loads, lists `load_tool` and not `read`, and lists `read` after `load_tool` loads it; `deny: [load_tool]` is refused naming tools.deny.

## Hook events `prompt_submit` and `subagent_stop`; a blocking `pre_compact` (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** Hooks could gate a tool call (`pre_tool` with `blocking`) but not the prompt itself, could not give the model context, saw nothing of sub-agents, and `pre_compact` could only watch a compaction it might want to stop (for example while an outside snapshot of the transcript is being taken).
- **`prompt_submit`.** Runs in `boundRunner.Run` after the run lock, before `PersistInputWithImages`, so before the model and tools are loaded. Stdin: `event, session, profile, turn_id, workspace_root, prompt` (the `input_text` parts of the input's user messages, one per line) and `prompt_cut`. Entry keys `name, argv, on_failure_only, blocking, context`. A blocking entry that does not exit 0 returns `*native.HookBlockedError`: nothing is stored, no provider request is made, the later commands do not start, and the client's error event reads "The prompt_submit hook <name> blocked the prompt; nothing was stored." (`ClientMessage`; the report goes to the log and to `hook_report` events like every run hook's). The trimmed stdout of each `context` entry that exits 0 is joined (`[prompt_submit hook <name>]` headers) into one `{"type":"message","role":<hooks.prompt_context_role>,"content":[{"type":"input_text",...}]}` item stored in the same input batch, after the input. The model key variable is read with `WithModel` only to keep it out of the commands' environment; a model file that does not load leaves the allowlist as the only filter, and the run then fails as before.
- **`subagent_stop`.** Fired by `subagentLauncher.Run` after every child run that started, in the session that called the tool, on `context.WithoutCancel`: `agent, call_id, rounds, tokens, answer` (the answer, or the last text of a failed child), `outcome` ok/error/cancelled, `error`. Log only; it never changes the tool result. External and remote sub-agents do not fire it.
- **`pre_compact` blocking.** A failing blocking entry skips the attempt: the automatic one returns `CompactionResult{}` and the run continues (the next response over the trigger fires the hook again); the manual one answers 409 with the client message. Accepting `blocking` here replaces the old refusal of the key.
- **Payload.** `encodeRunEvent` cuts the prompt, answer and error to half of `max_payload_bytes` each and then the longest until the JSON fits, on a character boundary, setting `*_cut`. `hooks.prompt_context_role` is `developer` (default) or `system`; `null` is refused.
- **messages.jsonl.** Unchanged unless a `prompt_submit` entry sets `context` and prints something: then one more input record, of an existing shape (a message item with batch fields), follows the user message. The web UI projection shows only `role: user` messages, so it is not displayed; it is part of the history every later request sends, and compaction keeps or drops it with its turn.
- **Tests.** sessionfs: the new lists, `blocking`/`context` per event, 14 refusals, schema keys equal the loader's per list. native: block stops later commands, context only from passing context commands that printed, a passing blocking command blocks nothing, payload cutting under escapes, list selection, client messages. app (HTTP, real child processes): context stored after the input and sent after the prompt; a blocked prompt leaves messages.jsonl byte-identical with no provider call; a blocked manual compaction answers 409 with the transcript unchanged; an automatic compaction keeps the old turn when blocked and drops it when not; a sub-agent's stop payload.
- **Not covered.** No hook can rewrite the prompt or edit compaction (compaction drops turns; it writes no summary to steer). No `session_start` event: `run_start` on the first run plays that part.

## OpenAI `prompt_cache_key` per session, opt-in (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** OpenAI routes requests to its prompt cache by prefix hash and, when given, `prompt_cache_key`; a stable key per conversation keeps a long session on the same cache. jad sent none.
- **Now.** Model file `prompt_cache_key`: `off` (default) or `session`. With `session`, `ModelSettings.PromptCacheKey` is `jad-` plus the first 16 bytes of SHA-256 of the session's messages path, hex (`promptCacheKey`), sent by `BuildRequestWithStateContext` on every request; a sub-agent hashes the parent's messages path plus the agent name, so the children of one agent share a key. The key holds no path or name. It is part of the runner fingerprint.
- **Off by default.** A Responses-compatible server other than OpenAI may refuse an unknown field. `session` needs `api: responses` and cannot be combined with `request.prompt_cache_key` (a fixed key); both are load errors. A fallback model on another api drops the field in its translation, as before.
- **Tests.** sessionfs: default, both values, five refusals. session: the field is sent only when set and leaves other request fields alone. app: two runs of a session send the same `jad-` digest, a model without the key sends none, children of a `session` child model share one key while the parent sends none.
- **Not proven.** No real OpenAI call was made, so the hit rate was not measured.

## Structured final output: `jad run --output-schema`, run body `output_format` (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** Scripts and CI that call `jad run` had to parse free text. Providers can constrain the final answer to a JSON Schema; jad had no way to ask.
- **Now.** The run route takes an optional `output_format` member: a Responses `text.format` of type `json_schema` (`name`, `description`, `schema`, `strict`). It is checked before the run starts (400 for another type, a bad name, unknown members, or a schema that does not compile, external `$ref` included), re-encoded, kept on the active run and handed to the runner as `RunOptions.OutputFormat`. The runner sends it as `text.format` on every request of the run (`ModelSettings.TextFormat`), next to any other `text` fields of the model file's `request` (a `text.format` there is replaced). It never reaches `messages.jsonl`. `jad run --output-schema <file>` reads and compiles the schema (exit 2 when it does not), sends it with `strict: true` (`--output-strict=false` to turn strict off), and when the run completes reads the answer as JSON (one whole ```` ```json ```` fence is removed, for providers that do not constrain) and validates it. A match is printed as compact JSON in text format and set as `output` in the result; anything else gives status `invalid_output`, exit 1, the reason on stderr (at most five mismatches named, each cut to 200 bytes), and nothing on stdout in text format.
- **Providers.** `responses` sends the field as is. `chat_completions` already translated it to `response_format`. The Anthropic translation (in process and the standalone adapter) now sends a `json_schema` format as `output_config.format` `{"type":"json_schema","schema":...}` next to any effort; `name`, `description` and `strict` are dropped. A new profile key `structured_output` (`native` default, `unsupported`) sends models without structured outputs through `unsupported_fields` as before; the sample sets `unsupported` on `default_profile`. `json_object` still goes through `unsupported_fields`.
- **Not done.** The server does not validate the answer itself: an API client that sends `output_format` gets the provider's constraint only. `jad eval` cases take no schema yet. (Both came with "Model on profile_message and subagent, ...".) Schema features a provider does not support (OpenAI strict mode needs every property required and `additionalProperties: false`; Anthropic refuses numeric and length constraints) are the provider's 400, not rewritten.
- **Tests.** cmd/jad: the provider receives `text.format` with the schema and strict on and off, a fenced answer prints as compact JSON, the transcript holds no schema, a run without the flag sends no `text`; not JSON, a wrong type and a missing member each give `invalid_output` and exit 1 with nothing on stdout; five usage errors exit 2. httpapi: the parser (2 accepted, null, 10 refused) and the route (format reaches the runner, a plain run gets none, a bad schema is a 400 that never reaches the runner). session: `text` merge in five shapes and a non-object `text` refused. anthropicadapter: native, with effort, `unsupported` with ignore and reject, a non-object schema, a bad `structured_output`. headless: fence handling, issue bounds, rune-safe cut.
- **Not proven.** No real OpenAI or Anthropic call was made.

## `memory` search and delete (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** The tool had `create`, `get`, `list` and `update`: a record that stopped being true could only be overwritten, a full type could never be freed, and finding a record meant paging through `list` with exact-match filters.
- **Search.** `{"operation":"search","type","query", filter?, limit?, order?, cursor?}` is `list` with one more condition: every whitespace-separated word of `query` appears, ignoring case (`strings.ToLower`), in some string or number value of the record at any depth. Keys are not searched; different words may match different values. There is no index: it scans under the same `max_scan_records`/`max_scan_bytes` as `list`, and the new `tools.native.memory.max_query_bytes` (default 1024, 1 to 65536) bounds the query. A search cursor stores the normalized query, which is part of its fingerprint, so it continues only the same search; a list cursor's fingerprint is unchanged, so cursors written before this still work.
- **Delete.** `{"operation":"delete","type","ref", if_match?}` removes the record file; with `if_match` a stale ETag is a `memory_conflict` with `current_etag`, and the conflict now names the operation it came from (it said `update` for every conflict before). A malformed record can be deleted. The type directory is removed with its last record, so the type stops counting toward `max_types` (the quota scan counts every type directory). No trash, no undo.
- **Cursors survive a delete.** A cursor's position is the last record of the previous page. It used to fail with "memory cursor position was not found" when that record was gone, which a list-then-delete loop would hit on every page. Record names sort in creation order, so the page now goes on after the place the missing name would have had (for a legacy v1 cursor, whose position is inclusive, it starts at that place).
- **Definition file.** The schema gained `search`, `delete` and `query`, so `sample/tools/native/memory.json` must be swapped together with the binary: the loader requires the file to equal the code schema exactly, in both orders. Copies of the old manifest under a local `.jad` fail to load until they are replaced.
- **messages.jsonl.** Unchanged for the four old operations (the create golden and the list and get checks pass as before); the new operations write ordinary tool results.
- **Tests.** `tools/native/memory_search_test.go`: delete with a stale and the right ETag, not-found after it, capacity freed, the empty type directory removed, a malformed record deleted, a ref of another type refused; a cursor continuing after its record was deleted, in both orders; search across fields, nested values, numbers and case, keys not matched, with a filter, cursor continuation, cursors of another query or of a list refused both ways, blank and too-long queries; a list fingerprint unchanged; the tool's delete, conflict, not-found with `available_refs`, search output and four refused argument shapes. `sessionfs`: `max_query_bytes` default, maximum and two refused values.

## Trigger notifications: `server.triggers.notify` (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** A scheduled or webhook run that finished, failed or timed out told nobody; the outcome was only in `GET /api/triggers`, in memory, for whoever polled it.
- **Now.** `server.triggers.notify` holds named notifiers and a trigger lists the ones it uses in `notify` (a name that is not defined stops the server at load). A notifier is an HTTP POST to `url`, or to the address in the environment variable `url_env` when the address is itself a secret (Slack, a private ntfy topic). `format` `json` (default) sends the run record as the list shows it, plus `text` (one line: trigger, status, session, source, duration, cost) and, when `answer_bytes` > 0 (default 0), `answer` with the start of the final answer and `answer_truncated`; `format` `text` sends the line, the error and the answer as `text/plain`, which ntfy-style services show as the message. `headers` are sent as written, `headers_env` read a value (a token) from the environment; Host and Content-Length are refused, a header may not be in both, a value must be one line, and a missing variable stops the server like a missing webhook secret. `on` picks the statuses (default every final one except `skipped`, so a schedule that outruns its runs does not report each firing).
- **Delivery.** One goroutine per notifier per run, counted in the manager's wait group so `Close` waits for them. An attempt has `timeout_ms` (10000); a failed one (transport error or a status outside 2xx) is retried `retries` times (2), `retry_delay_ms` apart (2000). Once the server stops, the attempt in progress ends and no retry starts, so shutdown is held up by at most one attempt. Each run in `GET /api/triggers` carries `notifications` `[{notifier, status: pending|sent|failed, attempts, error}]`, and a failure is logged. An error never repeats the address: a `*url.Error` is unwrapped and a load error does not quote the URL. The request goes through the default transport, so `HTTPS_PROXY` applies.
- **Not done.** No message templates; no per-service payloads (Slack wants `{"text"}` and gets the record, whose `text` member is that; whether Slack accepts the extra members was not checked); no signing of the notification; delivery results are in memory only, like the history; nothing outside triggers (a `jad run` or a web-client run) notifies.
- **Tests.** `triggers/notify_test.go` (`-race`): a completed run goes to the json notifier with the env URL, a literal and an env header and the record fields, to the text notifier with the answer cut on a rune boundary, and not to a failed-only one; the trigger list shows `notify`; a 502 then 200 is sent in two attempts, three 500s fail with `HTTP 500`, an unreachable address fails without its secret path in the error; `skipped` is sent only when listed; `Close` during a 60 s retry delay returns at once with the delivery `failed` after one attempt; `New` refuses an unset or non-http `url_env` and an unset or two-line header variable. `sessionfs`: values and defaults, 25 refusals, and 21 schema-versus-loader cases.
- **Not proven.** No real ntfy, Slack or other service was called.

## Fork a session: `POST /api/sessions/<ref>/fork` (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** There was no way to branch a conversation: to try another answer from an earlier point, the only tools were checkpoints (files only) and reset (everything).
- **Now.** `POST /api/sessions/<ref>/fork?profile=<category/name>` with `{"session": "<new ref>", "message_id"?: "<id>", "cursor"?: <turn cursor>, "keep_turn"?: true}` answers 201 `{session, profile, turns, bytes, images}`. The fork point is the turn that holds `message_id` (any message id of the session snapshot, found by normalizing the live transcript as the snapshot does) or the turn that starts at `cursor`; `keep_turn: false` ends the copy just before that turn, so the new session can ask that turn's question again; with neither, the whole transcript is copied. The session file is copied under the new name (created with `If-None-Match: *`, so an existing session is 412), and the profile's `messages.jsonl` up to that turn is written line for line, so the fork's transcript is a byte prefix of the source's and `messages.jsonl`'s format is untouched. The compaction backup (the history block) is copied whole, and the stored images that the copied lines and the backup refer to are copied when the two sessions' image folders differ (names are content hashes, so a file already there is kept).
- **Not copied.** Caches (compaction, usage, cursor, run error) are derived and rebuilt by the new session; the compaction cache records the hash of the transcript it was built from, so it would not have matched a prefix anyway. Tool storage (memory, tasks, processes, sub-agent runs), the statecontext snapshot and checkpoints start empty. Only the named profile is copied; the fork's other profiles start empty. The session file keeps its `name`, so the fork shows under the same display name until it is edited.
- **Rules.** The source is read under its mutation gate (no compaction or reset in between) and the session locks of both sessions, taken in name order so two forks never wait on each other; a run active on the source profile is 409, since its user message is already written and its answer is not. The new session must be in the source's session root (400 otherwise; a root that does not exist is 404), so relative paths in the copied session file mean the same. A message of the compacted history, an unknown message or a cursor that starts no turn is 404. A state folder left by an earlier session of the new name that holds a transcript for the profile is 409, and the session file just created is removed again, as on any failure after it.
- **Not done.** No web client button; the API only (the button came with "Model on profile_message and subagent, ..."). No fork of a sub-agent's or another profile's transcript in the same call.
- **Tests.** `app/session_fork_test.go` through the real service and provider: three turns; a fork before the second turn holds one user message and is a byte prefix of the source; a fork through the second turn holds two, then runs a turn of its own while the source transcript stays byte-identical; a whole fork equals the source; nine refusals (existing target 412, same session 400, unknown message and cursor 404, both fork points, `keep_turn` without one, no session and an unknown field 400, another root 404) leave no session file; a fork during an active run is 409 and works once the run ends; image copying copies a named file once, skips a missing one, an invalid name and the same folder. `-race` on these and the reset tests.

## MCP resources, prompts and OAuth; `jad mcp login` (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** The MCP client only listed and called tools. A server's resources (files, documents, records) and prompt templates could not be reached, and a remote server that asks for OAuth could not be used except by pasting a long-lived token into `headers_from`.
- **Resources and prompts.** A toolset's `mcp` entry takes `resources: true` and `prompts: true` (both off by default, so no session's tool list changes). They add tools beside the server's own, named like its tools (`mcp_<server>_<name>_<digest>`, IDs `mcp:<server>:resources/list` and so on; the slash keeps them from colliding with a tool the server lists): `resources/list` (`kind` `resources` or `templates`, `cursor`; a JSON listing with `next_cursor`), `resources/read` (`uri`; text as text, an image as an image, other binary data as a file, as an embedded resource of a tool result is shown), `prompts/list` (`cursor`; names, descriptions and arguments) and `prompts/get` (`name`, `arguments` as strings; each message as a `[role]` line and its content). Calls go one at a time per connection with the server's `timeout_ms`, like tool calls; every result is cut to `max_result_bytes`; a refusal is `{"status":"error",...,"error":"McpError"}` naming only the kind of failure, as for tools. A server that does not announce the capability the entry asks for fails the run with the key named.
- **OAuth.** A `streamable_http` transport takes `oauth`: `grant` `authorization_code` (default) or `client_credentials`, `client_id`, `client_secret_from` (an environment variable, as `env_from`), `scopes`, `redirect_url` (default `http://127.0.0.1:47681/callback`) and `refresh_margin_ms` (default 60000). With `authorization_code`, `jad mcp login <category>/<name>` connects, gets the server's 401, follows its protected-resource and authorization-server metadata, registers a public client dynamically when there is no `client_id`, prints the sign-in address (PKCE, `resource` set) and waits for the browser at the loopback `redirect_url`, or for the address the browser was sent to, pasted on stdin (an https `redirect_url` is paste only). It stores the client, the token endpoint and the tokens in `<state>/.mcp-oauth/<category>/<name>.json` (mode 0600; the leading dot keeps it apart from session folders); a secret from `client_secret_from` is never stored. A run sends the stored access token, refreshes it `refresh_margin_ms` before it expires (at most half its lifetime, so short-lived tokens are not refreshed before every request) and once more when the server answers 401, and writes a rotated refresh token back. Reading and refreshing hold the file's lock, so two sessions or processes do not spend one refresh token twice. `jad mcp logout` removes the file. With `client_credentials` no sign-in or file is needed: the SDK gets a token from the token endpoint the metadata names.
- **Rules.** A run never starts a sign-in (JAD asks nobody): no token file, a file for another `url`, a refused refresh (`invalid_grant`) or a 403 `insufficient_scope` fail the run with `needs an OAuth sign-in: run jad mcp login <category>/<name>`. A token error names only its HTTP status and error code, never the token endpoint's body. `oauth` and an `Authorization` header in `headers_from` are refused together. The OAuth settings and the resolved secret are part of the connection fingerprint. Outside the catalog layout there is no token folder, so `authorization_code` is refused there.
- **Not done.** Sampling: the 2026-07-28 MCP protocol deprecates it (SEP-2577), so the client does not offer it. Elicitation: JAD asks nobody, so the client does not announce it and a server that needs it gets the protocol's refusal. Prompts were tools for the model only; the `/` menu and `jad run` reach them since the slash-commands entry below. Resource subscriptions and change notifications, and `roots`, are not used. No sign-in from the web client.
- **Tests.** `tools/mcp/features_test.go` against an in-process streamable HTTP server: without the keys only the server's tool loads; with both, four more with distinct MCP names; list resources and templates, read a text, an image and a templated resource, a missing one as an error result; list prompts with their required argument, get one with an argument; the schemas refuse an unknown `kind` and a non-string argument; a server without resources or prompts is refused per key; a listing over `max_result_bytes` is cut. `tools/mcp/oauth_test.go` against a fake protected server with its own authorization server (`-race`): before sign-in the run names the login command; login through the loopback redirect registers a client and stores a 0600 file that prints as redacted; a run uses the token; after revocation a call refreshes once, retries and stores the rotated refresh token; a dead refresh token asks for sign-in again without the server's error text; logout removes the file once; a pasted redirect with a registered `client_id` does not register; a token 10 s from expiry is refreshed once before use and the new 30 s token is not refreshed on each of three calls; a token stored for another URL is not sent; `client_credentials` connects with the right secret, not with a wrong one, and needs no login; `authorization_code` without a token path is refused. `config_test.go`: defaults, 18 refusals, the fingerprint changes with the secret, and 18 schema-versus-loader cases. `sessionfs`: the toolset keys reach the reference, a typo and a non-boolean are refused, the token path is under `.mcp-oauth`, and the schema agrees on five entries. `cmd/jad/mcp_test.go`: logout with and without tokens, and seven usage and lookup refusals.
- **Not proven.** No real OAuth provider or public MCP server (GitHub, Linear, Notion and the like) was signed in to; the flow was checked against the fake server only, which follows the 2025-11-25 authorization spec as the Go SDK reads it.

## Plugin bundles: `jad plugin install|remove|list` (Go only, 2026-11-04)

- **Why.** Skills, hooks, MCP servers and agents were each configured separately, with no way to install a set of them that belong together, upgrade it, or take it back out.
- **Now.** A plugin is a folder with `plugin.json` (`schema_version` 1, `name`, optional `version` and `description`; `sample/schemes/plugin.schema.json`) and any of `models/`, `mcp/`, `tools/` (toolsets, which carry hooks), `profiles/` (agents), `skills/<skill>/` and `files/`. `jad plugin install <folder>` copies each catalog file to `<catalog>/<plugin name>/<file>.json`, so the plugin's own files name each other as `<plugin>/<file>`; each skill folder to the first of `libraries.skill_dirs`, so it is found before a skill of the same name further down; and `files/` to `<state>/.plugins/<name>/files` (file modes kept, so a script stays executable), whose path replaces `${JAD_PLUGIN_FILES}` in the catalog files, JSON-escaped, so an MCP server or a hook can run what the plugin ships. The record `<state>/.plugins/<name>.json` lists every installed part with its SHA-256 (a folder's is over its files' relative paths and hashes). `jad plugin list` shows each plugin, its version, its source and how many parts were changed since; `jad plugin remove <name>` takes back exactly the recorded parts. Installing an installed plugin upgrades it: changed parts are replaced and parts the new version drops are removed.
- **Rules.** Catalog files go through the catalog editor's own write (`WriteCatalogFile`: the loader's checks, If-None-Match for a new file, If-Match for the version being replaced) in the order models, MCP servers, toolsets, profiles, so what a file names is in place and checked first; removal goes through its delete, profiles first, which refuses a profile a session needs, a toolset a profile outside the plugin uses, and so on. A skill is read back the way the skill tool reads it. Every step is journaled: a failed check, a refused delete or a write error undoes the steps already taken (files restored, folders renamed back) and leaves no staging folder. A file that exists where the plugin writes and is not in its record is never overwritten, even with `--force`; a recorded part changed by hand since stops install and remove unless `--force`. Anything at the top of the plugin folder that is not a plugin part (except hidden entries and README, LICENSE and CHANGELOG files), a catalog file in a subfolder or not named `<name>.json`, a skill folder without `SKILL.md`, a link or special file, and `${JAD_PLUGIN_FILES}` without a `files/` folder are refused before anything is written. One plugin's installs and removals hold a lock beside its record. The catalogs are read on every request and the skill folders on every skill call, so an install needs no restart.
- **Not done.** No install from a git URL or an archive (clone or unpack it first); no signature or checksum of the source; no dependency between plugins; no web-client view; no install into a second layout (the catalogs are global to the root setting). Prompt documents and templates are not plugin parts. `${JAD_PLUGIN_FILES}` is the only substitution.
- **Tests.** `plugins/plugins_test.go` on the test layout: an install places the MCP server (its command naming the installed, executable script), toolset, profile, skill folder and files, and `jad check`'s own check finds no problem; list shows it unchanged; a reinstall is an upgrade; remove takes all of it back, folders included, with no staging or backup left. An upgrade over a hand-edited toolset is refused with that file named and changes nothing; forced, it replaces the toolset and removes the dropped profile and skill. A skill folder of the user's at the plugin's path is refused even with `--force`; a profile naming a missing model fails the install and undoes the MCP server, toolset, skill and files already written; an invalid `SKILL.md` is undone too. Removing while a session's scope names the plugin's profile is refused and changes nothing. Ten refusals of a malformed plugin folder, and a link in a skill. The manifest schema agrees with the reader on ten manifests. `cmd/jad/plugin_test.go`: install, list, upgrade and remove through the command, with the skill in the default skill folder, and six usage and failure exits.
- **Not proven.** No third-party plugin was installed; the format is JAD's own, not another harness's plugin format.

## Slash commands in `jad run`; MCP prompts in the `/` menu (Go and web client, 2026-11-04)

- **Headless.** `jad run` reads the web client's `/` words at the start of a prompt and reaches them through the same routes the web client calls, in process: `/compact`, `/reset` and `/state [text]` run the session action and end without a turn (text format prints a one-line summary; `json` and `jsonl` give the result with `command` and `command_result`, the route's answer, and a compaction's `usage`); `/prompt:<name>`, `/template:<name>` and `/skill:<name>` send the library item's text, followed by the rest of the prompt on a new paragraph; `/mcp:<server>:<prompt> [arg=value ...] [text]` sends the MCP prompt filled with its arguments. `<name>` is the menu's slug (the name in lower case, every run of characters that are not letters or digits one dash, `-2`, `-3` ... after a taken one, in the library's order); `headless.LibrarySlug` is the Go copy of `librarySlug`, tested on the same cases. An MCP prompt is found by its exact name or its slug; a value may be double-quoted with `\"` and `\\`; a prompt with one argument and no `name=value` word takes the whole text. A first word that is none of these (`/etc/hosts ...`) is a prompt as before; `--no-commands` sends a command word as a prompt too. `/compact` and `/reset` with text, and any action with `--model` or `--output-schema`, are usage errors (exit 2); an unknown library item, an MCP prompt missing a required argument and a refused route exit 1 with the reason; `--timeout` and an interrupt bound the command as they bound a run (124, 130), and a run after an expansion gets what is left of the timeout. `jad eval` does not read commands: a case's input is sent as written.
- **Piped prompts are data (impact trace C12, 2026-11-05).** A prompt read from standard input that is not a terminal (a pipe or a file) is sent as written, as with `--no-commands`: `cat notes.md | jad run ...` must never reset or compact the session because the file starts with `/reset`. A prompt given as arguments, or typed at a terminal, still runs its command; to run one from a script, pass it as an argument. `jad eval` is unchanged (its prompts come from the suite file and its own `commands` key). Tests: `TestRunSlashCommands` (piped `/reset everything above` and `/compact` with `-` are sent as text and the history stays; it failed before with "/reset takes no text"), `TestIsTerminalRefusesPipesFilesAndReaders`.
- **MCP prompts for the person.** `GET /api/sessions/<ref>/mcp-prompts` lists the prompts of every enabled server whose toolset entry has `prompts: true` (`server`, `name`, `title`, `description`, `arguments` with `required`), asked at the same time on the profile's shared MCP manager, so a run's connection is reused; a server that cannot be reached or does not offer prompts is in `failures` with the reason and the others are still listed; each server's list stops at its `max_tools`. `POST /api/sessions/<ref>/mcp-prompts/get` with `{"server","name","arguments"}` returns `{"prompt":{...,"text"}}`: the messages' text joined by blank lines, each message under a `[role]` line when any is not the user's, a resource's text in place, and a note for an image, audio or binary resource. It writes nothing to the session. Unlike a tool result, a refusal carries the server's own JSON-RPC message (cut to `max_description_bytes`), because the person needs it to fix an argument. A text over the server's `max_result_bytes` is refused, not cut. A server not in the profile's toolsets with `prompts: true` is 404, the server's failure is 502. Both routes are in the request log's route list, and so is `fork`, which was missing from it.
- **Web client.** The `/` menu lists the MCP prompts after the library, as `/mcp:<server>:<slug>`, loaded with the library and refreshed on the same `slash_menu_refresh_ms`; the two loads are independent, so either failing leaves the other listed. A prompt without arguments is inserted at once; one with arguments opens a dialog with a field per argument (required ones must have text; empty optional ones are not sent). The text is inserted like a library prompt, as a badge named `<server>:<prompt>` past the paste-as-token threshold. `slash_menu_mcp_prompts_enabled` (default true) in `frontend_new/src/config.json` turns the MCP entries off. The insert-failure notice is now "Slash menu item not inserted".
- **Not done.** No custom slash commands of the user's own, no plan mode, no `/` commands in `jad eval`, and no menu entry for MCP resources. Plan mode and `jad eval` commands came with the next entry.
- **Tests.** `headless/commands_test.go`: the command grammar (17 inputs), `Check`, the slug and numbering against the web client's cases, the three actions on their routes with no turn started, `Commands` off sending `/compact` as a prompt, a refused action, an action stopped by the timeout and by an interrupt, library items by slug with the rest of the prompt after them and the known names in a refusal, and MCP prompts with named and quoted arguments, the one-argument shortcut, a slug match and six refusals. `tools/mcp/user_prompts_test.go` on real SDK servers: only enabled servers with `prompts: true` are listed, a server that cannot be reached is a failure beside the others' prompts, role labels and content notes, not-found and a server refusal, a prompt over `max_result_bytes` and a server without prompts. `httpapi/mcp_prompt_handlers_test.go`: both routes, five refused bodies, a 502 passed through, 501 without a provider. `cmd/jad/slash_test.go`: `jad run` against the real service with a library prompt file and a real MCP server, `--no-commands`, `/reset`, two usage errors and two failures. Web client: `slash-commands.test.mjs` (entries, independent loads, argument rules) and `mcp-prompts-api.test.mjs` (both calls, malformed answers, a refusal); a browser check against a real backend and MCP server listed both prompts under `/mcp`, opened the dialog with Insert disabled until the required field had text, and put the filled prompt in the draft.

## Plan mode; slash commands in `jad eval` (Go and web client, 2026-11-04)

- **Plan mode without approval.** Rule 0 stands: nothing stops for a person. A plan-mode run is a run whose model sees fewer tools and one more message: `tools.Catalog.PlanMode` keeps the tools marked `ReadOnly` (read, grep, glob, lsp, fetch, skill, transcript, async and the like) and the ones `plan_mode.allow_tools` names (whole, as tools.allow entries); a `ReadOnly` tool that decides per call (`MayChangeFiles`, the sub-agent tool) stays listed, and a call that could change files is refused before it runs with status `forbidden` and `error_code` `plan_mode`. MCP tools are not `ReadOnly`, so they are out unless allowed. Hosted tools from the model file stay: they run at the provider. The `plan_mode.instructions` message (role `plan_mode.role`, `developer` by default) is sent after the history and every state-context block in each request of the run (`RunOptions.PlanPrompt`), and never reaches `messages.jsonl`, so the transcript format is unchanged; only the request's tail differs, which keeps the cached prefix. The run ends with the plan; the next run without plan mode does the work, so the person's next message is the approval.
- **Where it is asked for.** The run route takes `"plan": true` (anything but a boolean is 400 `plan must be a boolean`). `jad run --plan`, and `/plan <prompt>` at the start of a prompt, which may itself start with a library, MCP or custom command (`/plan /prompt:review main.go`); `/plan` alone, `/plan /compact`, and an action with `--plan` are usage errors. The result has `"plan": true`. The web client has a Plan switch in the composer's top row and a `/plan` entry in the `/` menu that flips it; it is per view, stays on until switched off, and changes the placeholder (`plan_mode_enabled`, `plan_mode_placeholder` in `frontend_new/src/config.json`).
- **Setting.** `plan_mode` in a settings object (root `defaults`, session, profile): `instructions` (default in `sessionfs.DefaultPlanModeInstructions` and the schema, at most 64 KiB), `role` (`developer` or `system`) and `allow_tools`; unknown keys, a bad role, an unknown tool and a repeated entry are refused like `tools.allow`'s.
- **`jad eval`.** A case prompt is now read as `jad run` reads it (`commands`, default true, on the suite or the case; `--no-commands` for the whole run), so library, MCP, custom commands and `/plan` work in a case. An action (`/compact`, `/reset`, `/state`) runs no turn, so a case prompt that is one is refused when the suite loads, as is what `jad run` refuses as a usage error. `plan` on the suite or the case runs it in plan mode. This changes what an existing suite does only for a case prompt that starts with one of those words; such a prompt now needs `"commands": false`.
- **Not done.** No read-only sandbox: plan mode limits the tools, not the processes, so a tool marked `ReadOnly` by mistake would still run. A tool loaded through `load_tool` that plan mode left out stays out. No plan file; the web client does not mark plan-mode turns in the transcript. (The plan-to-work handoff came with "Plugin commands, command run options, plan handoff, run time limits".)
- **Tests.** `sessionfs/plan_mode_settings_test.go`: defaults, every key, 11 refusals, and the schema's default text, maximum and role list against the loader. `tools/plan_mode_test.go`: kept tools and their order, a hidden write call answers unknown_tool, a read-only sub-agent call runs and a writing one is refused with `plan_mode` and the call id, an allow entry keeps a tool whole, the receiver is unchanged. `session/plan_mode_test.go`: the plan message is the last input item after the prompt, a state block and the history, with its own role, is not in `messages.jsonl`, and is absent from the next run. `httpapi/run_plan_test.go`: five accepted bodies and three refused. `headless/commands_test.go`: `/plan` parsing, eight `Check` cases, and the run body's `plan` for `/plan`, `/plan /prompt:...` and `Plan`. `cmd/jad/plan_test.go`: against the real service and a stand-in provider, `--plan` and `/plan` send only `grep`, `read` and the allowed `task` (not `write` or `edit`) and end the input with the configured instructions, a plain run sends all five and no instructions, the transcript holds neither, and three usage errors. `evalrun/commands_test.go` and `cmd/jad/headless_test.go`: commands and plan reach each case's request by suite and case values, actions and bad `/plan` prompts are refused at load unless commands are off, `--no-commands` overrules a case, and a `/plan` case passes through `jad eval` with `plan` in its report. Web client: `slash-commands.test.mjs` (the `/plan` entry, the list without it), `api-stream.test.mjs` (`plan` in the body only when set), `composer-plan-mode.test.mjs` (config keys and wiring).

## Custom slash commands; MCP resources in the `/` menu (Go and web client, 2026-11-04)

- **Custom commands.** A new library kind, `command`, read from `libraries.command_dirs` (default `.agent/commands`, beside the other kinds' defaults). A Markdown file is a command whose word is its path below the folder without `.md`, each part a slug (`contentlibrary.Slug`, which `headless.LibrarySlug` and the web client's `librarySlug` match) and the parts joined by `:`: `review.md` is `/review`, `git/commit.md` is `/git:commit`. Front matter may give `description` and `argument-hint`; the text may use `$ARGUMENTS` (everything typed after the word, trimmed) and `$1`–`$9` (its words; a double-quoted run is one word, with `\"` and `\\`); a text with neither gets the arguments as a new paragraph, as a library prompt does. A file whose word is a built-in (`compact`, `state`, `reset`, `plan`) or starts with `prompt:`, `template:`, `skill:`, `mcp:` or `resource:` is skipped, so a command never shadows another kind's word; a word found in an earlier folder hides the same word in a later one. The catalog (`GET /api/library`) gains `commands` (with `command`, `argument_hint`, `takes_arguments`), which only the `/` menu reads; the library panel is unchanged. `POST /api/library/command/<id>/expand` with `{"arguments": "..."}` returns `{"command","text"}`: the server fills the placeholders, so both clients share one implementation (an unknown field or a non-string is 400, an unknown id 404, an empty result 422). `jad run` and `jad eval` read `/<word> [arguments]`; a `/word` with a command's shape that the library has no command for is a prompt, sent as written, as `/etc/hosts` already was, so a prompt that happens to start with such a word is unaffected unless a command by that name exists. The web client lists commands after the built-ins; one that takes arguments opens a one-field dialog (the hint as placeholder), and the filled text is inserted as a badge named `/<word>` past the paste-as-token threshold.
- **A broken command folder costs only the commands (impact trace A7, 2026-11-05).** `.agent/commands` became a default folder, a path other tools may already use. A command folder that is a file, or a read error below it, used to fail `GET /api/library` whole, prompts, templates and skills included. `Library.List` now lists the other kinds, sends `commands: []` with `commands_error` (the reason; absent when there is no problem), and the handler logs it at warn. The web client already reads `commands` as optional and ignores the new field, so the `/` menu shows no custom commands and the library panel is unchanged. Reading or expanding one command still answers the error. Tests: `TestCommandFolderThatIsAFileIsReportedWithoutFailingTheList`, `TestDefaultCommandFolderThatIsAFileKeepsTheOtherKinds`, `TestCatalogWithoutCommandProblemHasNoCommandsError`.
- **MCP resources for the person.** `GET /api/sessions/<ref>/mcp-resources` lists the resources and the resource templates of every enabled server whose toolset entry has `resources: true` (the key the model-facing resource tools already use), asked at the same time on the profile's shared manager: a resource has `uri`, a template `uri_template` and the `variables` of its RFC 6570 template (a template that does not parse is left out); each server's list (both together) stops at its `max_tools`; a server that cannot be reached, does not offer resources or lists past `max_tools` is in `failures`, and a server without `resources/templates/list` (method not found) lists no templates. `POST .../mcp-resources/read` with `{"server","uri"}` or `{"server","uri_template","variables"}` returns `{"resource":{"server","uri","mime_type","text"}}`: the text contents joined by blank lines and a note for each binary one. A resource with no text is refused (502), as is one over `max_result_bytes`; a template filled with a variable it does not have, or given both or neither of `uri` and `uri_template`, is 400; a server outside the toolsets is 404. Nothing is written to the session. Both routes are in the request log's route list. `github.com/yosida95/uritemplate/v3`, already in the module graph through the MCP SDK, is now a direct dependency.
- **Where.** `jad run` and `jad eval` read `/resource:<server>:<resource> [variable=value ...] [text]`: the resource by its exact name, its URI, or the slug of its name (a slug two resources share is refused, naming the exact-name way); a template's variables are `name=value` words like an MCP prompt's arguments, and at least one is needed; a URI with `://` that the server does not list is read as given. The web client lists them after the MCP prompts as `/resource:<server>:<slug>` (numbered when a slug repeats), loaded with the library and the prompts and refreshed on the same `slash_menu_refresh_ms`; the three loads are independent. A template opens a dialog with a field per variable (an empty one is left undefined); the text is inserted as a badge named `<server>:<resource>`. `slash_menu_mcp_resources_enabled` (default true) turns the entries off.
- **MCP read tools in plan mode.** The four tools a toolset entry's `resources: true` and `prompts: true` add (`resources/list`, `resources/read`, `prompts/list`, `prompts/get`) are now `ReadOnly`: by the protocol they change nothing. A plan-mode run keeps them (a browser check found a plan-mode request with no tools at all before), and their calls may run beside other calls of a response; each still waits its turn on the server's one connection. The checkpoint judges MCP calls by source (`include_mcp_tools`), not by this flag, so it is unchanged. A server's own tools stay unmarked whatever their `readOnlyHint` says, since that hint is the server's claim. `tools/mcp/features_test.go` checks that plan mode keeps exactly those four.
- **Not done.** No resource subscriptions or change notifications; binary resources (images included) are not attached, only named. (Both came with "Model on profile_message and subagent, ...", subscriptions for stdio servers only.) Plugin commands and command front matter came with the next entry. The web client's menu cannot take arguments typed after the word in the draft: they go through the dialog.
- **Tests.** `contentlibrary/commands_test.go`: ten words (nested, reserved, prefixed), the slug cases, nine expansions (no placeholder, `$ARGUMENTS`, positional, quoted, missing, `$10`), two folders in order with a hidden duplicate, skipped reserved and empty files, the command fields in JSON only when set, a folder that is a file, and the schema's `command_dirs` default against the loader. `app/library_integration_test.go`: list and expand through the service, an empty `arguments`, five refusals, and the asset route for a command. `headless/commands_test.go`: custom-command parsing (eight new or changed inputs), expansion through the routes, an unknown word sent as written (also after `/plan`), a refused expansion; resource commands by name, slug, URI, an unlisted URI and a template with quoted variables, and six refusals. `tools/mcp/user_resources_test.go` on real SDK servers: only enabled servers with `resources: true`, resources before templates with their variables, a failure beside the others, reading a resource and a filled template, three not-found, five invalid, a binary resource, an unknown one, a server without resources, `max_result_bytes`, and `max_tools`. `httpapi/mcp_resource_handlers_test.go`: both routes, seven refused bodies, a 502 passed through, 501 without a provider; the route-label test has the three new routes. `cmd/jad/slash_test.go`: `jad run` with a custom command (`$1`, `$2`, a quoted word), an unknown word, a resource by slug with text after it, a template with a variable, and three failures, against the real service and a real MCP server. Web client: `library-commands-api.test.mjs`, `mcp-resources-api.test.mjs` and `slash-commands.test.mjs` (order, auxiliary data, match terms, independent loads).

## Plugin commands, command run options, plan handoff, run time limits (Go and web client, 2026-11-04)

- **Plugins ship commands.** A plugin folder may hold `commands/`: Markdown files, in subfolders or not (any other file is refused before anything is written). `jad plugin install` copies the folder whole to `<first of libraries.command_dirs>/<plugin name>` (new `contentlibrary.CommandDirectories`, beside `SkillDirectories`), so its commands are `/<plugin>:<path>` (`commands/git/commit.md` of plugin `acme` is `/acme:git:commit`) and cannot collide with the person's own top-level commands. The folder is one record entry of kind `commands` (hash over its files, like a skill folder): upgraded, refused when edited by hand unless `--force`, removed with the plugin, never written over a folder of the person's. After it is placed, `contentlibrary.CheckCommands` reads it the way the `/` menu does; a file over `libraries.max_asset_bytes`, one with no text, one whose word another kind owns (a plugin named `mcp`, `prompt`, `skill`, `template` or `resource`) and one with invalid run options fail the install, which is undone. With no command folder configured, a plugin with commands is refused before anything is written.
- **Command front matter: `model`, `profile`, `plan`, `allowed-tools`.** `model` is a model reference (`<category>/<name>[@effort]`, no white space), `profile` a profile the run route takes, `plan` `true` or `false`, `allowed-tools` tools.allow entries as a flow list (`[read, grep]`), a comma-separated line, or a block list of `- entry` lines (repeats dropped; none at all is refused). The shape is checked when the library is read; whether the session has the model, profile or tool is the run's to say. A command with a bad value is still listed, with `invalid` naming the problem, and its expand answers 422 with that text, so the person sees why. The catalog and the expand answer carry `model`, `profile`, `plan` and `allowed_tools` when set. **`jad run` and `jad eval`** apply them to the turn: model and profile only when the request has none (`--model`, `--profile`, a suite's or case's own win), `plan: true` turns plan mode on, `allowed-tools` narrows the tools unless `--tools` is given. **The web client** turns its Plan switch on for `plan: true` and keeps `model` and `tools` for the view's next message, shown as a removable token (`/review: model … · tools …`) in the composer's top row; `profile` is not applied there, because a tab runs in its own profile. This adds a per-message `model` to the web client's run request, which before carried none: the drawer's pick is still the profile's `model_default` (`app-run-model-wiring.test.mjs` now checks that the only model in the request is the submit option).
- **`tools` on the run route.** A run body may carry `tools`, an array of distinct tools.allow entries (`read`, `mcp:<server>:<tool>`, `mcp:<server>:*`; checked like `plan_mode.allow_tools`, 400 otherwise). The run's catalog is narrowed to the tools they cover (`tools.Catalog.Only`), after plan mode when both apply. An entry the profile does not have narrows nothing more: the list is "at most these". `[]` runs with no tools. Hosted tools from the model file stay, as in plan mode, and a tool `load_tool` would load is out unless listed. `jad run --tools read,grep` sets it from the command line.
- **Plan-to-work handoff: `execute_plan`.** A run body with `"execute_plan": true` carries out the plan the transcript ends with: the user message is the session's `plan_mode.execute_prompt` (new key, default `Carry out the plan above, step by step. Check the result as the plan says.`, at most 64 KiB), followed by the body's `input`, when it has text, on a new paragraph. `input` may be left out or empty; it must be a string (an array of parts is 400), and `execute_plan` with `plan: true` is 400. The message is stored in `messages.jsonl` as an ordinary user message, so the transcript format is unchanged; the substitution happens in the bound runner before the `prompt_submit` hooks, which see the final text. `jad run --execute-plan [text]` sends it (the prompt is optional; standard input is read only for `-`, and the text is not read for commands); `--plan` with it is a usage error. The web client shows an **Execute plan** button beside the Plan switch while plan mode is on and nothing runs: it sends the draft's text with `execute_plan`, switches plan mode off for the view and keeps attachments in the draft. Its label and the local echo text are `plan_mode_execute_label` and `plan_mode_execute_message` in `frontend_new/src/config.json`; the stored message is the server's. Nothing checks that the transcript really ends with a plan.
- **`max_run_duration_ms` (setting, in `defaults`, a session or a profile).** Absent means no limit (the default); otherwise 1000 to 604800000 (a week). It counts from when the run holds the session's run lock, so a run queued behind another has not started. When it is reached the run's context is cancelled with `session.RunDurationLimitError` as its cause (`context.WithTimeoutCause` in `boundRunner.Run`, which now wraps the old body as `runLocked`): the provider call, the tool calls in flight and the sub-agents of the run stop, and the run fails (not "aborted") with `The run was stopped: it ran for the max_run_duration_ms limit of 1m0s.`, which the client may read. A run that completed just as the time ran out keeps its result. It applies to every run of the session (web client, API, `jad run`, `jad eval`, triggers); `jad run --timeout` stays the caller's own limit.
- **`provider_stream.header_timeout_ms`.** 0 (off, the default) or 1000 to 3600000: `ResponseHeaderTimeout` on the provider transport. `providerHTTPClient` clones `http.DefaultTransport` (or uses the `provider_network` transport) when it is set, so the OAuth and `http_debug` wrappers and the anthropic_messages and chat_completions translators' own calls all go through it. A request that hits it failed to open, which `isRetryableError` already counts as a transport failure, so `provider_retry` sends it again; the idle timeout still bounds what comes after the headers. The runner identity already includes `provider_stream`, so a change builds a new client.
- **Not done.** `jad eval` cases take no `tools` or `execute_plan` of their own (a custom command's options reach a case through its prompt). The web client does not apply a command's `profile`, and a command's model is not shown in the pane header's model line. No check that a narrowed tool list leaves the model any tool. `execute_plan` does not look for a plan in the transcript. The header timeout is not applied to MCP servers or `fetch`.
- **Tests.** `plugins/plugins_test.go`: an install places `commands/` under the plugin's name and the words are `/acme:review` and `/acme:git:commit`; an edited command blocks an upgrade unless forced, a forced upgrade that drops the folder removes it, remove takes it back; a bad `plan`, an empty command and a reserved plugin name fail and are undone; a commands folder of the person's is not written over; no command folder refuses the install; two new `readBundle` refusals. `cmd/jad/plugin_test.go`: the command reaches the default `.agent/commands/acme`. `contentlibrary/commands_test.go`: the three list forms, repeats, every option in JSON only when set, five invalid values with their messages, `CheckCommands` counts and refusals. `app/library_integration_test.go`: expand returns the options, a command with a bad value is listed and its expand is 422 with the reason. `headless/commands_test.go`: a command's options fill the run body and the profile path, the request's own win; `ExecutePlan` sends `{"input":"","execute_plan":true}`, does not read commands, and refuses plan mode. `httpapi/run_plan_test.go`: `tools` and `execute_plan` bodies, nine refusals. `tools/plan_mode_test.go`: `Only`. `sessionfs`: `execute_prompt`, `header_timeout_ms` and `max_run_duration_ms` values, refusals and their schema limits. `cmd/jad/plan_test.go` against the real service and a stand-in provider: `--tools` narrows the request's tools, `--execute-plan` sends the configured prompt alone and then with text, both stored in `messages.jsonl`, and two usage errors. `cmd/jad/run_limits_test.go`: a provider that sends headers and then nothing is stopped by `max_run_duration_ms: 1000` after about a second with the limit's message; a provider that holds the first request without headers is retried after `header_timeout_ms: 1000` and the run completes on the second request. Web client: `library-commands-api.test.mjs` (options in the catalog and the expand answer, six malformed values), `api-stream.test.mjs` (`tools`, `model` and `execute_plan` in the body only when set), `composer-plan-mode.test.mjs` (config keys, Execute plan wiring, the options token). A browser check against a backend, a stand-in provider and a stand-in MCP server: picking `/review` (front matter `plan: true`, `model`, `allowed-tools: read, grep`) put the filled text in the draft, switched plan mode on and showed the token; the request ended with the plan-mode message and had no tools (this profile has only MCP tools, none of them listed); Execute plan with `skip the docs` sent all four tools and the user message `<execute_prompt>\n\nskip the docs`, which is the stored transcript message, and switched plan mode off.

## Model on profile_message and subagent, checked output, binary and watched MCP resources, efforts, fork button, profile ETag (Go and web client, 2026-11-04)

- **`model` on `profile_message` and `subagent`.** Both tools take an optional `model`: a model ID, optionally `@<effort>`. Absent keeps what ran before: the target profile's `model_default` for `profile_message`, the agent's own model (else the calling session's) for `subagent`. `profile_message` resolves the target with that model (`ProfileMessageRequest.Model` → `httpapi.ProfileRunRequest.Model` → the run route's model resolution), so the model must be one the *target* profile lists; a model it refuses is `model_unavailable` (invalid input, `native.ProfileModelError`), and only when the target resolves without the model, so a broken target still reports its own error. The model applies to that message only; the target's `model_default` is not written. `subagent` takes a model the *calling* profile lists (`NativeSubagentSettings.ProfileModels`, filled from `SessionConfig.ModelPaths` by the layout loader) and checks the effort against the model file (`sessionfs.CheckModelEffort`); the agent definition the run starts with, and so its fingerprint, carries the chosen model. The tool description lists those models. A remote target (`remote:...`) refuses `model`: the remote runs its own. Sample definitions `sample/tools/native/{profile_message,subagent}.json` match the built-in schemas exactly, as the loader requires.
- **Definition file (upgrade).** Both input schemas gained `model`, so a copy of the old `profile_message.json` or `subagent.json` under a local `.jad` fails the session load ("inputSchema must exactly match") until it is replaced with this version's sample file, in both orders (old binary, new file). Since 2026-11-05 that refusal names the sample file to copy (`sample/tools/<source>/<name>.json`). The `model` property is always offered when the tool is on; there is no switch to hide it, and the model can pick any model the profile lists (for `subagent`, the calling profile's models), which may cost more than the agent's own.
- **Checked output (server side) and `jad eval` `output_schema`.** The answer check moved to `internal/outputcheck` (`Check`, `JSONText`, `DescribeIssues`), shared by `jad run`, `jad eval` and the run route. A run sent with `output_format` gets an `output` member on its `completed` event: `{"valid":true,"value":<compact JSON>}` or `{"valid":false,"error":"the final answer ..."}`; a run without one gets none. `headless` uses the server's verdict and checks itself only when the server sent none. An eval case may give `output_schema` (a JSON Schema object) and `output_strict` (default true, only with `output_schema`); the run sends it as `output_format`, an `invalid_output` run fails the attempt with the verdict's error as the reason, and a case with only `output_schema` counts as one that can fail. Schema: `eval-suite.schema.json`.
- **Binary MCP resources are attached.** `POST .../mcp-resources/read` returns `attachments`: each PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP content as `{"type":"image",...}` and each PDF as `{"type":"file","name":...}`, base64 in `data`, the shape a run input part takes. Other binary types keep the `[resource ... not inserted]` note. A resource with only attachments is no longer refused; text and attachment bytes together must fit `max_result_bytes`. `jad run` and `jad eval` send them as image and file parts of the message (`headless.Request.Attachments`); the web client turns them into `File`s and adds them to the draft's image and file attachments, under the same limits and notices as a dropped file.
- **MCP resource subscriptions and change notifications.** The profile's MCP manager records `notifications/resources/updated` and `notifications/resources/list_changed` per server, the newest `resource_events_max` (new MCP server file key, default 100, 1 to 10000, in `mcp-server.schema.json`), and the subscriptions it made, which it makes again on a new connection (a refused one is dropped). A server with `resources: true` that announces `resources.subscribe` gives the model `resources/subscribe` and `resources/unsubscribe` tools. At the start of the profile's next run, the changes no run was told about yet are sent as one note in the model's prompt role after the history (`RunOptions.Notes`, never stored), then marked told. `POST .../mcp-resources/subscribe` and `.../unsubscribe` (`{"server","uri"}`) and `GET .../mcp-resources/events?after=<seq>` (`{events, latest, subscriptions}`; reading does not mark them told) serve API clients (optional `httpapi.SessionMCPResourceWatch`; 501 without it).
- **Limit found: subscriptions are stdio only.** A `streamable_http` connection never opens the standalone SSE stream that server-initiated messages travel on under the 2025 protocol: the SDK opens it from `sessionUpdated`, an unexported method of its own connection type, and `structuredContentConnection` (which keeps tool results' exact `structuredContent`) embeds the `Connection` interface and so hides it. The same hook stores the initialize result the streamable connection uses later. A plain SDK client against the same test server received both notifications; jad's did not. So subscriptions and their tools are offered for stdio servers only, and a `streamable_http` subscribe is refused with that reason. Fixing it needs the capture moved out of the connection wrapper; not done here.
- **Efforts in the catalog models list.** `GET /api/catalog/models` gives each model file's `efforts` (left out when none, or when the list is not all strings); the catalog editor's model list shows them in an Efforts column. The composer's effort picker and `model_options.efforts` already existed (2026-10-16), so the older "Not done yet: the frontend picker, listing efforts in the models endpoint" is settled.
- **Fork button.** Each user message of the live transcript has a Fork button beside Copy (`fork_message_label`). It forks the pane's profile into a new session named `<session id><fork_session_suffix><base-36 time>` in the same root (cut to the 128-character id limit) with `message_id` and `keep_turn: false`, so the new session holds the turns before that message; it opens the new session once the session list holds it (activating it earlier is undone: a view the list does not know is pruned back, which a browser check showed) and puts the message's text in its draft (`pendingComposerTextRef`, applied when that view's composer registers). Hidden while the pane runs or selects a front-compaction point. The handler is kept per view, so the memoised rows do not re-render.
- **Profile ETag on the model-default route.** The session list record and snapshot carry `profile_etag`, the strong ETag of the profile file (the catalog routes' `CatalogETag`), and the list version stamps the profile file. `POST .../model-default` takes an optional `If-Match` with it: a profile that changed since is 412 and nothing is written; the response carries the new tag in `profile_etag` and `ETag`. No `If-Match` behaves as before. The web client sends the tag only when the record it shows was read for the profile being written (a pinned pane can show the session's record before its own arrives), and on 409 or 412 reloads the sessions and says to pick again.
- **Not done.** Subscriptions over `streamable_http` (above). `profile_message` and `subagent` to a remote take no model. (The subscribe control, the events polling and "fork after" were added the same day; see the next entry.) The catalog editor's own routes already used `If-Match`; only `model-default` was missing it.
- **Tests.** native: `model` reaches the messenger trimmed, a model refusal is `model_unavailable`, a remote with a model is refused. app: a subagent call's model runs the child on that model with the models listed in the description and `model` in the schema; a model the profile does not list is refused before any child; `profile_message` with a model runs the target on it, without one on its default, an unlisted one is a `ProfileModelError` that reaches no provider; model-default with the listed tag, a stale tag (412, file unchanged) and none; the catalog models list's efforts. outputcheck via headless (unchanged tests) and httpapi: a fenced matching answer, a mismatch, not JSON, and no verdict without `output_format`. evalrun: the schema reaches the request with strict on and off, a mismatch fails the attempt, three refusals at load. mcp: an image resource is an attachment, `attachmentFor` over eight types and names; over a stdio fixture, the subscribe tool, an updated and a list-changed notification recorded, listed after a seq, told once, unsubscribe; a `streamable_http` server and one without subscriptions get no tools and are refused; per-server retention; config refusals and the schema's key list. session: notes go after the history and before the plan prompt and are not stored. httpapi: the watch routes, five refusals, 501 without the port. cmd/jad: `/resource:docs:logo describe it` sends the text and an `input_image` part. Web client: `catalog-api`, `catalog-list-view`, `session-fork`, `fork-api`, `model-default-etag`, `mcp-resources-api`, `resource-attachments`. Browser (own backend, stand-in provider and MCP server): the fork opens the new session with the forked message in the draft; an image resource is attached and sent as `input_image`; a model pick after the profile file changed is refused with the reload notice and the file is untouched, the next pick writes `openai/deep@low`; the catalog's model list shows `low, high`.

## Watched resources panel, fork after a turn; `Runner.Run` split (Go and web client, 2026-11-04)

- **Watched resources panel.** A composer toolbar toggle (broadcast icon) opens a pane panel, beside the run inspector and the prompt library (`ConversationPanelId` gains `resources`). It lists the profile's MCP resources that have a URI (templates have none to watch), each with Watch / Stop watching (`POST .../mcp-resources/subscribe|unsubscribe`), and the change notifications, newest first. While it is open it asks `GET .../mcp-resources/events?after=<latest>` every `mcp_resource_events_poll_ms` (web client config, default 5000) and keeps at most `mcp_resource_events_shown_max` (default 50) events; a `latest` below the newest event held means the server's log started again, and the list is replaced (`resource-watch.ts`). A refused subscription (a server without subscriptions, an HTTP server) shows the server's reason. The model is told about changes at the start of its next run whether the panel is open or not, as before.
- **Fork after a turn.** Each answer has a Fork after this turn button (`fork_after_message_label`): it forks with `keep_turn: true`, so the new session holds the conversation through the end of that answer's turn, and opens it with an empty draft. The client names the turn by the user message that started it (`forkPointMessage`): its id is the transcript offset, unique, where an answer's id is the provider's response id. The server now refuses a `message_id` found in more than one turn with 409 instead of forking at the first: a browser check against a stand-in provider that reused one response id forked at the wrong turn.
- **`Runner.Run` split.** `Run` keeps the run's setup (input, turn ID, checkpoint, history handle, cost meter) and loops over `runRound`, which sends one provider round and runs its calls; what one round hands the next is in `runRounds`. Behaviour is unchanged.
- **Batch failure test without a sleep.** `TestRunToolBatchFailureStartsNoFurtherCallsAndLetsInFlightCallsSettle` held call 1 for 40 ms so that call 0's failure was recorded first. Call 1 now settles only after call 0 gave back its leaf token (a budget of two), which happens after the failure is recorded and before its slot is freed; removing the failure check makes the test fail every time.
- **Tests.** app: an answer id found in two turns is refused with 409 and leaves no session file. Web client: `resource-watch.test.mjs` (the events answer and refusals, merge order, cap and restart, the watch and events calls), `session-fork.test.mjs` (`forkPointMessage`), `fork-api.test.mjs` (`keep_turn: true`). A browser check against a backend, a stand-in provider with unique response ids and a stand-in stdio MCP server that reports a change every 3 s: Fork after this turn on the first of two answers opened a session holding that one turn with an empty draft; the panel listed two resources, Watch made it Stop watching and changes arrived, Stop watching stopped them, and the next run's request ended with the four changes in a system note that `messages.jsonl` does not hold.

## Read-only sandbox for plan mode (Go, 2026-11-04)

- **What.** Plan mode keeps the tools marked `ReadOnly` and those a `plan` allow entry names; shell tools are not read-only, so they are dropped unless an allow entry keeps them, or unless one is wrongly marked read-only. Every tool a plan-mode catalog keeps now marks its calls with `ExecutionContext.PlanMode`. When the shell sandbox is on (`sandbox.enabled`, backend `bwrap`), the children of `bash`, `exec`, `process` and `tool_smith` in such a call start in the sandbox's plan-mode form: the same sandbox, with `sandbox.plan_writable_paths` (new key, default `["{tmp}"]`, same rules as `writable_paths`, in `setting.schema.json`) as its only writable paths. The project and its git directory are read-only, and a write there fails with "Read-only file system". The temp folder stays writable for exec and tool_smith's own files. The sandbox description the model reads names the plan-mode paths when they differ.
- **Limits.** Without the sandbox, plan mode is what it was: the in-process tool filter and the `MayChangeFiles` guard. A `command` backend is the person's own program and is not changed in plan mode. An exec or tool_smith standby started in plan mode is a different process from a normal one (the standby key includes the sandbox), so neither is reused for the other. Hooks are not plan-mode calls.
- **Tests.** tools: a kept tool's calls carry `PlanMode`, the full catalog's do not. sessionfs: the default, a configured list, two refusals, `ForPlanMode` for bwrap, command and none. native: with bwrap, a `bash` kept by an allow entry in plan mode cannot write the project but writes `$TMPDIR`, and the same call outside plan mode writes the project (the test fails when plan mode uses the normal sandbox); `callSandbox`, the standby key and the description.

## Notifier templates and the slack format (Go, 2026-11-04)

- **`format: "slack"`.** A `server.triggers.notify` entry with format `slack` posts `{"text": ...}`, the shape a Slack incoming webhook takes: the one-line summary, the error and, with `answer_bytes`, the start of the answer, as format `text` has them, with `&`, `<` and `>` escaped as Slack needs. The address is usually a secret, so `url_env` fits it.
- **`template`.** A notifier may give a Go `text/template` (at most 16384 bytes) the body is rendered from in place of its format's. The format then only sets the content type: `text/plain` for `text`, `application/json` for `json` and `slack`, whose rendered body must be valid JSON. The template reads the json format's fields by their Go names (`.Trigger`, `.Status`, `.Session`, `.Error`, `.Text`, `.Answer` and the rest), with `.CostUSD` a plain number, 0 when unknown, so `printf` and `if` take it. Two functions are added: `json` (a value as JSON, a string with its quotes) and `slack` (Slack escaping). A missing field is an error, not an empty value. A template that does not parse is refused when the settings load (the server does not start). One that fails when it runs, or produces no JSON where JSON is sent, fails that delivery at once without a retry (the same record renders the same way), and the reason is in the run's `notifications`. Example for Discord: `{"content": {{json .Text}}}`.
- **Tests.** sessionfs: the schema and the loader agree on a slack notifier with a template, an empty template and a template that is not a string; the loader refuses an unparsable template and an unknown function. triggers: a slack body with its escaping; a JSON template body; a text template that formats the cost and escapes the answer; a template that renders no JSON fails with 0 attempts and sends nothing although it has retries.

## Plugins from a git URL (Go, 2026-11-04)

- **What.** `jad plugin install` takes a git URL in place of a folder: `https://`, `http://`, `ssh://`, `git://`, `file://` or git's `user@host:path`. An existing folder of the same name is still a folder. jad clones it into a temporary folder with the system `git` and installs the plugin there as from a folder (the same checks, conflicts, upgrade and undo), then removes the clone. `--ref` picks a branch or tag (a shallow clone of that ref) or a commit (7 to 64 hex digits: a full clone, then that commit checked out); the default branch otherwise. `--subdir` is the plugin's folder in the repository. `--timeout` bounds the clone (default 5m, the same kind of flag as `jad mcp login --timeout`; both defaults are root setting `cli` keys since 2026-11-05). The record's `source`, which `jad plugin list` shows, is the URL without user name or password, `#<ref>`, the folder and `@ <commit>`, so an install says exactly what it took. Upgrading is installing again, from any ref.
- **Safety.** git runs with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and no askpass program, so a private repository needs an SSH key or a credential helper and a missing credential fails at once instead of waiting for input; `protocol.ext.allow=never`; no submodules are fetched; the URL follows `--`.
