A ref starting with `-` or holding spaces, `..` or the characters git refuses in ref names is refused before git runs. `--subdir` must be a relative folder inside the clone, also after symbolic links are followed, so a link in the repository cannot point the install at a folder outside it. git's error messages are passed on with the URL's credentials replaced. Nothing of the repository runs: the install copies files and checks them, as from a folder.
- **Tests.** plugins: a `file://` repository with two tagged versions installs the default branch (2.0.0, source with the commit), `--ref v1` (1.0.0, an upgrade) and an abbreviated commit; a plugin in a subfolder; refusals for a repository top that is no plugin, `../x`, an absolute and a missing subfolder, a ref that is an option or holds `..`, an unknown branch, a folder name, a missing repository, and an https URL with a password that does not reach the error; a subfolder that is a link out of the clone; `IsGitURL` and `RedactGitURL`. cmd/jad: install with `--ref` and `--subdir` from a `file://` URL and the list line; `--ref` with a folder, a negative `--timeout` and `--ref` on remove are usage errors.

## `compaction.summary`: the model summarizes the turns a compaction drops (Go, 2026-11-05, user request: off by default)

- **Why.** A compaction kept the newest turns and dropped the rest; the state context re-adds tasks, memory, processes and sub-agents but nothing of what was said, decided or learned. On a long task the model lost it.
- **What.** With `compaction.summary.enabled` true, a compaction (automatic, overflow or manual, and the profile and `profile_message` compactions that go through the manual one) first sends the turns it is about to drop to the session's model and stores the answer as one message (`role`, default `developer`; text `header` + blank line + summary) in front of the retained turns. The dropped turns are those the turn rule does not keep; the tool pairs `assistant_final` strips from a kept turn are not part of them. They go as plain text (`session.RenderItemsForSummary`): one block per message, call and result, reasoning left out, each block cut to `max_item_bytes`, the whole to `max_input_bytes` keeping the newest blocks. A message that starts with `header` is an earlier summary: it is passed on first, so summaries roll forward. The request is the model's `request` without `tools`, `tool_choice` and `parallel_tool_calls`, with `prompt` in place of the session's prompt and `max_output_tokens` replaced when that key is above 0; it goes through the run's model client (fallback, retry, pricing; inside a run the run's cost meter and `max_run_cost_usd` count it), bounded by `timeout_ms`. Nothing to drop sends nothing.
- **Where it is stored.** In the compaction cache's `items`, the same place the retained turns live after a compaction; `messages.jsonl` is replaced by the active tail exactly as before, so its records and their shapes are unchanged. A front compaction, reset or new compaction treats the summary as a history item like any other.
- **Locking.** `TurnStore.CompactHistory` takes `CompactionOptions.Summarize`. It plans the compaction under the store lock, releases the lock while the model writes (a reader of the history is not held up for the request), then plans again under the lock and refuses with "the transcript changed while the compaction summary was written" when the dropped turns differ (by a digest of their bytes). Both callers hold the session's run lock, so that refusal is a guard, not an expected path. The `pre_compact` hook fires before the summary request, so a blocking hook costs no request.
- **Failure.** `on_failure: "compact"` (default) logs `compaction summary failed` and compacts without a summary; `"skip"` leaves the transcript as it is (`transcript.ErrCompactionSkipped`). A cancelled run returns its cancellation.
- **Not covered.** Sub-agent compaction (`compactionFor`) does not summarize. The summary's usage is not added to the session's usage summary (it is not a response of the transcript). The model is always the session's; there is no separate summary model.
- **Tests.** session: the automatic compaction stores the summary first and drops the old turn, the summary request carries the prompt and the dropped text but not the session prompt, the tool choice or the kept turn; failure with each `on_failure`; no request with nothing to drop; a turn appended while the summary is written is refused and a skipped compaction changes nothing; rendering keeps the earlier summary and the newest blocks within the budget; cancellation. sessionfs: off by default, every value, 19 refusals, schema defaults and bounds equal the loader's, unknown keys refused in both. app (HTTP, fake provider): a manual compaction with the summary on sends one request and stores the summary in the cache, not in `messages.jsonl`; with it off nothing is sent.

## `compaction.overflow`: compact and send the round again when the context window is full (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **What.** When a provider round fails and the provider's error contains one of `compaction.overflow.patterns` (case-insensitive; defaults cover OpenAI's `context_length_exceeded`, Anthropic's "prompt is too long" and common OpenAI-compatible wordings), the runner compacts with the automatic compaction settings (and the summary, when on) and sends the same round again, at most `max_attempts` (default 1) times per run. The retried response is staged as `<turn>-<round>-overflow-<n>`. A compaction that drops nothing, a cancelled run, an error that matches no pattern, or attempts used up return the provider's error as before. The state context is refreshed after the compaction, as after an automatic one.
- **Default.** Off (`enabled` false; changed 2026-11-05 after the impact trace, it was on), and it also needs `compaction.auto_enabled`. New features are off by default, and this one rewrites messages.jsonl.
- **What is matched.** For a stream that ended with `response.failed` or an `error` event, only the provider's error is matched: the `type`, `code` and `message` of `response.error`, else of a top-level `error` (or that string), else of the event itself (`session.ResponseFailedError.Detail`). The echoed `response` (instructions, tools) is not: an unrelated server error whose prompt says "context window" used to count as an overflow. Any other error (an HTTP status with the provider's body) is matched by its text. The error's text is unchanged.
- **The run's turn is kept whole.** The compaction sets `transcript.CompactionOptions.KeepLatestTurn`: the newest turn (the latest user message and everything after it) is kept with all its calls and results, even with `keep_turns` 0, and `keep_tool_pairs` counts only in the kept turns before it. Before, the turn rule stripped the run's own calls and results, so the retry restarted from the user message with the run's tool work gone. When only that turn is left, nothing is compacted and the provider's error stands. A run split into several turns by steering messages keeps only the newest of them whole.
- **Hook and log.** The compaction goes through the run's `CompactHistory`, so `pre_compact` fires with trigger `automatic` and a blocking hook leaves the round failed. Each attempt is logged at info (`context overflow compaction`, attempt, whether it compacted, the provider error).
- **Not covered.** Sub-agent runs (their own compaction settings) do not recover. Only the error text is matched: a provider whose overflow error names none of the patterns needs its wording added.
- **Tests.** session: an overflow compacts, refreshes the state context and the retry succeeds without the dropped turn; another error, automatic compaction off, nothing to drop and attempts used up each return the provider's error with the expected requests and callbacks; matching ignores case and blank patterns; a mid-loop overflow keeps the run's calls and results in the retry and the history; with only the run's turn left nothing is rewritten; a failed stream matches only its error code and message (echoed instructions and tools do not). transcript: `KeepLatestTurn` keeps the newest turn whole and counts `keep_tool_pairs` before it. sessionfs: defaults (off), values, refusals and the schema with the summary's tests.

## Steering a running run: `POST /api/sessions/<ref>/run/steer` (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **What.** While a run of the session works, `POST .../run/steer` with `{"input": ...}` (the shapes `POST /run` takes) queues a user message for it and answers 202 `{"steer":{"turn_id","pending"}}`. The run stores queued messages as user input marked as steering (`AppendSteerInputItemsAt`, since 2026-11-05; see the C6 entry below; images to the image store) at the start of each provider round, after the previous round's tool results, so the model sees them without the run stopping. When the model ends its turn while a message waits, the run takes one more round instead of returning. Each stored message is published on the run stream as a `steer` event (`{turn_id, items}`).
- **Refusals.** No running run (none, or only finished ones): 404. Steering off (`server.steer_max_pending` 0): 409. The queue at `server.steer_max_pending` (default 16, 0 to 1000): 409. A run that has finished taking messages: 409, because the queue closes atomically when the run decides to end with nothing waiting (`closeIfEmpty`), so no message is accepted after the run decided to end. A body with another key or no input: 400. The input goes through `parseRunInput`, the check `POST /run` uses (a non-empty string, or text/image/file parts within the `server.max_request_*` limits; the body limit is the run route's), then `NormalizeInput`/`ValidateInputItem`: anything else is 400 (corrected 2026-11-05 after the impact trace: the first version took any Responses item, a `role: system` message included, and no image or file limit applied).
- **messages.jsonl.** A steered run has one more user message record per steering message, of the existing input shape plus `"__agent_session_input_steer":true` (since 2026-11-05); a run without steering is unchanged. The web UI projection shows it as a user message; the frontend does not yet send steering messages or show the `steer` event live.
- **Limits.** A run that fails or is stopped drops messages still waiting (they were never stored). They are not lost silently: `RunOptions.OnSteerDropped` gets them, and the executor publishes a `steer_dropped` event (`{turn_id, inputs}`) and logs a warning. The executor also closes the queue when the run returns, so a run that failed before the session runner took its queue (a blocked prompt, a model that did not load) reports what it accepted and refuses what comes after (it used to answer 202 and keep it). A continuation round counts toward `max_tool_rounds` like any round. A queued second run (the run queue) is not affected: steering goes to the running one.
- **Tests.** session: a message pushed during round 1 is stored after the first answer and gets a second round, `OnSteer` sees it, and a push after the run is refused; queue limits, bad input, close and a nil queue. httpapi: 404 without a run, six bad bodies, 202 with the pending count, 409 over the limit, 409 when turned off; a system message, too many images, an unknown part field and a blank string are 400, a text and image message is 202, a run that fails before taking its queue reports it as `steer_dropped`, and a finished run is 404. session: a message of a failed round reaches `OnSteerDropped`. sessionfs: `server.steer_max_pending` default, values, refusals and the root schema.

## `tools.extension.web_search`: a search API as an extension tool (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** Web search was only a hosted tool of OpenAI models; the Anthropic and Chat Completions translators refuse hosted tools, so those models had `fetch` but no way to find a URL.
- **What.** `web_search` takes `query`, optional `max_results` (capped by `max_results`), `allowed_domains` and `blocked_domains`, calls the configured provider and returns a numbered list of titles, URLs and snippets (highlight tags removed, repeated URLs dropped, snippets cut to `max_snippet_chars`). Providers: `brave` (GET, `X-Subscription-Token`, at most 20 per request), `tavily` (POST JSON with `include_domains`/`exclude_domains`, Bearer key), `searxng` (GET `format=json`, no key unless `api_key_env` is set). `provider` is required; `base_url` and `api_key_env` default per provider (searxng needs `base_url`). The key is read from the environment on each call, like `decide_systemone`, and never logged or shown.
- **Domains.** The setting's `allowed_domains` bound every call: a call may narrow them, not widen them (a call naming none of them is refused). Blocked domains add up. Results are filtered by host (a domain matches itself and its subdomains, `www.` ignored) after the provider answers; brave and searxng also get `site:` operators for the allowed domains.
- **Errors** are tool results, never Go errors: `api_key_missing`, `invalid_input`, `search_failed` (HTTP status and the start of the body), `search_timeout`, `response_too_large` (`max_response_bytes`).
- **Shape.** Read-only and counted by the leaf budget, like `fetch`; needs `extension.web_search` in `tool_definition_paths` (`sample/tools/extension/web_search.json`). The name is the one models know; do not enable it next to a model.json hosted `web_search` tool (not checked).
- **An extension tool, not a native one** (moved the same day, before any release): like `decide_systemone` it calls a third-party API with a key of its own, so it lives under `tools.extension` (setting key `tools.extension.web_search`, tool id `extension.web_search`, package `internal/tools/extension`). `tools.native.web_search` is refused as an unknown native tool. Profile `tools.allow`/`deny`, sub-agent tool lists and tool_smith still name it `web_search`.
- **Tests.** extension: brave request (key, user agent, count capped, query collapsed) and filtered, de-duplicated, cut output; tavily body with narrowed allowed and normalized blocked domains; searxng without a key and with `site:`; seven error results; a call cannot widen the allowed domains. native: the catalog refuses the setting without the manifest and builds the sample manifest. sessionfs: defaults per provider, 17 refusals, schema keys, defaults and bounds equal the loader's.

## `tools.native.apply_patch`: several files in one call (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** `edit` replaces one exact text per call; changing several places or files took many calls, and GPT models write the `*** Begin Patch` format natively. `edit`'s schema is unchanged, so session manifests keep loading.
- **What.** `apply_patch` takes `patch`: `*** Begin Patch`, then `*** Add File: <path>` (every line `+`), `*** Delete File: <path>`, `*** Update File: <path>` with an optional `*** Move to: <path>` and hunks (`@@ [line to find first]`, then ` `/`-`/`+` lines, optional `*** End of File`), then `*** End Patch`. Hunks apply in file order, each searched after the previous one, lines matched exactly, then ignoring trailing whitespace, then ignoring surrounding whitespace (the format's own rules). A unified-diff range (`@@ -1,3 +1,4 @@`) is no anchor. The file's line ending (CRLF kept) and final newline are kept; an added file ends with a newline.
- **All checked first.** Every operation is parsed, its paths resolved through the same path policy, sub-agent scope and symlink checks as `write`, and its new bytes computed before any file is written; a patch that does not apply anywhere changes nothing (`invalid_patch`, `no_match` with the hunk and patch line, `already_exists`, `not_found`, `duplicate_path`, `size_limit`, `too_many_files`). The paths are locked in sorted order, so two patches cannot deadlock. Writing then goes file by file; a write failure stops there and the result lists what was already applied.
- **Backups and reports.** Updated and deleted files are backed up like `write`'s (a delete is a backup and a remove); each change is reported to `OnFileChange` (created, edited, deleted), so post_edit checks run on every changed file (post_edit now checks each file a call changed, once, not only the last) and the sub-agent change list names them. It counts as a workspace change for checkpoints, as a change tool for sub-agents and as an editor for sub-agent `paths`.
- **Settings.** `max_bytes` (each file, default 1 MiB), `max_files` (default 50), `max_patch_bytes` (default 1 MiB) and the `edit_style` keys. Needs `tools/native/apply_patch.json` in `tool_definition_paths`.
- **Tests.** native: one patch adds, updates (anchor and blank-context hunks), moves and deletes, with the change reports and a backup; one failing file changes nothing; CRLF and loose whitespace; End of File; nine refusals leave the file alone; post_edit runs once per changed file; the sample manifest equals the code. sessionfs: defaults and overrides, refusals, schema bounds and defaults.

## `budget`: caps per session and per day (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** `max_run_tokens` and `max_run_cost_usd` stop one run; nothing capped a session that scheduled triggers or a chatty user run again and again.
- **What.** `budget.max_session_tokens`, `max_session_cost_usd`, `max_daily_tokens` and `max_daily_cost_usd` (day in `time_zone`, default UTC; `Local` refused). All absent by default: the budget is off and nothing is counted or written. With a cap set, each response's reported tokens and the priced cost the run's meter counted since the last response (a compaction summary written for the run included) are added to `<state dir>/budget.json` (`session` totals and one entry per day, `keep_days` days kept). The process holds one ledger per file, so every profile of a session counts into the same one under one lock; the file is read again when another process changed it.
- **Refusal.** A run is refused before prompt_submit runs and before its input is stored when a cap is already reached, and checked between rounds like `max_run_tokens`: asked-for calls get a `budget_limit` result and the run fails with a message naming the count and the limit (`BudgetLimitError`, readable by clients). A cost cap without the model's pricing refuses the run, as `max_run_cost_usd` does.
- **Rounds and order (2026-11-05, after the impact trace).** Every round a run adds is checked, not only tool rounds: a round a stop hook asks for, or one for a waiting steering message, is not started once `max_run_tokens`, `max_run_cost_usd` or the budget is reached (`session.runLimit`): the stop hook is not run and the run ends as it would without one (since the impact-trace fixes of a1b2402^..0253bf2; before, it ran and its message was not stored), and with a steering message waiting the run fails with the limit and the message is reported dropped. A run whose caller stored the input (`PersistedInput`, the app's bound runner, which checks the budget first) is not checked again before its first round, so a cap another profile of the session reached in between no longer leaves the stored message unanswered.
- **Not covered.** Sub-agent tokens are not counted (their cost is, see the run cost entry). There is no server-wide cap across sessions. Resetting means deleting `budget.json`.
- **Tests.** session: daily cap rolls over at Berlin midnight, a second ledger over the file sees the counts, the session cost cap, pruning; error texts; a run stops between rounds with a `budget_limit` result and the next run is refused without storing input or calling the provider. sessionfs: off by default, values, refusals, schema. app (HTTP): a run past the cap refuses the next one, no ledger without a budget, a cost cap without pricing is refused.

## Hooks that change things: rewrite, stop, session_start, notification, shell changes (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **pre_tool `rewrite`.** A rewrite command that exits 0 and prints `{"arguments":{...}}` makes the call run with those arguments (and their raw JSON), checked against the tool's schema first. An output that is not such an object, was cut at `max_output_bytes`, or fails the schema blocks the call (`blocked_by_hook`) with the reason; empty output keeps the arguments. Later pre_tool and the post_tool commands read the new arguments. A JSON-answer command's long lines are not cut at `max_line_chars`.
- **prompt_submit `rewrite`.** `{"prompt":"..."}` replaces the input's text before it is stored: the first user message's input_text parts become one part, later user messages lose theirs, other parts stay; the item is written with `type`, `role`, `content` first. The last rewrite wins (each reads the original). A bad output refuses the run like a blocking command.
- **stop.** When a run would end without an error (no call, no steering message waiting, not after a declined draft in that same round: since 2026-11-05 a decline no longer carries into the round a steering message adds after it, so the stop commands run when that round ends; past `max_run_tokens`, `max_run_cost_usd` or the budget the stop commands are not run and the run ends as it would without them), the stop commands run with the final answer and the count so far; the first one that exits 0 printing `{"continue":"<message>"}` makes the run store `[stop hook <name>]` + the message (role `stop_message_role`, default user) and go on, at most `stop_max_continues` (default 3) times per run. Any other outcome lets the run end. These messages are ordinary input records in `messages.jsonl`, only when a stop hook asks.
- **session_start.** Before the first run of a conversation (the next session turn is 1: a new session or after a reset), before prompt_submit; context entries add their output after the input like prompt_submit's (role `prompt_context_role`).
- **notification.** When a tool of the run asks the user a question (`RequestInteraction`), the notification commands run beside it (a goroutine on a context the run's end does not cancel) with the tool, call id and title; reports go to the log.
- **shell_changes `scan`.** Off by default. Around each bash and exec call the workspace's regular files are listed (size and mtime, no symlinks followed, `shell_scan_skip_dirs` not entered, at most `shell_scan_max_files`) and the created, updated and deleted files are reported to `OnFileChange`, at most `shell_max_changed_files`, so post_edit checks and the sub-agent change list see them. Misses a same-size change within the clock resolution and a background exec program still running.
- **Tests.** native: rewrite with a long line reaches the call and the later command; four unusable rewrites block; empty or failing rewrites keep the arguments; prompt rewrite, empty prompt refused, stop continue with payload, a failing stop does not continue; the scan reports created/updated/deleted, skips a skipped dir, runs post_edit on changed files and reports nothing past the limit; off by default. session: two continues then the limit; no message ends the run. sessionfs: every new key and 12 refusals; schema keys equal the loader's. app (HTTP): session_start context and the prompt rewrite reach the first request, a stop hook adds one round, the second run gets no session_start; a notification command reads the question.

## MCP client: roots, sampling, elicitation, server logs, list changes (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **What.** Per server file: `roots` (`workspace` gives the session's working directory as a `file://` root; `none` offers no roots capability — before, the SDK advertised roots with an empty list; `workspace` was the default until the impact trace made it `none`, see "Off switches" below); `server_log` (off by default; a level asks the server for its log messages — per call in `_meta` on protocol 2026-07-28, `logging/setLevel` on older ones — and writes them to the jad log as `mcp server log`, cut to `server_log_max_bytes`; at debug progress notifications too); `sampling` (off by default; the session's model answers `sampling/createMessage` with the server's system prompt and text messages, no tools, output capped by `max_output_tokens` and the server's `maxTokens`, at most `max_requests` per connection, through the run's model client — `session.CompleteText`); `elicitation` (off by default; a form-mode question during one of the server's tool calls is asked of that call's user like a request_draft — draft `{server, message, content}` with the schema's defaults; accept or update accepts with the draft's content, decline declines, a timeout, a stop or no user cancels; URL mode is declined). Tool and prompt list changes are recorded with the resource events and told to the model at its next turn when the server file sets `changes_note` (see "Off switches" below); the tool list itself is listed again at every run, as before.
- **Which call asks.** A connection runs one call at a time (`callMu`), so the call that holds it is the one whose user an elicitation reaches; the call is carried in the context and set on the connection only while the lock is held.
- **Protocol 2026-07-28.** Servers on it ask for input with multi round-trip requests; the SDK's client middleware calls the handlers and retries the call. A handler that refuses (sampling over budget, a capability not offered) fails that call in the SDK, which the model sees as the tool's error.
- **Not covered.** No progress tokens are sent with calls, and the tool list is not refreshed within a run. Sampling and logging are deprecated by the protocol (SEP-2577) but still served.
- **Subscribe waits for the acknowledgement (2026-11-05, after the impact trace).** Under protocol 2026-07-28 the SDK's `Subscribe` (v1.8.0) only sends a `subscriptions/listen` request and returns; the server handles requests concurrently, so a call made right after could change the resource before the server held the subscription, and the `resources/updated` reached nobody (the cause of `TestResourceSubscriptionsRecordChangesAndTellTheNextRunOnce` failing about once in 60 runs). `subscribeAcknowledged` now waits, within the request timeout, for the server's `notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged` listing the URI (seen by a receiving middleware); one that does not come withdraws the listen and refuses the subscription. A URI already subscribed is not sent again (the SDK would send nothing and no acknowledgement would come). The legacy `resources/subscribe` request is unchanged. Tests: the subscription test passed 300 runs in a row (it failed within 300 with the wait off), a second subscribe returns at once, and the waiter wakes only the acknowledged URIs of that server.
- **Tests.** mcp (stdio fixture asking for roots, sampling and elicitation): roots name the workspace, sampling reaches the sampler with the system prompt and the capped tokens, an edited draft is accepted with its content and the question carries the schema default, timeout and title; the server's warning reaches the log; the tool list change makes a note; a declined question declines; a sampling request over the budget fails the call; no call is set between calls; defaults (roots on, sampling and elicitation not offered, `roots: none` empty); nine config refusals; schema keys and defaults. session: `CompleteText` request shape and refusals.

## Sandbox: allowed hosts, a macOS backend, MCP servers (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **`network_allow`.** Backend bwrap with network false: the sandbox still has no network. jad runs one filtering proxy per allow list on a Unix socket (a temp folder, for the life of the process), binds the socket into the sandbox and starts the child through its own binary (`jad __sandbox-net-forward`), which listens on loopback `proxy_port` (default 3128) and forwards each connection to the socket, with HTTP_PROXY, HTTPS_PROXY and ALL_PROXY (both cases) set and NO_PROXY unset. The proxy lets CONNECT tunnels and absolute `http://` requests through to the allowed hosts and their subdomains and answers 403 to anything else, so a program that ignores the proxy variables reaches nothing. The forwarder passes signals on and returns the child's status (128 + signal). Refused with network true and with other backends. Not run under a real bwrap here (none installed): the proxy, the forwarder and the bwrap arguments are tested separately.
- **`seatbelt` backend.** macOS only: jad writes a sandbox-exec profile from the same settings — writes only to the writable paths (resolved through symlinks, since macOS names /tmp through /private) and the terminal and null devices, the hidden paths neither readable nor writable, no network but local Unix sockets unless network is true. Plan mode swaps in `plan_writable_paths` as for bwrap. Written to the profile language's rules but not run on macOS.
- **`mcp` in `sandbox.tools`.** The session's stdio MCP servers start through the sandbox from their own cwd (`native.SandboxCommand` handed to the manager as a command wrapper); a changed sandbox reconnects them. Not in the default: servers often need the network or files outside the project.
- **Not covered.** glob and grep (ripgrep) and jad's own git checkpoints still run outside the sandbox; they read or are jad's own code, and hide_paths do not apply to them.
- **Tests.** native: the proxy passes an allowed host over HTTP and a CONNECT tunnel and refuses others (403, failed tunnel), one proxy per allow list; subdomain matching; the forwarder keeps the child's name and status and refuses a missing socket; bwrap arguments drop `--share-net`, bind the socket, set the variables before `--` and start the forwarder; the seatbelt profile from writable, hidden and network settings with resolved paths, and a launch through a stand-in sandbox-exec. mcp: a stdio server starts through the wrapper once and again after the wrapper changes. sessionfs: values, eight refusals, seatbelt off macOS, seatbelt plan mode.

## `project_instructions`: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md found by name (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** Each instruction file had to be listed in `prompt_documents`; a project's `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` and the ones of its subfolders were not found.
- **What.** Off by default. With `enabled`, the folders from cwd up to `stop_at` (`git_root`: the nearest folder holding `.git`, or cwd alone when there is none; `cwd`; `filesystem_root`) are searched when the session loads. Each folder gives at most one file, the first of `file_names` (default `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) it has, so a CLAUDE.md that repeats AGENTS.md is not read twice. The files go into the prompt after `prompt_documents`, outermost first (the nearer folder last, so it reads as the more specific), each under `# Project instructions: <absolute path>`. A file already listed in `prompt_documents` is skipped. `max_bytes` (64 KiB) bounds them together: the file that crosses it is cut at a whole character with a note, and the ones after it are not read. `SessionConfig.ProjectInstructionFiles` lists what was read.
- **Subfolders.** With `subfolders`, a read, write, edit, apply_patch (each patch file and move target) or notebook call that names a path in a folder below the workspace gets the instruction files of the folders between the workspace root and that path added to its result, outermost first, each once per run (the tracker belongs to the catalog, which is built for each run). Each file is cut at `subfolder_max_bytes` (16 KiB). The workspace root's own file is never added there: it is in the prompt.
- **messages.jsonl.** The upward files are prompt text, which is not stored in messages.jsonl. The subfolder files are part of a tool result and are stored with it; nothing changes while the setting is off.
- **Limits.** The files are read when the session loads, like `prompt_documents`: an edit shows from the next load. A file that compaction drops from an old result comes back only in the next run. glob, grep, bash and exec do not trigger the subfolder files.
- **Tests.** sessionfs: default off, every key, nine refusals, schema keys, defaults and bounds; the walk to the git root picks one file per folder, outermost first, skips a prompt document, `cwd` and `filesystem_root`; with no git root only cwd, and the cut keeps whole characters. native: a read below the workspace gets both subfolder files in order, once per run, nothing for the root, outside paths, a folder named like the file or a non-file tool; a new catalog gives them again; apply_patch names its files; off adds nothing. app: the file reaches the provider request and not messages.jsonl.

## `server.metrics`: OpenTelemetry metrics over OTLP/HTTP JSON (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** server.tracing exported spans, but there were no metrics to chart or alert on (run counts, latency, tokens, tool failures).
- **What.** Off by default. With `enabled`, the server, `jad run` and `jad eval` record at the points where the run, model request and tool call spans start and end (the call sites did not change): `jad.runs` (by `jad.run.status` and `jad.run.source`, `web` when no source is named), `jad.run.duration` (s), `jad.runs.active`, `gen_ai.client.operation.duration` (s, by `gen_ai.request.model` and `error.type` for a failed request) and `gen_ai.client.token.usage` (by model and `gen_ai.token.type` input/output), `jad.tool.calls` (by `gen_ai.tool.name` and `jad.tool.status`: the status a native tool result's first line states, `error` for a Go error, `ok` for any other output) and `jad.tool.duration`. Cumulative temporality; pushed to `<endpoint>/v1/metrics` every `export_interval_ms` (60 s) and once more at shutdown, each push bounded by `export_timeout_ms`. Endpoint and headers follow the OTLP variables like tracing (`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_METRICS_ENDPOINT`, `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`, `..._HEADERS`). Histogram bounds are `duration_buckets_s` and `token_buckets`, by default the GenAI conventions' advice.
- **No metric SDK.** `go.opentelemetry.io/otel/sdk/metric` is not a dependency; like the span exporter, a small recorder in `internal/tracing` keeps every series in memory under one mutex and writes the OTLP/JSON shapes itself. The span the Start functions return is wrapped while metrics are on (the trace span stays in the context), so tracing and metrics work on and off independently; with both off the Start/End functions still allocate nothing.
- **Cardinality and privacy.** Attributes are model names, tool names, statuses and the run source only: no session, profile or turn ids, prompts, answers, arguments or output. MCP and tool_smith tool names are attribute values, so the series grow with the number of distinct tools.
- **Limits.** No Prometheus pull endpoint; no exemplars; values are lost if the process dies without its shutdown. Sub-agent runs are not runs here; their model requests and tool calls are counted.
- **Tests.** tracing: runs, the active gauge, model durations with an error type, token histograms and their bucket, tool statuses from the result header, a Go error and plain output; nothing private in the export; Setup pushes OTLP/JSON with the headers to /v1/metrics and on shutdown, and restores; disabled installs nothing; the endpoint rules. sessionfs: defaults, every key, twelve refusals, schema keys, defaults and bounds.

## Fork into a profile of the same session; Duplicate (Go and web client, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** The transcript's fork buttons made a new session per fork, so a session's branches were scattered over the session list. A fork now stays in its session as another profile, which the session's own rows group.
- **API.** `POST /api/sessions/<ref>/fork?profile=<category/name>` takes `profile` (a new profile name) instead of `session`, with an optional `title`. The source profile's file is copied to `<category>/<profile>` in the same category (`title`, when given, replaces its `name`; the other keys keep their order), the session's `scope.profiles` gets the new ID appended when the scope is a list that does not select it yet (a session without a scope, or one that selects the category, is not written), and the transcript up to the fork point (same `message_id`/`cursor`/`keep_turn` rules) is copied to the new profile's state in the same session. The answer names the session as the client wrote it and the new profile. A failure deletes the new profile file and puts the session file back. Refusals: both `session` and `profile`, a name outside the catalog identifier rule or equal to the source, `title` without `profile` (400); an existing profile file (412); a run of the source profile (409); a profile outside the catalog layout (400).
- **`state`.** `"copy"` (default) or `"none"`: `none` copies no transcript and no history block, for a duplicate of the settings only, and takes no fork point. Works for both targets.
- **Web client.** Fork from here / Fork after this turn open a dialog (`ForkDialog`) that proposes `<name><fork_session_suffix><stamp>` in the source profile's category, checks the name as the server does, and takes an optional display name; the new profile's view opens, with the message's text in its draft for a fork before a message. The row menu of a session or profile row has Duplicate: the same dialog names a new session (in the source's root) or a new profile of the same session, with Copy the conversation (on by default; off sends `state: "none"`). A session duplicate copies the row's profile's conversation, as the session fork always did. The catalog editor has Duplicate for an open file (profiles, models, toolsets, MCP servers, sessions): it starts a new file with the open text and the ID `<id><catalog_duplicate_suffix>` (`-copy`), which the user changes before Create.
- **Limits.** A new profile ID that sorts before the session's others becomes its default profile when the session names none (the default is the first in scope by ID). The forked profile shares the source's tool storage settings (memory, tasks), as two profiles of one session always do; checkpoints start empty. The catalog Duplicate copies one file; the toolset or model a profile names is not duplicated with it.
- **Tests.** app: a fork into a profile through the real service — the new transcript is a shorter prefix, the source is untouched, the profile file carries the title, a session without a scope is not written, a scope list gets the new ID, `state: none` writes an empty transcript, eight refusals leave no profile file; the order-keeping key edit. Web client: the fork body for a profile, a title, `state`; the proposed name and the name checks; the dialog, the row menu's Duplicate and the editor's Duplicate are wired.

## Fork memory and the hardcoded fork and CLI limits (Go and web client, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** The impact trace of 754992d..8884e05 found the fork read the whole transcript into memory (C14) and four limits fixed in code (C15, project rule 3).
- **Fork streams (C14).** `forkPoint` now finds only where the copy ends and how many turns it holds; `writeFork` streams the lines from the shared store into the temporary file (`writeStreamAtomic`, buffered) and collects image names on the way, and the history block is copied the same way (`copyHistoryBlock`, line by line, bytes unchanged). A fork point named by `message_id` is searched one turn at a time, normalizing each turn as the snapshot pages and the message route do. Both run under the gate and session lock the fork already held, so no compaction or reset changes the offsets in between. No size limit was added: memory follows the largest turn, not the transcript. Measured by `TestForkHoldsATurnInMemoryNotTheTranscript` (peak live heap, collector at 5%, 32 turns of 512 KiB, 16.8 MB transcript): before, 32.6 to 34.5 MB for a whole fork and 35.3 to 36.8 MB for a fork by message id; after, 0.9 to 3.2 MB and 3.7 to 4.8 MB. The test bounds both at half the transcript. It does not measure the snapshot or other routes, and one very large turn is still held whole.
- **`server.fork_name_max_bytes` (C15).** Was `maxForkTitleBytes = 200` in `httpapi`. Now a server setting, default 200 (unchanged), 1 to 250: the name becomes `<name>.json`, which must fit the 255 bytes most file systems allow. It bounds the new profile's name and `title`; the refusals name the setting. `httpapi.Config.ForkNameMaxBytes` 0 means the default, so embedders and tests that do not set it keep 200.
- **Web client.** `MAX_PROFILE_NAME` in `session-fork.ts` reads `fork_profile_name_max` (200) from `config.json`; the schema says to keep it equal to the server's. `MAX_SESSION_ID` (128) stays a constant: it is the session identifier format (`remote.SessionPattern`), not a limit the server can be configured with.
- **Root setting `cli` (C15).** A new top-level section for defaults of jad subcommands: `plugin_install_timeout_ms` (default 300000, 0 for no limit, at most a day) bounds the git clone of `jad plugin install <git URL>`; `mcp_login_timeout_ms` (default 600000, 1 to a day) is how long `jad mcp login` waits. A `--timeout` flag on the command line still wins (`flagGiven` tells a set flag from its zero default). Unknown keys in `cli` are refused, as in the other sections. Without a root setting the defaults apply.
- **Tests.** app: `TestForkHoldsATurnInMemoryNotTheTranscript` (fails before: 32.6 MB and 36.8 MB against an 8.4 MB bound), `TestCopyHistoryBlockStreamsTheBlockAndItsImages`, and the four fork tests unchanged. httpapi: `TestForkNameLimitIsTheConfiguredOne`, `TestHandlerTakesTheForkNameLimitFromItsConfig`. sessionfs: `TestServerForkNameMaxBytesDefaultValuesAndRefusals`, `TestCLISettingsDefaultsValuesAndRefusals` (with the schema), `TestLoadReadsTheCLISection`. cmd/jad: `TestPluginInstallTimeoutComesFromTheRootSetting` (a 1 ms setting stops the clone; `--timeout 1m` installs; it fails with the old fixed 5m default). web: `session-fork.test.mjs` reads the limit from `config.json`.

## Off switches for what 754992d..8884e05 changed at default settings (Go and web client, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** The impact trace found four changes that reached every user with no setting to undo them (A4, A5, C9, A8), against project rule 3 and the rule that new features are off by default. Each now has a setting. A4, A5 and C9 default to the behaviour before the change; the fork buttons and Duplicate (A8) were asked for by the user and stay on, with a flag to hide them.
- **A4: `model_choice` on `tools.native.subagent` and `tools.native.profile_message`** (boolean, default false). Off, the tool's schema has no `model` property (byte for byte the schema before the property existed, `TestModelChoiceOffKeepsTheToolSchemasTheyHadBefore`), the subagent description lists no models, and the loader leaves `ProfileModels` nil; a call that still names a model is refused by the schema (`additionalProperties: false`). On, everything is as 8884e05 made it. The schema line is cut from the one schema literal (`modelChoiceSchema`, which panics if the line is not there exactly once) rather than kept as a second copy. Not covered: the memory tool's description, enum and query changes and its always-available `delete`, which change the prompt once and offer no new spend; they stay.
- **A5: MCP server file `roots` defaults to `none`; new `changes_note`** (boolean, default false). `roots: none` offers no roots capability, so a server is not given the workspace path unless its file says `workspace`. `changes_note` decides whether the server's list-changed and resource-updated notifications go into the developer note of the profile's next run; they are recorded either way for `GET .../mcp-resources/events`, and every notification is marked told whether or not it was told, so turning the key on later does not replay old ones. `changes_note` is left out of the server's connection fingerprint when false (`omitempty`), so a file without the key keeps its fingerprint; the roots default change does alter it, which only means a new connection after a restart.
- **C9: the anthropic adapter's `structured_output` defaults to `unsupported`**, which is what the adapter did before `native` existed (a json_schema `text.format` goes through `unsupported_fields`). A profile for a model with structured outputs sets `native`. `sample/anthropic-adapter.json` already set `unsupported`. The User-Agent (`jad-anthropic-adapter`) and the missing X-Stainless-* headers got no switch of their own: `upstream.extra_headers` already sets both (they are not reserved), and the schema now says so.
- **A8: web client flags** in `frontend_new/src/config.json`: `fork_messages_enabled` (true) hides the transcript's fork buttons when false, `duplicate_enabled` (true) the row menu's and the catalog editor's Duplicate, and `resource_watch_enabled` (false) the watched-resources toggle and panel. Plan mode and the MCP prompts and resources in the / menu already had flags (`plan_mode_enabled`, `slash_menu_mcp_prompts_enabled`, `slash_menu_mcp_resources_enabled`); they are unchanged.
- **Tests.** native: the off schemas equal the old literals, a profile_message call naming a model with the switch off never reaches the messenger, the model list needs the switch. sessionfs: `model_choice` defaults, is read per tool, refuses a non-boolean, and the loader fills `ProfileModels` only when on (fails without the gate). app: with the default a subagent call naming a model starts no child and the parent sees no model property or list; the two model tests turn the switch on. mcp: `TestTakeResourceChangesTellsOnlyServersWithChangesNote` (fails without the filter), defaults (`roots 0`, `workspace` gives the root), refusal of a non-boolean `changes_note`, schema keys and defaults. anthropicadapter: a profile without `structured_output` sends no `output_config` and reports `text` ignored. web client: `ui-feature-flags.test.mjs` (flag defaults and the gates in App, ViewPane and CatalogEditorDialog; static source checks, not a rendered page).

## Fallback clients per runner, and a run's steering messages in its compaction turn (Go, 2026-11-05)

- **Why.** The impact trace of 754992d..8884e05 found two run-loop effects: `prepareRun` built the provider client, and with `model_fallback` loaded every fallback's file and key, on every run, logging a left-out fallback each time (A9); and steering messages are stored as user messages, which compaction takes as the start of a new turn, so one run became several turns (C6).
- **A9.** `runStreamer` is split into `planRunStreamer` (loads the fallback model files and keys and gives the runner's identity, logs nothing) and `buildRunStreamer` (builds the clients and logs the fallbacks left out). `prepareRun` plans on every run, because a changed fallback file or key must still give a new runner, and builds only when it creates a runner, as before 8884e05. A cached runner costs no client, and a fallback that cannot be used is logged once per runner instead of once per run. The other callers (compaction summary, sub-agents, MCP sampling) still build every time through `runStreamer`, as they did. The model files are still read on every run, as the session's own model file is.
- **C6, marked in messages.jsonl (format change asked for by the user).** A steering message's records carry one more reserved key after the batch keys, `"__agent_session_input_steer":true` (`transcript.SessionInputSteerField`, written by `AppendSteerInputItemsAt`). Every other record is byte for byte as before (the `messages-input-batch.jsonl` golden is unchanged). The key is stripped with the batch keys whenever a record becomes a history item (`expandTranscriptRecord`), so it is never sent to a provider or a summarizer, and `ValidateInputItem` refuses an input item that carries it, so a client cannot mark its own message.
  - **Compaction.** `planCompaction` starts a turn at a user message unless it is marked: a marked message joins the turn before it, so a compaction (automatic, overflow, manual or a later run's) keeps or drops a steered run whole. With `keep_turns` 1, or an overflow compaction that keeps the latest turn, the run's first message and its tool work stay with the steering message instead of going as an older turn.
  - **Compaction cache.** A compaction moves the kept history out of messages.jsonl into `compaction-cache.json`, whose items carry no reserved keys. The cache gets `continued_items` (indexes of the kept user messages that continue their turn: in each kept turn, every user message but the one that started it; omitted when none), and the next compaction reads the flags from there. A cache without the key, or an index out of range, splits as before. The cache's `schema_version` stays 1: an older build ignores the key.
  - **Not changed.** The assistant-final items are still chosen per user message, so the answer the model gave before a steering message stays kept in `assistant_final` mode. The session turn counter, the turn starts, the web UI history, the session catalog, front compaction and the `transcript_scan` tool read only the batch keys and ignore the new one: a steering message is still shown and counted as a user message of its own. The in-memory count from the first version of this fix (`ContinuedUserMessages`) is gone.
  - **Rollback.** A build before this change does not strip the key: it would send `__agent_session_input_steer` inside the message to the provider, which would likely refuse the request, for every session holding a steered record in messages.jsonl (one already compacted holds it only as `continued_items`, which is safe). Going back needs those records' key removed first: `jad transcript strip-steer` does it (see "Follow-ups of the stop hook traces").
- **Tests.** app: `TestModelFallbackLeftOutIsReportedOncePerRunner` (three runs, one warning; fails before with three). session: `TestOverflowKeepsTheRunsWorkBeforeASteeringMessage` (the retry after an overflow carries the run's request, its call and output and the steering message, not the older turn; fails when steering is stored unmarked, when the retry held only the steering message). transcript: `TestSteerInputIsMarkedInTheTranscriptOnly` (one record marked, the history has no key, a client item with the key is refused), `TestCompactHistoryKeepsASteeringMessageInTheRunsTurn`, `TestCompactHistoryKeepsTheSteerMarkAcrossCompactions` (`continued_items` names the steering message, and a second compaction keeps the run whole; fails without `continued_items`), `TestCompactHistorySplitsAtEveryUnmarkedUserMessage`.

## Fixes from the impact trace of a1b2402^..0253bf2 (Go and web client, 2026-11-05)

- **Webhook replay (B1, B2, B4, R9).** A correctly signed webhook that started no run (the trigger was busy, 409 with `run`; or the server is stopping, 503) no longer uses up its signature: the record `firstDelivery` made is dropped (`forgetDelivery`), so the sender's retry runs. A record now lasts until `replay_window_ms` after the later of its acceptance and its timestamp, so a sender whose clock runs ahead cannot replay a captured request in the last seconds its timestamp is valid: "can never run twice" now holds for any clock skew the window accepts. The timestamp's age is compared to the window in milliseconds (a value beyond the window in whole seconds is refused first, so nothing overflows), and `timestamp_header` needs `replay_window_ms` of at least 1000, the timestamp's resolution (999 was accepted and refused requests from an exact clock). New key `server.triggers.replay_max_records` (default 10000, 1 to 1000000): the most records held at once. With the record full, a new correctly signed request is refused with 503 until records expire, rather than dropping a live record, which would let its request run again. Records are pruned in expiry order (`container/heap`), so a request no longer scans every record; the heap is rebuilt when entries of forgotten records outnumber the records. Before this change the record had no cap.
- **MCP subscribe race (B3).** The "already subscribed" check and the record of a subscription are made inside the request, under the connection's call lock. Two concurrent subscribes to one URI no longer both send: under the listen protocol the SDK makes the second a local no-op, whose acknowledgement never came, and its timeout cleanup withdrew the first call's listen.
- **Resubscribe (R6).** A new connection makes its subscriptions again in one request: every subscribe is sent, then the acknowledgements are awaited together. A server that never acknowledges costs one `timeout_ms` instead of one per subscription (measured in the test: 4 subscriptions, `timeout_ms` 1000, 1.08 s now, 4.02 s before). Each subscription it does not acknowledge is withdrawn and dropped, as before.
- **Review of the fixes (9d27b8f).** `resubscribe` reads its list of subscriptions and drops the failed ones inside its request, under the call lock `subscribe` also takes. This is a safeguard, not a fix of a reachable race (corrected by the impact trace of 93e3b65): `getConnection` holds the server's lock through the connect and the resubscribe, so no subscribe reaches the new connection before the resubscribe ends, and the old connection is closed, after its requests, before the new one starts. Only a test that calls `resubscribe` on a connection already in use shows the difference. A resubscribe whose request cannot be made (the connection closed already, by disabling the server or by Close) keeps the list for the next connection instead of dropping it. A replay record lasts one millisecond more than `replay_window_ms`: the timestamp's age is compared in whole milliseconds, so a timestamp is accepted until just under a millisecond past the window, and a captured request replayed in that millisecond found no record and ran again (202). Tests: `TestResubscribeDoesNotUndoAnUnsubscribeMadeMeanwhile`, `TestWebhookReplayInTheWindowsLastMillisecondIsRefused`, and `TestStopHookRoundIsNotStartedOverTheTokenCap` now wants the run to end without an error; each fails without its fix.
- **Skipped stop hooks are reported (impact trace of 93e3b65; decided by the user).** When the stop commands are not run because the run reached `max_run_tokens`, `max_run_cost_usd` or the budget, the runner calls `RunOptions.OnStopSkipped` with the cap's error; the app writes a warning to the log and sends a `hook_report` with `event` `stop`, `trigger` `cap_reached` (`session.StopSkippedTrigger`) and `report` `[stop hooks] not run: <the cap's message>` (a hook name cannot hold a space, so no command's report looks like it). The web client now accepts `stop` reports (before, it dropped every `stop` report, including those of commands that ran) and shows the skip as an error entry, "Hook skipped: stop (cap reached)", so a stop hook used as a check does not pass unseen. The run still ends as it would without stop hooks. The cap is checked only when the stop commands would be asked, so a run without them no longer reads the budget ledger at its end, and a ledger that cannot be read then fails the run instead of skipping the commands silently. Nothing is stored; `messages.jsonl` is unchanged. Tests: `TestSkippedStopHooksAreReported` (app), `TestStopHookWithAnUnreadableBudgetFailsTheRun` and the `OnStopSkipped` check in `TestStopHookRoundIsNotStartedOverTheTokenCap` (session), and the stop case in `hook-report-view.test.mjs`.
- **Skipped stop hooks outside the web client (impact trace of 2eaeef4).** `jad run` keeps the skip report as `stop_hooks_skipped` in its result (json; the jsonl stream already had the `hook_report` event) and prints it on stderr in text format as `jad: [stop hooks] not run: ...`, whatever the status; the exit code is unchanged. A trigger run copies it into its record (`stop_hooks_skipped` in `GET /api/triggers`), and the notifications carry it: the json format through the record, text and slack as a line after the error, a template as `.StopHooksSkipped`. Other hook reports are still not in either. `subagent_stop` reports stayed log-only here: the trace of 2eaeef4 said the web client dropped them, but the server never sent them (`subagentStopHook` passed no reporter; the launcher is built per session, not per run); the follow-up below carries the parent run's reporter to the sub-agent tool. Tests: `TestRunKeepsTheReportOfSkippedStopHooks` (headless), `TestRunReportsStopHooksSkippedAtTheTokenCap` (jad run, text and json, the command not run), `TestSkippedStopHooksReachTheRecordAndTheNotifications` (triggers).
- **Skipped stop hooks: notifiers and evals (impact trace of 61f50c6).** A run whose stop hooks were skipped at a cap ends `completed`, so a notifier whose `on` lists only failures never heard of it. `on` takes a new value, `stop_hooks_skipped`, which is not a status: it reports such a run whatever its status (`TriggerNotifier.Wants(status, stopHooksSkipped)`; schema enum and description updated). The default `on` is unchanged; it already holds every status a run that ran can end with. `jad eval` does not change an attempt's outcome for it (the case's `checks` decide that, not the profile's stop hooks), but the attempt's progress line ends with the skip report, `summary.stop_hooks_skipped` counts the attempts (omitted when 0), and the stderr summary line adds `, N with stop hooks not run at a cap` when N > 0. Left as it is: in text format `jad run` shows the skip twice on stderr at the default log level, as the `hook stop` warning of the log and as the `jad:` line, as a failed run already does with its error. Tests: `TestFailuresOnlyNotifierHearsOfSkippedStopHooksWhenItAsks` (triggers), the notifier loader and schema agreement cases (sessionfs), `TestRunCountsAttemptsWhoseStopHooksWereSkipped` (evalrun); the first and the last fail without the change. Impact trace of 70cea54: the loader's errors for `on` no longer call `stop_hooks_skipped` a status (`... statuses (completed, ...) and stop_hooks_skipped`, `... is not a status or stop_hooks_skipped, or is listed twice`), and the stderr line is built by `evalSummaryLine` (`cmd/jad`), tested with and without the count (`TestEvalSummaryLineCountsSkippedStopHooksOnlyWhenThereAreSome`).
- **Old definition files (D2).** `requireModelChoiceDefinition` accepts a `subagent.json` or `profile_message.json` whose schema is the full one or the one before the `model` argument existed, with `model_choice` off or on: the tools build the schema the model sees themselves (`modelChoiceSchema`), so the file's copy is only checked. Any other schema is still refused. "Off switches" said the definition is the one it had before; that now holds for the definition file too.
- **Stop hooks (R2, R3; decided by the user).** The cap check (`runLimit`) now runs before the stop hook: with `max_run_tokens`, `max_run_cost_usd` or the budget reached, the stop commands are not run (they may have side effects), and the run ends as it would without stop hooks: it completes with its final answer, or fails with the cap when a steering message is waiting. Before, the hook ran and its message was discarded. (The first version of this fix failed every run that ended at or past a cap with stop hooks configured, even when no hook would have asked to go on; the review of commit 9d27b8f found it.) A stop hook's message is now stored as steering input (`AppendSteerInputItemsAt`), so its records carry `"__agent_session_input_steer":true` and compaction keeps it in the run's turn, with the run's earlier tool work (format change asked for by the user; records without a stop hook are unchanged). The rollback note of the steering marker applies to these records too.
- **Command folders (R7; decided by the user).** When the library could not list its commands (`commands_error`), `jad run "/<word> ..."` with a word the catalog does not list fails (exit 1) with the reason and a hint to use `--no-commands`, instead of sending the text to the model, since the word may be a command of the broken folder. The web `/` menu shows a `/commands: unavailable` entry after the built-ins; picking it shows the reason. `fetchRootLibrary` reads `commands_error` into `commandsError`.
- **Fork name limit (R8).** New route `GET /api/server` (API token, GET only, `no-store`): `{"fork_name_max_bytes": N}`. The web client reads it once at start (`fetchServerInfo`, `setForkProfileNameMax`), so the proposed fork name and the name check follow the server's `server.fork_name_max_bytes`; until it answers, or with an older server (404), `config.json`'s `fork_profile_name_max` stands.
- **Turns for backup_min_turns (R4).** `countUserTurns` no longer counts a record marked as steering, as compaction does not start a turn at it. The session's turn numbers, turn starts, the web UI turn index, fork cursors and front compaction still count a steering message as a turn of its own (unchanged; noted in `countUserTurns`).
- **Comments (D3, D4).** `handleRunSteer` says the input is a string or an array of parts (an array of Responses items is 400). `CostMeter` says sub-agent spend counts toward the caller's cost, `max_run_cost_usd` and budget, and that spend a background child makes after the caller's last round is in the caller's meter but in no budget record (unchanged).
- **Not changed** (R1, R5, D4 and the four R10 items named below were done later: see "Follow-ups of the stop hook traces"). R1 (rollback sends the marker), R5 (`KeepLatestTurn` cannot shrink an overflow inside one run), the rest of R10, D4's unrecorded background spend, and `timestamp_header` without a schema `default` (an empty string is refused, so there is no default value to give).
- **Tests.** triggers: `TestWebhookRefusedFiringKeepsTheSignature`, `TestWebhookTimestampAheadCannotBeReplayed`, `TestWebhookTimestampWindowCountsMilliseconds`, `TestReplayRecordIsBounded`; sessionfs: the 999/1000 window and `replay_max_records` cases in the loader and schema tables. mcp: `TestConcurrentSubscribesToOneResourceKeepTheSubscription` (3/3 failed before), `TestResubscribeWaitsForTheAcknowledgementsTogether`. native: `TestDefinitionFilesFromBeforeModelChoiceStillLoad`. session: `TestStopHookRoundIsNotStartedOverTheTokenCap` (the hook does not run), `TestStopHookMakesTheRunGoOnUpToItsLimit` (records marked, requests unmarked). headless: the broken-folder case of `TestCustomCommandsSendTheirFilledText`. transcript: `TestCountUserTurnsSkipsSteeringRecords`. httpapi: `TestServerInfoReportsTheForkNameLimit`. web client: `slash-commands`, `library-commands-api`, `session-fork` and `fork-api` cases. Each new Go test failed with its fix undone.

## Follow-ups of the stop hook traces (Go and web client, 2026-11-05; decided by the user)

- **`subagent_stop` reports reach the run stream.** The run's context carries its `OnHookReport` (`session.WithHookReporter`, set in `Runner.Run` beside the cost meter). The subagent tool call hands its context to the launcher as `CallerValues`, which reads the reporter (`session.HookReporterFrom`), passes it to `subagentStopHook` and puts it on the child's context, so a child's own children report to the same stream. The web client accepts `subagent_stop` reports (`HOOK_EVENTS`) and words them like the others: "Hook ran: subagent_stop", or "Hook failed: subagent_stop" for a failed, timed out, unstartable or stopped command. A background child that ends after the calling run reaches the log only: the ended run's stream has no reader. Tests: `TestSubagentStopHookReportsTheChildRun` checks the `hook_report` frame in the parent stream (fails without the reporter), `hook-report-view.test.mjs`.
- **Failing stop commands stay errors.** A stop command asks the run to go on only by passing (exit 0 with `{"continue": ...}` on stdout), so a stop report that says `failed`, `timed out`, `could not start` or `stopped before` is a broken check, and the client already showed it as "Hook failed: stop". Unchanged; a test now pins it (`hook-report-view.test.mjs`). The "what next" note of 70cea54 that said every stop report showed as "Hook ran" was wrong.
- **`jad transcript strip-steer [--dry-run] <messages.jsonl or folder>...` (R1).** Before a rollback to a build from before the steering marker, it blanks the `__agent_session_input_steer` field of every record that carries it, in place: the field (always the record's last, `,"__agent_session_input_steer":true`) is overwritten with spaces, so the file keeps its size, its inode (a server that kept it open still appends to the same file) and every record's offset (message ids, fork cursors, the offsets of the existing turn starts). It cannot be undone, and it changes the turns the current build sees: the marker is what tells it that a steering or stop hook message continues the run's turn (`countUserTurns`, `CompactHistory`), so each stripped record starts a turn of its own, and `keep_turns` compaction, the overflow compaction's kept latest turn and the counted turns may cut those runs differently if the session goes on with this build; the usage text says so and to run it only right before the rollback. Backups (`messages.backup.jsonl`) and compaction archives (`messages-<time>.jsonl`) are not searched and keep the marker: no build sends them to the provider. The transcript caches hash the bytes they read, so the next server rebuilds them, as after any change they did not make. A folder is searched for files named `messages.jsonl` (session profiles and sub-agent transcripts). Each file is changed under its transcript lock (`sessionlock.TryAcquire`); one in use by a run is skipped and reported, and the command exits 1. A marked record in another form (not last, not `true`) is an error and nothing in that file is written. The command is meant to be run with the server stopped; a run started afterwards marks new records again. `transcript.StripSteerMarks`. Tests: `TestStripSteerMarksBlanksTheMarkerInPlace` (size, other bytes, valid JSON, same history, idempotent), `TestStripSteerMarksRefusesAMarkerItDoesNotRecognize`, `TestTranscriptStripSteerWalksFoldersAndSkipsATranscriptInUse`.
- **An overflow inside the run's own turn (R5).** New key `compaction.overflow.tool_output_max_bytes` (default 8192; 0 off, else 256 to 1000000; loader, schema, config tests). When an overflow compaction finds nothing to drop (only the run's turn is left), the round is sent again with the text of each tool output longer than the cap (`function_call_output` and `custom_tool_call_output`, a string output or the text parts of a list) cut to about the cap: its start and end kept, a note between them saying how many bytes were left out and, since the model cannot read the transcript, that they cannot be read back and to run the tool again with a narrower request (it names no setting; the server log does). The transcript keeps the whole output. The cap lasts for the rest of the run, so later rounds are cut too, and each further overflow halves it, down to 256; `max_attempts` counts these attempts with the compactions. A cut that cuts nothing does not send the same request again: the provider's error stands, as it does with the key 0. Only the request changes (`cutToolOutputs`, after the image resize in `buildRoundRequest`; the memoized history items are never written), so `messages.jsonl` is untouched. The server log gets "context overflow tool outputs cut" (attempt, cap, count); the memory trace's `request-built` mark gets `tool_outputs_cut`. The default is below the native tools' usual output caps, so the first cut shrinks their outputs. Tests: `TestOverflowInsideTheRunsTurnCutsItsToolOutputs` (the retry and the next round cut, the transcript whole), `TestOverflowCutHalvesOnTheNextAttempt`, `TestOverflowCutThatCutsNothingKeepsTheProviderError`, `TestCutToolOutputsLeavesTheHistoryItemsAlone` (UTF-8 boundaries, list outputs, images kept), `TestOverflowSettingsCarryTheToolOutputCapAndLogTheCut`; the first two fail with the cut off.
- **A background child's late spend reaches the budget (D4).** The part of a run's meter already recorded in the budget is now kept on the meter (`CostMeter.recorded`, with the run's `budget`), not in the run. A run records `takeUnrecorded()` after each round as before; the sub-agent launcher calls `RecordUnrecordedSpend` on the caller's meter when a child ends, which records what the nearest meter with a budget holds beyond its last record (cost only, no tokens, as for any child). Nothing is counted twice: whichever records first takes the delta. A failed record is a warning in the log. Test: `TestBackgroundChildSpendAfterTheCallersLastRoundReachesTheBudget` (0.25 recorded by the next round, 0.5 spent after the run recorded once; fails without the flush).
- **R10 leftovers.**
  - *`steer_dropped`* leaves inline data out: an `image_url` or `file_data` data URL keeps its header (`data:image/png;base64,`) and gets `omitted_bytes` beside it (`withoutInlineData`); the event went to every reader's queue, and the sender holds its own copy. The `steer` event of a stored message is unchanged. Test: the dropped-message case of `TestRunSteerChecksInputLikeRunAndReportsDroppedMessages` (fails without it).
  - *Fork lock.* `copyForkLines` reads a snapshot (`ReadRecordsSnapshot`), so the source journal lock is not held while the fork writes each line; a transcript rewritten during the copy fails the fork with 409 instead of copying stale lines (the fork's gate keeps compaction and reset out, so this should not happen). Test: `TestForkCopyDoesNotHoldTheSourceJournalLock` (a reader of the source waited 2 s before).
  - *`/` menu retry.* A source whose load failed is tried again `slash_menu_retry_ms` (new, web `config.json`, default 2000) after the failure, not on every keystroke; a source that loads keeps `slash_menu_refresh_ms`. Test: `slash-commands.test.mjs` (fails on the old source).
  - *Overflow `Detail`.* A failed stream event that is not a JSON object keeps its whole text as `Detail` (it cannot be an echoed response), so a pattern can match it; before, it was empty and never matched. Test: two non-JSON cases in `TestIsContextOverflowReadsOnlyTheProvidersErrorOfAFailedStream`.
- **Not changed.** `messages.jsonl`: no change writes it, except `jad transcript strip-steer`, which a user runs for a rollback. The `steer` event's images, the "Fork after" optimistic id and the rollback refusal of new keys (R10's other items).

## `exec` replaces the file, search, shell, process, patch, fetch and notebook tools (Go, web client, 2026-11-05; decided by the user)

The models in use did not call `read`, `write`, `edit`, `grep`, `glob`, `bash`, `process`, `apply_patch`, `fetch` or `notebook` even when a prompt told them to: they wrote one `exec` call that chains the commands. Saved exec helpers cover the rest without a tool definition each. The ten tools are removed; `exec`, `read_image` and `lsp` stay, `task` and `memory` stay as they are (a redesign and a rename come separately).

- **Refused by name, never ignored.** `tools.native.<name>` for each of the ten answers "`tools.native.<name>` was removed; <what to do instead>" (`removedNativeTools`, `validateToolSettingsShape`). The same hint answers a profile `tools` entry, an agent `tools` entry, a `tool_smith.tools` entry and a `sandbox.tools` entry that names one. Other removed keys: an agent's `paths` (inline and `agents_dir`; it narrowed `write` and `edit` only, and was always refused next to a shell), `subagent.process_max_running`, `project_instructions.subfolders` and `subfolder_max_bytes` (they added a subfolder's file to `read`/`write`/`edit`/`apply_patch`/`notebook` results; nothing calls with a path now), `spill.allow_unreadable` (the load-time check that `read`/`grep` could read spill files back is gone; exec reads them with `cat`), the `process` state-context provider, and `server.recover_processes_at_start`.
- **Moved.** `tools.native.read.paths` is now `session_files.paths` (`SessionConfig.FilePolicy`): the UI file list and the `transcript`, `transcripts` and `profile` tools use it. Absent, only the working directory is listed, as before without `read`.
- **What stays from the removed code.** The child-process runner of `bash` (`shell_run.go`: `runShellChild`, the environment allowlist) serves `exec`, `tool_smith` and hooks; the `tools.native.bash` key is gone, the `NativeBashSettings` type stays as `exec`'s `Child`. Glob matching serves the transcript tools (`glob_match.go`). Exec's async handles are what `process` was for long commands (a call that outlasts `block_ms`); a child's handles are stopped when the child's run ends. A server that must outlive the backend has no tool now. Sub-agent children still list what they changed (`written_files`), from the `hooks.shell_changes` scan of each exec call, and the `post_edit` hooks run on those files; with the scan off (the default) a child lists no files and `post_edit` never runs.
- **Defaults that changed.** `sandbox.tools` defaults to `exec`, `tool_smith`; `tool_smith.tools` defaults to none (it was `read`, `grep`, `glob`). An agent without a `tools` list gets the read-only tools (`read_image`, `lsp`, `skill`, transcript tools, ...): an agent that reads the workspace lists `exec`. Plan mode sees only read-only tools, so a plan-mode run reads the workspace only when `plan_mode.allow_tools` lists `exec`. `exec` always counts as changing state, so several `exec` calls in one response run one after another.
- **Build.** `fetch` was the only user of Xberg's cgo HTML converter and of brotli; both left `go.mod`, and the backend builds without cgo or `go generate`. A machine-local `internal/tools/native/html-to-markdown-rs_cgo_link.go` from the old setup now breaks the build and must be deleted.
- **Samples and schema.** `sample/tools/native/{read,write,edit,apply_patch,notebook,grep,glob,bash,process,fetch}.json` are deleted; the sample toolsets, profile, agent and root setting use `exec`; `setting.schema.json` and `root-setting.schema.json` drop the keys above and add `session_files.paths`. The web client's built-in @-mention list names `exec.json` instead of `read.json`.
- **`messages.jsonl`.** Its format is unchanged. Old transcripts keep their calls to the removed tools and replay as stored; `checkpoint/run.go` still treats those names as changing calls. New text in tool results: exec's suggestions for oversized code or input, the spill hint ("read it with exec (sed -n, tail, rg) instead of re-running the command"), the empty `written_files` line, and `lsp`'s and `read_image`'s pointers.
- **Tests.** Each refusal: `TestRemovedNativeToolsAreRefusedByName`, `TestNormalizeSessionFilesPolicyDefaultsAndRules`, `TestProjectInstructionsSubfolderKeysAreRefused`, `TestSubagentPathsAndProcessMaxRunningAreRefused`, `TestNativeExecSpillAllowUnreadableIsRefused`, `TestToolListsNamingARemovedToolSayItWasRemoved`, `TestNormalizeStateContextSettingsDefaultsAndRemovedProcessProvider`, `TestToolSmithToolsDefaultToNoneAndRefuseRemovedTools`, `TestLoadServerRecoverProcessesAtStartIsRefused`, `TestLoadConfigRefusesTheRemovedRecoverProcessesSwitch`, the `removed bash`/`removed process` cases of `TestSandboxRefusals` and a `removed <tool>` case per name in the schema table. Tests that only covered the removed tools are deleted; the ones that used them as stand-ins now use `exec`, `read_image`, `lsp`, `skill` or a fake tool (the scheduler's edit-edit-edit-read ordering test, the tool_smith nested call, the hooks). `TestHooksAreWiredIntoExecWithTheChangeScan` and `TestHooksDoNotRunForExecWithoutTheChangeScan` replace the write/edit hook wiring tests. The write/edit `messages.jsonl` golden test is deleted; `testdata/native-write-edit.messages.jsonl` stays as frozen data, as do `internal/tools/native/testdata/{native-fetch-definition.json,native-fetch-output.golden.jsonl,messages-native-process-status.jsonl}`, which no test reads any more. The panic recovery after a sub-agent launcher returned (`recoverRun`) lost its seam with the process sweep; `SubagentManager.afterLaunch`, a test-only hook nil in production, replaces it for `TestSubagentPanicAfterTheLauncherFailsTheRunInsteadOfEndingTheServer`. `TestRunnerNativeExecMessagesJSONLMatchesGolden` (`session/testdata/native-exec.messages.jsonl`, run time and fence token normalized) is the `messages.jsonl` golden of an exec call and its result. A removed tool named in a list (a profile's, an agent's, `tool_smith.tools`, a sandbox's `tools`) ends its refusal with "remove it from the list", a removed key with "delete the key".
- **Not done.** Exec helpers for fetch and notebook (the `tool_smith` socket bridge in `tool_smith_bridge.go` is the starting point for helpers that call back into the backend), ~~the `task` redesign~~ (done 2026-11-08, see "Native `task` redesign" below) and the `memory` rename.

## `exec` calls that only read: `read_only` per call and per agent; sample change scan (Go, 2026-11-06; decided by the user)

The tool removal above left four losses: plan mode could not read the workspace, the `explore` sample agent counted as a writer, several `exec` calls of one response ran one after another, and changed-file tracking was off in the samples. The first three share one cause, that `exec` always counted as a change; one argument fixes them.

- **`read_only` argument.** `exec` takes `read_only` (boolean). The tool is `ReadOnly` with `MayChangeFiles` true only for a call that has `code` and no `read_only: true`; a helper listing never changes files. So a response whose calls all only read runs them at the same time under `tool_call_scheduling: "ordered"`, and plan mode keeps `exec` without an allow entry: a read-only call runs, any other call is refused with `error_code` `plan_mode` before it starts. This replaces "a plan-mode run reads the workspace only when `plan_mode.allow_tools` lists `exec`" and "several `exec` calls in one response run one after another" above. An allow entry still keeps the whole tool.
- **Enforcement.** Under a bwrap or seatbelt sandbox a read-only call starts in the sandbox of a plan-mode call (`execReadOnlyExecution` sets `PlanMode`, so `callSandbox` uses `sandbox.plan_writable_paths`, by default only the temp folder) and cannot write the project. Without a sandbox, or with a `command` backend, `read_only` is the model's promise, as the schema says; the container stays the boundary. The checkpoint still treats every `exec` call as changing (`checkpoint.CallChangesWorkspace`), and with `hooks.shell_changes: "scan"` the scan still runs on it, so a broken promise is listed and checked like any other change.
- **Agents.** `exec` is now in the read-only pool, so it joins the opt-in tools of `subagentChildTools`: an agent without a `tools` list still does not get it. A new agent key `read_only` (inline and `agent.json`) makes every exec call of the agent read-only (`withReadOnlyExec` sets it in `NormalizeArguments` and `Execute`, whatever the model sends), and `ChangeTools` leaves `exec` out for it, so the agent is a reader: no checkpoint, no change marker in the list, and it may run as an async handle. It needs `exec` in `tools` and refuses `tool_smith`, `subagent` and `profile_message` beside it; `isolation: "worktree"` with it is refused because nothing would change files. `NativeSubagentAgent.ReadOnly` is `omitempty`, so other agents' fingerprints are unchanged. The sample `explore` agent sets it.
- **Older definition files.** A `tool_definition_paths` file whose `native.exec` schema is the one from before `read_only` still loads (`requireExecDefinition`); the model is shown the current schema with the file's description. `sample/tools/native/exec.json` has the new property.
- **Changed-file tracking.** The default of `hooks.shell_changes` stays `off`: the scan walks the workspace twice per exec call, which a large tree should not pay without asking. The sample session (`sample/jad/sessions/default.json`) turns it on, so sub-agents list their `written_files` and `post_edit` runs there.
