It needs a decision: whether the owner's `tools.lazy` governs toolset tools at all (then `GateFor` must gate the given tool, and `load_tool describe` shows the owner's definition), or toolset tools are never gated. Closing it: that decision, then the change in `GateFor`/`bindMCP` with a toolset test. **Closed by T-105 (below).**
- **A `pre_tool` rewrite that turns a `load_tool` list or describe into a run is judged by the arguments before the hook.** The judgments (checkpoint, scheduling) read the model's call; the runner then runs what the hook wrote, with its own checks. The same holds for every tool whose judgment depends on its arguments (`MayChangeFiles`, such as the subagent tool), so it is a limit of rewrite hooks, not of the runner; not changed. Closing it would mean judging calls after the pre hooks, which the round cannot do before it schedules.
- **Frontend.** The type fix has no runtime test: nothing calls `fetchSessionTools`, so there is no behaviour to test; `npm run typecheck` covers it. `npm test` has one failure that this change does not touch: `composer-triggers.test.mjs` ("every built-in path ... exists in this repository") still lists `MEMORY_DESIGN.md`, which ddb70b1a (memory renamed to records) deleted. Not fixed here (unrelated); closing it means updating that built-in path list.
- The T-103 non-goals (accuracy with live models, a live second server) are unchanged.

### Verification

- `verify-go.sh --race "./internal/tools/ ./internal/session/ ./internal/app/ ./internal/sessionfs/"`: gofmt, vet, the full `./internal/... ./cmd/...` tests, `-race -count=3` of those packages and `git diff --check`. `internal/tools/native` ran apart, `go test -race -count=1 -timeout 25m ./internal/tools/native`: ok in 226 s, no race report. Under the gate's `-race -count=3` it exceeds the default 10 m test timeout of the binary (three runs of about 226 s), so the gate cannot run it; that is the size of the package, not a failure of a test.

## A toolset child keeps its own copy of an MCP tool; the owner's `tools.lazy` only gates it (Go, 2026-11-08; T-105)

Closes the open item of T-104 with its Option A: the owner's `tools.lazy` governs toolset tools too, and `GateFor` gates the tool it is given. D1 to D7 are unchanged.

### The rule

- `LazySet.GateFor(tool)` keeps its two guards (`CanBeLazy(tool.Definition)`, the ID is bound) and now wraps **the tool it is given** (`lazy.go`, `GateFor`), no longer the set's bound copy. The set supplies the decision (is this ID lazy) and, through `Entries`/`Match`, the description `load_tool` shows. `Gate` (the one-shot of the owner's catalog, `gateLazy`/`GateLazyTools`) is unchanged and still goes through `GateFor`, with the owner's own copy as the argument, so its result is the same.
- A toolset child holds `hook(toolset hooks)(gate(toolset copy))`. `bindMCP` gates first and hooks after for both pools; the session pool keeps the owner's hooks, the toolset pool takes the hooks of its own toolset. Order evidence: `WithToolEventHooks` bakes the hooks into `Execute`, and the gate's refusal is returned by the gate's `Execute`, so a refused call fires the hooks only if the hooks wrap the gate. Before this change the resolver hooked the toolset copy first, so gating it (even with the right copy) would have dropped the toolset's hooks; the resolver now passes the raw toolset tools (`native.ToolsetMCP{Tools, Hooks}`, the second argument of `SubagentMCP.BindToolsets`) and the subagent tool hooks them after the gate. No new config.
- `load_tool run` of the tool dispatches through the child's catalog to the gated toolset copy (ViaRunner), whose private inner catalog holds the unhooked real toolset tool: hooks fire once, on the outer wrapper. `load_tool` list and describe still read the owner's bound entry, so the model sees the owner's description and `inputSchema` whatever the toolset's copy says. A toolset tool whose ID the owner does not bind stays ungated and fully listed; a native tool is never lazy; a nil set gates nothing.

### Tests (each fails without its fix, checked with `mutate-check.sh`)

- `TestGateForGatesTheGivenCopyNotTheBoundOne` (`tools`): gate returns the given copy's definition and executor. Mutation `WithLazyGate(s.bound[0], s)`: caught.
- `TestToolsetChildKeepsItsOwnCopyOfALazyOwnerServer` (`native`): owner and toolset stubs with different description, schema and counters; describe shows the owner's, a direct call is refused with the toolset's hook firing once and the owner's never, a run increments the toolset's counter only and fires the toolset's hook once more. Mutations caught: the swap (`bound[0]`), hooking before the gate, as before.
- `TestToolsetChildOfALazyOwnerServerKeepsItsOwnCopyAndHooks` (`app`, refusal and run): the owner's hooks (in the session's toolset file) and the toolset's hooks write to different markers; the toolset's fire exactly `pre`, `post` once, the owner's never. Mutations caught: toolset hooks replaced by the owner's, by none, and hooking before the gate. It uses the new test-only `layouttest.Spec.ToolsetExtra` / fixture key `__session_toolset`.
- The existing child-gate tests and `TestLazyMCPProfileGivesTheChildItsLazySetBeforeBindingMCP` with its textual pin pass unchanged; the callers of `BindToolsets` in tests changed type only.

### Fingerprint on resume

`subagentFingerprint` hashes `catalog.RouteDefinitions()`, so a toolset child with a same-ID lazy server now hashes the toolset's definition instead of the owner's. If the two differ, a child started before this change and resumed after it gets the `config_note` ("The agent definition changed since the source run ...") once. It is informational: `configNoteFor` only adds a `config_note` information entry to the resume result (`subagent_changes.go`), and the resume itself runs as before. No migration; `RouteDefinitions` and the T-104 fingerprint decision are untouched. Children whose two copies are identical hash as before.

### Left open

- **The model sees the owner's definition, the toolset copy validates its own.** If a toolset's server really differs from the owner's (another version), a `load_tool run` built from the owner's `inputSchema` is checked against the toolset's schema and may fail with `validation_failed`. That is the accepted consequence of "the owner's `tools.lazy` governs and supplies the description". Closing it would need `describe` to read the child's catalog copy, which was not chosen.
- **The toolset's own profile `tools.lazy` is not consulted for its tool.** Only the owner's set decides, as decided. A toolset tool with a lazy entry only in the toolset's profile stays eager in the child.
- **The app test tells the copies apart by hooks, not by connection.** The fixture server is one in-process server both managers connect to, so the connection itself is pinned only by the unit tests' counters. Closing it would need a fixture server that records which manager connected.
- **Toolset hooks and the owner's differ only in one layout.** A toolset that sets `hooks` while the settings also set them is a load error, so the toolset's hooks differ from the owner's only when the owner's hooks come from its own toolset file (the test's layout). Not a gap in the change, noted because it limits how often the case occurs.

## Reasoning and tool blocks open the right panel (Go, 2026-05-20)

T-106, frontend only. A reasoning or tool block no longer expands inline; it opens the pane's `FloatingPanel` through a new `activity` entry of `ConversationPanelId`, so one `panel` state still holds the single open panel.

### Left open

- **No live browser check.** Typecheck and `npm test` ran (only the pre-existing `composer-triggers` failure); layout, focus return and scrolling in a real browser were not exercised. Close it by opening a conversation with a tool call and a reasoning block and trying click, Enter, Space, Escape and a text drag.
- **A reasoning block loses its link to the open panel when its live message is committed.** Its panel key is `scope:reasoning:index` and the live message's scope (`live`) differs from the committed id, so the panel keeps the last streamed text instead of following to the committed copy. Tool blocks key on `callId` and follow. Closing it needs a message id on the live message.
- **Inline images stay under the tool row** and are not repeated in the panel (the old inline expand did not show them either).
- **`message.tools` (legacy `ChatToolCalls defaultIsExpanded` in `AssistantMessage`) and the run inspector's `ChatToolCalls` still expand inline.** They are not the transcript's reasoning/tool blocks from parts; changing them was out of scope.

T-107, frontend only. A tool group with two or more calls is one `ChatToolCalls` summary row again, and it opens an `ActivityDetail` of kind `tools` that lists every call. Its key is `toolGroupActivityKey(scope, firstPartIndex)`.

### Left open

- **`npm run build` was not run.** The repo rules forbid it for this agent; typecheck and `npm test` ran instead. Closing it needs someone to run the build.
- **No live browser check** of the grouped row, the panel list, Escape, focus return or live updates while a group streams.

T-108, frontend only; it replaces the grouped panel of T-107.

## Subagent transcript in the right panel: backend field not emitted yet (Go, 2026-05-20)

T-110 (frontend only). No payload carried a subagent's steps: the native subagent tool result only has `information {key:"transcript", value:"<path>/messages.jsonl"}`, a `run` id and the final answer, `webui.Part` has no subagent field, and the frontend never fetches a child transcript. The frontend now accepts an optional field on a `tool_result` part and shows it below the result in `ToolCallDetail`; absent, the panel is unchanged. Still open: the backend does not emit it. Expected JSON on the `tool_result` part:

```json
{"type":"tool_result","callId":"…","output":"…","status":"success",
 "subagent":{"agent":"researcher",
  "steps":[{"id":"s1","status":"completed",
            "parts":[{"type":"text","text":"…"},{"type":"reasoning","text":"…"},
                     {"type":"tool_call","callId":"…","name":"…","arguments":"{…}"}]}]}}
```

`agent` is optional. `steps[].status` must be `completed` for a step to render; any other value (`running`, `pending`, …) is ignored, as are steps with no parts. `parts` use the existing webui part shapes; only text, reasoning and tool_call parts render (tool_result parts inside a step are accepted but not shown; T-112 now keeps and shows them, paired with their calls). The snapshot is one-shot: no streaming, no polling. A malformed `subagent` is dropped by the frontend and the `tool_result` part stays (T-111). Also open: no real-browser check; `npm run build` not run.

## Subagent snapshot on the tool_result part: how it is built and what is still open (Go, 2026-05-20)

T-111. The backend now emits the `subagent` field T-110 documented, from the child's own `messages.jsonl`.

**Where.** `webui.Part.Subagent` (`*SubagentSnapshot{agent, steps[{id,status:"completed",parts}]}`, shared and never edited after it is built). `webui/subagent_snapshot.go` holds the pure part: `SubagentBuilder` (one `response.completed` line is one step, normalized by `NormalizeTranscript`, so no second transcript parser), `AttachSubagents`, `SubagentCallOf`, and the caps. `app/subagent_snapshot.go` reads the file, resolves the folder and caches. Replay attaches in `readMessagePage`, `historyResponse` and `ReadSessionMessage`, after normalizing and before `CapMessages`; live attaches in `publishToolResult` through the `subagentSnapshotter` interface that `boundRunner` implements.

**Discriminator.** The tool name of the sibling `tool_call` part, `subagent` or `native.subagent`. `profile_message` prints the same `transcript:` line and never gets the field. A result with no tool_call part (orphan) gets none.

**Resolution.** The folder `<storage_dir>/<sanitised call id>-<8 hex>` is found by name, because the 8 hex characters are random and a manifest is keyed by `sub_...`, not by call id. If no folder or more than one carries the call id, the `transcript:` line of the result is used. Either way the path is resolved with `EvalSymlinks` and must be `messages.jsonl` in a folder directly under the real storage dir, never `.runs`; anything else gives no field and no error. The storage dir is the session config's `tools.native.subagent.storage_dir`, so a call made under another profile's storage is not found.

**Caps** (`webui.DefaultSubagentLimits`, `app.defaultSubagentSnapshotLimits`): 32 steps (the first ones; the scan stops there), 12 parts per step, 2048 bytes per text/reasoning/arguments (cut at a UTF-8 boundary, marked with `truncated.bytes`), 48 KiB of text in all, lines over 1 MiB skipped, 16 MiB scanned at most. `CapMessages` also cuts snapshot text to `max_inline_part_bytes` and gives the message its cursor. Only text, reasoning and tool_call parts are kept (T-112: and the result of each kept call); a response whose `status` is present and not `completed` adds no step; an incomplete last line is never read.

**Cache.** Process-wide LRU of 256 snapshots keyed by storage dir, call id, agent and transcript hint. A found transcript is valid while its size and mtime are unchanged; a missing one is remembered while the storage dir's mtime is unchanged. A hit costs one stat.

**Measured** (Ryzen 5 3600, `go test -bench SubagentSnapshot ./internal/app`): a 7-round child with 14 KB results, 252 us cold, 1.6 us cached; a 40 MB hostile child 2.9 ms (stops at the scan cap). The real child of call `toolu_011fhsQnew2oxCCw45f8GbbB` gives 7 steps, 8.8 KB of JSON, 0.9 ms cold.

**Cost on other paths.** Fork (`messageTurnCursor`) and the inline image route call `NormalizeTranscript` and never attach: no cost. The snapshot, history and one-message routes pay one stat per subagent tool result when warm, and one folder listing plus one bounded read when cold.

Still open:

- The limits are constants, not server settings (no `sample/schemes` key). Closing it needs a `server.*` block with defaults and tests.
- A child still running when a result is served (a background call, `block_ms: 0`) shows the steps written so far. The live message does not change as the child goes on, and the snapshot and history ETags come from the parent's files, so a browser holding them gets 304 and does not see later steps until the parent changes. An open panel needs a history refetch. Closing it needs the child's stamp in the ETag, or an event.
- A child that compacted its own transcript shows only the steps still in the file.
- A call id carried by two folders gives no field unless the result's `transcript:` line names one.
- A remote subagent (`subagent_remote.go`) has no local transcript and gets none.
- The live snapshot is read synchronously inside `publishToolResult`, on the runner's tool-result callback; it is bounded (about 1 ms real, 3 ms worst case) but not asynchronous.
- Not checked in a real browser or against the running server (it was not restarted).

## Subagent snapshot renders as a read-only conversation, calls paired with results (Go, 2026-11-08)

T-112. The right panel shows the snapshot as a conversation again: reasoning and tool calls are rows that expand in place, text is Markdown, and the same renderer is meant for a future transcript reader.

**Wire change** (backend, same `subagent` field, no new key). A step now holds `tool_result` parts next to their `tool_call` parts, in the shape the conversation uses (`type`, `callId`, `output`, `status`, `truncated`), so the browser pairs them by call id. `images`, `inlineImages` and a nested `subagent` are never set on them. A step is completed when its `response.completed` record is written and every call it kept has its result; when the child has moved on (the next response or a user message) a step is reported with the results it has. The last step of a child that is still running a tool is left out until its results are written: `SubagentBuilder.Snapshot` never reports it. `MaxPartsPerStep` (12) counts text, reasoning and tool_call parts only; the results of kept calls come on top, so a step holds at most 24 parts. A call over the part limit is dropped with its result.

**Caps, checked against the real child** of call `toolu_011fhsQnew2oxCCw45f8GbbB` (7 steps, 6 calls, outputs of 25, 23, 9, 5, 11 and 4 KB). JSON of the served snapshot: 22,571 bytes with a 2 KiB result cap, **35,002 bytes with 4 KiB** (7 steps, 19 parts, 6 results, 5 of them cut and marked `truncated`), 53,007 with 8 KiB; the 48 KiB total never bound at any of them. Decision: results get their own cap `MaxResultBytes` = 4096 (text, reasoning and arguments stay 2048; total 48 KiB; 32 steps unchanged). 2 KiB cut most outputs before the part that matters; 8 KiB pushes one subagent call past 50 KB of JSON for a seven-round child. Worst case one snapshot is the 48 KiB text budget plus JSON framing, and the cache holds 256 of them, so the cache stays in the low tens of MB. The 4 KiB value is a constant like the others (see the T-111 gap about configuration).

**Frontend.** `components/ReadOnlyTranscript.tsx` (+ pure `read-only-transcript.ts`) is the shared renderer: `<ReadOnlyTranscript messages={ReadOnlyMessage[]} rowWidthPx? />`, `ReadOnlyMessage = {key, role: 'user'|'assistant', parts: WebUiPart[], steps?}`; the API is documented in the file headers. It reuses the conversation's grouping (moved unchanged to `web-ui-part-groups.ts`, which the live `WebUiParts` now imports), `toolCallItem`, `ToolCallDetail`, `ReasoningDetail` and the Astryx tool-call row, whose own chevron expands a row in place (Enter and Space work on it) and folds several calls into one counted group row. It registers nothing with the pane's panel (no `ActivityRow`, no `ActivityPanelContext`), so a click opens, replaces and closes nothing. The snapshot is one caller: `SubagentTranscript` maps the completed steps to one assistant message with a step range per step (so tool runs end at step boundaries and rows of different steps stack tight, as live) under the "Subagent transcript · <agent>" heading. `ToolCallDetail` gained a `children` slot instead of knowing the snapshot; `ActivityPanel` passes `SubagentTranscript` there, and the inline detail of a read-only row passes nothing, so snapshots never nest. The live conversation is unchanged apart from two mechanical extractions (`reasoningCallItem`, the grouping move) and three newly exported helpers.

**Checked.** Unit tests for the builder (pairing, order, stray and duplicate results, withheld running step, result cap, part cap, byte budget, `CapMessages` on results), the served-JSON fixture test (paired calls in a real run), `read-only-transcript.test.mjs` (selection, pairing, step boundaries, scoped keys, completed-only mapping, and a source-level pin that the renderer reaches no panel code). The real snapshot (35,002 bytes) passes `validSubagentTranscript` and yields 6 of 6 paired calls in 12 rows. A throwaway jsdom harness outside the repo (jsdom installed in `/tmp`, vite `ssrLoadModule`, then deleted) mounted `ToolCallDetail` + `SubagentTranscript` under a counting `ActivityPanelContext`: a click and Enter each expanded a row in place and the panel control was called 0 times; a two-call step showed one counted group row that expanded to its calls; a running step's text was absent.

Still open:

- No real browser look. The browser tool cannot reach a local dev server (SOCKS error), so spacing, the narrow-panel width, the expanded code blocks and the user-bubble branch of the shared renderer (the snapshot only uses `assistant`) are unseen. Closing it: open a session with the subagent call in the restarted UI.
- `npm run build` was not run (forbidden for this role; it also rewrites `src/themes/neutral/theme.css`). `go test ./...` was not run either; the permitted scope `./internal/... ./cmd/...` was.
- The tool and result code blocks inside an expanded row keep their own max-height scroll (`tool_arguments_max_height_px`, `tool_result_max_height_px`) as in the live panel, so an expanded row can scroll inside the panel's own scroll; T-110 asked for no nested scroll area for the section itself, which still holds when rows are collapsed. Closing it needs a decision to lift the heights in this renderer.
- A cut result (4 KiB) has no "Load full": a snapshot has no cursor, so the notice shows only "Truncated for transport · N KB total". Reading the full child output stays in the child's transcript.
- A result record over 1 MiB is skipped by the line scanner, so its call stays unpaired: the step is withheld while it is the last one in the file and is shown without that result once the child moved on. A call without a result renders settled (never running); the renderer cannot tell it from a call whose result was cut by the part limit.
- Tool-result images of the child are not shown (the server strips them; the renderer would not resolve them against the parent's session).
- The T-111 gaps stand unchanged: constants instead of settings, a still-running child is not refreshed in an open panel (and a 304 hides later steps), compacted children, duplicate call-id folders, remote subagents, the synchronous live read.

## T-113: subagent transcript behind "Show Transcript", child summary not repeated (Go, 2026-11-08)

Frontend only; no Go touched. The right panel shows a `Show Transcript` / `Hide Transcript` button below the tool result, closed on every open (`ActivityPanel` keys `SubagentTranscript` by the call's `detail.key`, so a switch to another call resets it and closing unmounts it). `subagentTranscriptMessages` drops the trailing completed step's text parts when the snapshot has a completed tool call, and drops that step if nothing is left; a tool-less snapshot is shown whole. The shared `ReadOnlyTranscript` and the backend payload are unchanged.

Still open:

- No DOM in the frontend suite and the browser tool could not reach a local dev server earlier (T-112), so the disclosure (closed default, toggle on click, reset on switching calls) is pinned at the source plus mutation-checked, not exercised in a rendered page. Closing it: a component test with a DOM, or a look in a real browser.
- `aria-expanded` is passed to the Astryx `Button`; it type-checks but whether the component forwards it to the DOM was not checked.
- The rule takes the last *completed* step as the trailing one. If a snapshot ever ends with a still-running step, the last completed step's text would be hidden although it is not the child's summary; today the snapshot is served only for a finished child (T-112), so this does not occur.
- A trailing step that mixes text with tool calls loses its text (e.g. "running this now"): accepted by the rule, not distinguished from a summary.

## T-114: subagent transcript costs nothing until it is opened (Go, 2026-11-09)

The user's "disable" means cost, not visibility: many parallel subagents must not slow the page. Before, every transcript payload carried the whole child (35,002 bytes of `subagent` for the real jad/test call `toolu_011fhsQnew2oxCCw45f8GbbB`, 7 steps) and `SubagentTranscript` validated and mapped it on every render, closed or not.

**Wire.** `tool_result.subagent` is now a descriptor, `webui.SubagentSummary` = `{"agent"?: string, "steps": <completed step count>}`, no step parts. Measured on the real child (`readSubagentSteps` on `.jad/state/jad/jad/test/tools/.subagents`): the field was **35,008 bytes**, is now **29 bytes** (`{"agent":"explore","steps":7}`). Replay (`AttachSubagents`, in `readMessagePage`, `historyResponse`, `ReadSessionMessage`) and live (`publishToolResult`) both take the summary from the same child reader (`SubagentSnapshot.Summary()` on what `readSubagentSteps` returned). No snapshot, or one with no completed step, leaves the part untouched. `CapMessages` no longer has anything to cut there (`capSubagent`/`subagentEqual` removed; `partsEqual` compares the summary by value). Nothing else in the payload changed.

**Route.** `GET /api/sessions/{reference}/subagents/{callId}[?profile=<profile>]` (`httpapi/subagent_handler.go`, optional interface `SessionSubagents`, implemented by `app.Resolver.ReadSubagentSnapshot`).
- 200 `{"subagent": {"agent"?: string, "steps": [{"id", "status": "completed", "parts": [...]}]}}`: the `webui.SubagentSnapshot` the T-112 builder makes, same caps (`DefaultSubagentLimits`, 1 MiB line, 16 MiB scan), so `validSubagentTranscript` applies unchanged. The inline-part cut (`max_inline_part_bytes`) is not applied; the builder caps (2 KiB text, 4 KiB results) are lower than any sane setting.
- The child folder is found by the call id alone (`<sanitised call id>-<8 hex>` under the profile's `tools.native.subagent.storage_dir`, same confinement as before); the result's `transcript:` hint is not used. A child still running gives the completed steps so far, and `{"steps":[]}` before its first. The answer is `Cache-Control: no-store`; the server cache (256 snapshots, validated by size+mtime) makes a repeat read one stat.
- 404 `{"error":"subagent transcript not found"}` for an unknown call id, a session without subagent storage, or a call id that two folders carry. 501 when the resolver cannot read subagents. `agent` is not in the answer (the call id alone does not say which agent ran); the browser keeps the name from the summary.
- Tests: `TestSubagentToolResultCarriesOnlyASummaryAndTheRouteServesTheSteps` (real run: live and served payload carry only the summary, route returns the child's 2 steps with paired results, unknown id is 404), `TestSubagentRouteReadsAChildThatIsStillRunning` (200, empty steps), `TestReadSubagentStepsSaysWhetherTheChildWasFound` (found without a step, not found, torn last line, growth), `TestSubagentRoute*` in httpapi (route, profile, decoded call id, no-store, 401, GET only, 501), webui tests for the summary, `CapMessages` and `partsEqual`. The real call id returns the full 7-step snapshot through the same reader (35,021-byte body incl. wrapper).

**A bug found on the way** (T-111 code, hit by "works while the child is running"): a running child creates its folder before its `messages.jsonl`, and creating the file does not change the storage directory's mtime, so a read in between cached the call as absent and hid the child until the cache evicted it (the first race run of the running-child test failed on it). `readSubagentSteps` now answers (nil, found) without caching when the call's folder exists but has no transcript yet; `TestChildFolderWithoutItsTranscriptYetIsNotRememberedAsAbsent` fails without it (mutation CAUGHT).

**Frontend.** `SubagentTranscript` renders one `Show Transcript · N steps` button while closed (label from `summary.steps`; `validSubagentSummary` is three field reads): no request, no step validation, no `subagentTranscriptMessages`, no `ReadOnlyTranscript`. The work is in `OpenSubagentTranscript`, mounted only while open: the first open fetches `fetchSubagentTranscript(sessionRef, callId, profile)` through `subagent-transcript-store.ts`, shows "Loading transcript…", then the shared `ReadOnlyTranscript` with the T-113 summary drop; a failure shows the message and a Retry button (a failure is not cached). The store is an LRU of `subagent_transcript_cache_entries` (16, `config.json`) keyed by session, profile, call id **and step count**, with one shared in-flight request per key; hiding keeps the data, reopening reads it. A newer summary (the child finished more steps, seen after a history refetch) is a new key, which is the cache invalidation. `ActivityPanel` got `sessionRef` and `profile` from `ViewPane` (it sits outside the image-scope provider) and keeps `key={detail.key}`, so a switch of call or a reopen starts closed. No prefetch, no timer.

**Checked in a real render.** A throwaway jsdom + vite harness outside the repo (deleted): closed = one button, 0 requests, no heading; open = 1 request to `/api/sessions/jad/subagents/toolu_1?profile=jad%2Ftest`, heading with the agent, the child's rows rendered and its final summary text absent; hide removes the content; reopen = still 1 request; a 500 shows "Could not load the transcript: nope" with Retry, and Retry fetches again and renders. The repo suite has no DOM, so the committed tests pin the same at the source plus the store and client in isolation.

Still open:

- Not run against the live server (it was not restarted), so the new route and the 29-byte payload are checked by the Go integration tests and the harness above, not in the user's browser. Closing it: restart, open the jad/test session, press Show Transcript on the subagent call.
- The old step-less behaviour "no button when nothing survives" cannot hold: whether anything survives is known only after the fetch. A child whose only completed step is its summary now shows the button and, once opened, "No steps to show." Closing it needs the backend to count only the steps the browser will show (apply the summary-drop rule server-side), which the T-113 constraint kept in the frontend.
- A running child's summary count is fixed when the result was served (live) or read (history). An open transcript is not refreshed (no polling); a refetched history gives a newer count and so a new key, and the button label then shows the new count. The route itself always reads the file as it is now, so a reopen after that sees the newer steps.
- The route does not check that the call id belongs to the session's transcript, only that a child folder of that name exists in the profile's storage; the storage is per profile, so this exposes nothing the session's own tool results do not already name. Two folders with one call id are a 404 here, as the summary also finds none then (unless the result's transcript hint names one: then the summary shows a count and the route answers 404; the browser shows the error with Retry).
- The agent name is only in the summary (call id alone cannot give it), so a transcript opened from a tool result whose summary lacks `agent` shows the heading without a name.
- The caches stay constants/threads as T-111 (limits are not server settings); the T-111/T-112 gaps about compacted children, remote subagents and the synchronous live read are unchanged. The T-113 gaps about the trailing-step rule also stand.
- The step count on the button is the number of completed steps the child had; the view then drops the child's closing summary (T-113), so a child with a tool call shows one step fewer than the label says, and a child whose steps are all the summary shows "No steps to show.". Closing it: the same server-side count as above.

## T-115: review of the subagent transcript (270ee58c..946901aa), four defects fixed (Go, 2026-11-08)

Reviewed the five commits end to end: the live summary (`publishToolResult`), the replay attach (`AttachSubagents` from `readMessagePage`, `historyResponse`, `ReadSessionMessage`), `CapMessages`, the route (`Handler.route` → `handleSubagent` → `Resolver.ReadSubagentSnapshot` → `readSubagentSteps`), path confinement, the frontend validators, store, `SubagentTranscript`, `ReadOnlyTranscript` and the pairing/grouping helpers.

**Fixed.**

- *An output-less tool result took the whole transcript down.* `webui.Part.Output` is `omitempty`, so a tool that printed nothing gives `{"type":"tool_result","callId":…,"status":…}` with no `output` (pinned in `TestSubagentBuilderKeepsAResultThatPrintedNothing`, `internal/webui/webui_test.go:348`). `isWebUiPart` required a string, so `validSubagentTranscript` rejected the snapshot and the panel showed "invalid subagent transcript"; on the live stream the same check silently dropped a `ui.part` / `ui.message` carrying such a result (main conversation too). Now `isWebUiPart` reads a missing output as `''` (`frontend_new/src/api.ts:1671`), and `validSubagentTranscript` (`api.ts:1610`) drops a malformed part from its step instead of rejecting the snapshot (a malformed frame or step is still no transcript). Tests: `subagent-transcript-store.test.mjs:54`, `api-stream.test.mjs:984`; both mutation CAUGHT.
- *An unreadable child answered 404 "not found".* `readSubagentSteps` returned (nil, false) on open, stat, non-regular-file and scan errors, so the route said "subagent transcript not found" for a child that exists. It now returns `(snapshot, found, err)` (`internal/app/subagent_snapshot.go:178`): a file removed between resolve and open is still "not found", any other read failure is an error, never cached; `ReadSubagentSnapshot` (`:130`) returns it, so the client gets the generic 500 `internal server error` (no path; the cause goes to the request's recorded cause) and the panel shows it with Retry. The replay/live summary path still ignores the error (no summary, as before). Tests: `TestReadSubagentStepsTellsAnUnreadableChildFromAMissingOne` (`subagent_snapshot_test.go:299`), `TestSubagentRouteSaysAnUnreadableChildIsAnErrorNotMissing` (`subagent_snapshot_integration_test.go:190`, real run, asserts 500 and no file name in the body); both mutation CAUGHT.
- *`validSubagentSummary` was exported but unused.* History bodies are not validated part by part, and `SubagentTranscript` only checked `typeof summary.steps === 'number'` (a `null` summary would throw, a 0 or 1.5 count would render). The guard now uses `validSubagentSummary(summary)` (`frontend_new/src/components/SubagentTranscript.tsx:45`); pinned at the source (`subagent-transcript-store.test.mjs`, closed-section test), mutation CAUGHT.

- *The trailing-text rule hid real content whenever the tool result did not hold the child's last text whole* (two follow-ups, authorised by the reviewer). `shownSubagentSteps` dropped the last completed step's text whenever any step had a tool call, on the T-113 assumption that the text is the copy the tool result already shows. **Fixed** at `frontend_new/src/subagent-transcript.ts:53`: the text is kept when the result's summary says `omitsLastText`. **Field semantics:** `omitsLastText` is true when the tool result does not hold, whole, the text of the child's last completed step; absent (false) when it does, and from an older server. **Mechanism:** the tool result's own INFORMATION section, read on the server where `agent`/`transcript` already are. `subagentInformation` (`internal/webui/subagent_snapshot.go:148`) also reads `state`, `error_code` and `truncated`; `SubagentCallOf` sets `SubagentCall.OmitsLastText` (`:108`) by `state != "" || truncated || (error_code != "" && error_code != "subagent_timeout")` (`:135`); `SubagentSummaryOf` (`:51`) copies it to `SubagentSummary.OmitsLastText` (`json:"omitsLastText,omitempty"`, `:36`). The live path (`internal/httpapi/executor.go:347`) and the replay (`AttachSubagents`, `subagent_snapshot.go:211`) both build the summary there, so they cannot differ. The browser validates it as a boolean (`frontend_new/src/api.ts:1590`; anything else drops the summary) and `SubagentTranscript` passes it on (`components/SubagentTranscript.tsx:62`, `:119`). **Why that rule** (native `renderRun`, `internal/tools/native/subagent_call.go:341-428`): a finished result's content is the final response's text (`internal/app/subagent.go:130`), and a stopped or timed-out one's is the newest text of a committed response (`app/subagent.go:128`); the runner writes a result for every call a cancelled or timed-out round leaves (`internal/session/runner.go:1121`), so that response's step closes and is the snapshot's last step with text: leaving it out is deduplication. Every other result omits it: queued/running names `state` and holds a run id (`subagent_call.go:351`); a cut answer names `truncated: true` (`:420` finished, `:285` stopped/timed out/lost), so the result holds only its head; failed holds only the reason although the child's last text exists (`subagent_call.go:398` drops the `lastText` that `app/subagent.go:128` kept); empty holds none; lost (`subagent_manager.go:159`, a run still going when the server died) holds the newest text of any `response.completed` line (`subagent_manager.go:516`), which can be a step whose tool results were never written and which the builder therefore withholds (`Snapshot`, `subagent_snapshot.go` builder), so its shown last text is not the result's. A state or an error code this rule does not name counts as omitting (text kept): a repeat, never a loss; a result with no INFORMATION section is not a native subagent result and keeps the T-113 rule. **Rejected:** a "child finished" flag on the route body (the route describes the child, not the result: a handed-off child that later finished would lose its answer again, and the flag would need the run manifest, keyed by run id, kept out of the size+mtime cache); matching the trailing text against the result's output (two encodings of one text); a structural "no CONTENT section" check (post-edit hooks can append a content section to a result, `internal/tools/native/hooks.go:53`). **Per state** (frontend `subagent-transcript.test.mjs`; the T-113 tests are kept, renamed "finished: …"): finished — trailing text left out ("finished: …" tests); finished and cut — kept ("finished and cut: …"); stopped / timed out — left out ("stopped and timed out: …"); stopped / timed out and cut — kept (same flag as finished and cut); lost — kept ("lost: …"); failed — kept ("failed: …"); handed off, still running — kept ("still running: …"); handed off, ended later — kept ("handed off: …"). Go: `TestRenderedSubagentResultsSayWhetherTheyOmitTheChildsLastText` (`internal/tools/native/subagent_render_webui_test.go`; real `renderRun` output of all thirteen state variants through `webui.SubagentCallOf`), `TestSubagentSummarySaysWhetherTheResultOmitsTheLastText` (`internal/webui/webui_test.go:443`; replay via `AttachSubagents`, `state:`/`truncated:` inside the content ignored, JSON omits the field when false), `TestLiveSubagentResultSaysWhetherItOmitsTheLastText` (`internal/httpapi/live_subagent_test.go:75`; handed off, finished, finished and cut). Mutations CAUGHT: `truncated` dropped from the rule (each of the three Go tests alone); the error-code clause dropped; `subagent_timeout` no longer excepted; the `truncated` value never read; the flag not copied in `SubagentSummaryOf`; `Summary()` instead of `SubagentSummaryOf` in `publishToolResult`; the flag check removed from `shownSubagentSteps` (each of the cut, failed and lost frontend tests alone); the validator's boolean check removed; the prop forced to `false` in `SubagentTranscript.tsx`. Contract: the route body and `webui.SubagentSnapshot` are unchanged, so the Go snapshot cache (keyed by root, call id, agent, hint; size+mtime) holds nothing new; the flag is not in the frontend store key, since it changes only the mapping. An older client ignores the field; a newer client against an older server keeps the T-113 rule. The `handedOff` field of the first follow-up was never committed and is replaced, not kept beside it.

**Checked, not a defect.** Live and replay give the same `{agent?, steps}` shape from the same reader and `SubagentCallOf`; the completed message keeps the part (`mergeCompletedResponse` never replaces a tool_result); `AttachSubagents` copies before it writes; `CapMessages` keeps the summary. The route's cache key (call id only) differs from the summary's (agent + transcript hint), which costs a second read of the same file, never different steps. A URL call id is only compared, sanitised, against `ReadDir` names; the joined folder comes from the listing and `confinedTranscript` checks the real path; the route passes no transcript hint. React keys are positional (`scope:call-<index>`), so a repeated call id cannot collide; pairing stays inside a step. Closed = one button and no request; a failed load shows Retry and is not cached; the store key carries the step count; a load that resolves after unmount or a key change is ignored by `isCurrent` and still cached.

Still open:

- *Trailing-text rule: a snapshot whose last step is not the child's last response* (**closed by T-116**, below, with the mechanism and its residuals; this is the T-115 analysis, line numbers as of 0c15536d). Under a result that holds the child's last text (finished, stopped, timed out; no `omitsLastText`), `shownSubagentSteps` (`frontend_new/src/subagent-transcript.ts:53`) still drops the text of the snapshot's last step in two cases where that step is not the one the result's text came from, so that step's text is shown nowhere: (1) the snapshot stopped early at `MaxSteps` (32, `internal/webui/subagent_snapshot.go:397`) or the byte budget (`:362-366`, `:420-424`), so step 32 (or the step the budget ran out in) loses its text although the child went on; (2) a stop or deadline that ends the run after a response with tool calls is committed but before its tool round writes any result: `recordBudget` (`internal/session/runner.go:789`) or automatic compaction (`:792`) returning an error ends the run without outputs, the builder withholds that step, and the result's text is that step's while the snapshot's last step is an earlier one (whether a stop during automatic compaction does surface as such an error was not traced). Why it was not closed then: only the snapshot reader knows that its last step is not the child's newest response (the builder ended on a limit, or with a pending step), and this round keeps `webui.SubagentSnapshot` and the route body unchanged. Closing it, as proposed then: let the reader report that bit next to the snapshot (not in the route body) and have `SubagentSummaryOf` set `omitsLastText` from it. T-116 replaced that proposal (see its first point).
- *A live handed-off result often has no button until reload.* `publishToolResult` reads the child when the handoff is served, usually before its first completed step, so the summary is nil; the replay attaches one later. Unchanged T-114 behaviour (open message not refreshed), noted because it is how the "still running" state is usually reached.
- *Button count vs shown steps* (T-114 note) stands: the label counts completed steps, the view may drop the trailing summary step; the view does not number steps, so the user cannot see the mismatch. Left as documented.
- *Summary found by the transcript hint, route by call id only.* When two folders carry the call id, the summary can show a count (hint) and the route answers 404 (`subagent_snapshot.go:130`, `resolveSubagentTranscript` at `:233`). Closing it needs the route to read the hint from the session's own tool result.
- *Sanitised call ids can collide* (`subagentFolderFor`, `unsafeDirectoryCharacters` in `internal/app/subagent.go:24`): `a.b` and `a_b` name the same folder. Provider call ids use `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` only, so this is theoretical.
- *No client timeout on the transcript fetch* (`fetchSubagentTranscript`, `api.ts:588`), like the other read routes: a request that never settles keeps "Loading transcript…" and the store's shared in-flight promise for that key until reload.
- *History messages still reach the renderer unvalidated*, so an output-less result from history keeps `output` undefined there; only the stream and the transcript route normalise it. Whether `toolResultText` (`frontend_new/src/components/TranscriptParts.tsx:397`) copes with an undefined output was not checked (pre-existing, outside this feature).
- No DOM test and no real-browser look for the panel (T-113/T-114 gaps unchanged); `npm run build` not run (forbidden for this role).

## T-116: the snapshot says when its last step may not be the child's newest response (Go, 2026-11-10)

Closes the T-115 open gap "a snapshot whose last step is not the child's last response". The route body now carries `mayHaveNewer` (`webui.SubagentSnapshot.MayHaveNewer`, `internal/webui/subagent_snapshot.go:32`, `omitempty`), and the panel keeps the trailing text of a snapshot that sets it (`shownSubagentSteps`, `frontend_new/src/subagent-transcript.ts:63`; read from the validated body by `validSubagentTranscript`, `frontend_new/src/api.ts:1634`). Meaning: *a response.completed record follows the last step shown and the snapshot does not show it (or the reader could not give the builder every line), so that step's text may not be the one the tool result holds.* Committed together with its tests as the T-116 commit (Go and node tests only; no DOM or real-browser check).

**Decisions.**

- *Route body, not summary.* This replaces the T-115 proposal to route the bit through `SubagentSummaryOf` and `omitsLastText`. `omitsLastText` is a fact about the result (`SubagentCallOf`, from the result's own INFORMATION); the new bit is a fact about one read of the child's file. Folding it in would give one flag two meanings and move the decision to summary time, where a handed-off or still-growing child can differ from the route read. In the body, one read gives the steps and the bit together, the cache entry (size + mtime) covers it with no new field, and `SubagentSummary`, `SubagentSummaryOf`, the live path and the replay are unchanged (a test pins that `Summary()` and the served summary keep two keys). `omitempty` keeps the body of an ordinary child byte-identical.
- *Failure direction: never hide text.* Duplicated text is accepted and documented; wherever exact detection costs too much the bit over-reports.
- *Exact look-ahead, bounded, over a conservative flag at a limit.* A snapshot at `MaxSteps` or the byte budget used to stop reading (`Add` returned false). A conservative "stopped at a limit, so true" would show the text twice for every child that ends exactly at 32 steps or in its last response's budget; the look-ahead costs the lines up to the first response.completed after the limit (or EOF), parsing only lines that contain `response.completed`. It never reads more than the same file read to EOF, which the reader already bounds by `maxScan` (16 MiB) for a child under the limits, and the cache (size + mtime) means it is paid once per change of the file, not per poll. `Add`'s contract is now "false once a limit is reached *and* a newer response was seen"; `TestSubagentBuilderStopsAtItsLimits` pins both halves.

**Per case** (E = exact, C = conservative, i.e. may show a text twice):

- *`MaxSteps`* (`closePending`, `subagent_snapshot.go:444-447`): E. The look-ahead sets the bit on the next response.completed; EOF leaves it false.
- *Byte budget* (`keep` `:472`, `addResult` `:412`): E for the same reason. A response the budget leaves with no part is a refused response (next line).
- *Withheld pending step* (`Snapshot()`, `:458`): E (the step is a response the snapshot drops).
- *Refused newer response* (`addResponse` `:364-381`: limit reached, status not completed, unreadable, nothing to keep): C. Any response.completed record after the last kept step sets the bit, even one that could not have produced the result's text (an `incomplete` record, an empty one). A step added later clears it. A line that names a response and does not parse as a record also sets it.
- *`maxScan` window* (`readSubagentSteps`, `internal/app/subagent_snapshot.go:223-226`): C. A file larger than the window sets the bit whether or not a response lies beyond it; exact would mean reading the rest.
- *Line over `maxLine`* (`native.ScanTranscriptLinesSkipping`, `internal/tools/native/subagent_view.go:26`, plumbed to `SubagentBuilder.Unseen`): C. The skipped line may be a tool output; any skipped line after the last kept step sets the bit; one before a later step does not.
- *Torn last line* of a file still being written: not counted. It is not a record yet, and a running child's result has `omitsLastText`, so its text is kept anyway.

**Same-count flip (cache and store).** The store key is the summary's step count. At one count the bit can only go false to true (a new response after the last step), never back: clearing needs a new kept step, which changes the count and the key. A transcript cached with a stale false could hide text only under a result with `omitsLastText` false, i.e. a finished, stopped or timed-out child, whose file no longer changes, so the cached and the current read agree. A running child's result has `omitsLastText` and shows everything regardless. So no re-map and no key change is needed; `useMemo` depends on `transcript`, which carries the bit.

**Still open.**

- *Duplicated text in two rare settled-looking cases.* A finished, stopped or timed-out child whose file ends with a refused response (an `incomplete` or empty record), and a child that ends exactly at a limit with a response after it that is not its text, show the last step's text twice (in the panel and in the result). Whether the runner ever writes such a trailing record to a stopped child's transcript was not traced; the real finished child in `TestSubagentToolResultCarriesOnlyASummaryAndTheRouteServesTheSteps` does not (its route body has no bit). Closing it: classify the refused response (status incomplete with no text is not a candidate) instead of counting all of them.
- *A file over 16 MiB always sets the bit* (`internal/app/subagent_snapshot.go:223-226`), as does any line over 1 MiB after the last step. Closing it: a bounded tail read, or a longer window.
- *Summary and route can still read different files* (hint vs call id only) and so carry different counts; unchanged (T-115 note).
- No DOM test and no real-browser look for the panel; `npm run build` not run (forbidden for this role).

## T-117: the subagent route says when the child is running, and a handed-off child with no step has a summary (Go, 2026-11-10)

Batch 1 of 2 (backend). The panel will poll the existing route; nothing is pushed on the run stream. Done: `GET .../subagents/{callId}` carries `"running": true` (omitempty) while the session's subagent manager holds a queued or running run for that call id (`SubagentManager.CallRunning`, read at request time, never stored or cached; liveness is read before the steps so a child that ends between the two reads is reported running with its final steps, never finished with missing ones). A finished child's body is unchanged byte for byte. A result that names a run state (`state:` in its information) now always has a summary, `{agent?, steps: 0, omitsLastText: true}` before the child's first step, live (`publishToolResult`) and on replay (`SubagentSummaryOf`, new `SubagentCall.HandedOff`).

Open:
- Batch 2 (frontend) is done and committed together with batch 1: `validSubagentSummary` accepts `steps >= 0`, the panel polls while the child runs, and the store is keyed without the step count (see the next entry).
- A zero-step summary is a fact about the result text, not the disk: a handed-off result whose child was pruned or whose folder was deleted keeps `steps: 0` on replay, and its route answers 404 (the panel shows "not found"). Closing it needs the replay lookup to tell "no folder" from "no step" (readSubagentSteps already does) and SubagentSummaryOf to take that; not done to keep SubagentSummaryOf as the single builder.
- The agent name is absent from a handed-off zero-step summary when the call's arguments do not name it (the hand-off result does not print it). The route's `agent` has the same source.
- The step being written stays withheld until all its tool results are in, so a long tool call (minutes) shows nothing new for that time. Chosen on purpose (decider), not closed.
- Each changed read rescans the child's transcript from offset 0 (no incremental resume), bounded by the existing 16 MiB scan cap; a poll with no change costs one stat. Not measured under a long-running child with a large transcript.
- `running` knows only runs this process holds. After a restart a child that was going is loaded as lost and the field is absent (correct: not live). A remote subagent has no local run or folder here: the route is 404 and there is no `running`.
- Not browser-verified (backend tests only): `TestSubagentRouteFillsInWhileTheParentCallIsUnsettled`, `TestHandedOffChildWithNoStepHasASummaryLiveAndOnReplay`, `TestHandedOffChildHasASummaryBeforeItsFirstStep`, `TestLiveHandedOffResultOfAChildWithNoStepCarriesAZeroStepSummary`, `TestCallRunningAsksOnlyTheRunsTheManagerHolds`.

## T-117 batch 2: the right-panel subagent transcript polls while open (Go, 2026-11-10)

Batch 2 of 2 (frontend, `frontend_new`), committed in one commit with batch 1. This closes the first open item of the batch 1 entry above: `validSubagentSummary` accepts `steps: 0`, the panel polls, and the store key no longer carries the frozen step count. Done: the section shows for a subagent `tool_call` with no result yet (`subagentSectionOf`, gate in `ActivityPanel.tsx`); `subagent-transcript-poll.ts` fetches at once and then every `subagent_transcript_poll_ms` (2000) while the call is unsettled or the last answer said `running: true`, doubles the delay up to `subagent_transcript_poll_max_delay_ms` (15000) after a failed read, pauses in a hidden tab, and sets no timer once the panel is closed; the trailing-text trim applies only to a settled, uncut result of a finished child. Both settings are in `frontend_new/src/config.json`.

Open:
- Not browser-verified: the tests run the poller on a fake clock and read the components as source (no DOM in the suite). A browser check must still confirm that the panel fills in per step while a real child runs, that the "Working on step N+1…" line and the labels read well, that a hidden tab pauses and refetches on show, and that closing the panel stops the requests.
- A closed button cannot know a child is running: a settled summary with steps shows the plain count, and a zero-step summary is labelled "running, no step completed yet" until the panel is opened, even for a handed-off child that ended without a step (the open panel then says "0 steps"). Closing it would need the server to put `running` on the summary, which batch 1 was told not to do.
- An unsettled call whose child has ended and whose parent run never settles it (a stopped or crashed parent) is polled every `subagent_transcript_poll_ms` while the panel is open and visible, one `os.Stat` per poll on a cache hit. It stops when the call gets a result. Not bounded by a count.
- A parent's `steps` in a settled summary that the snapshot can never reach (a limit cut it) triggers one extra read per open, not a loop.
- Left as it was: `frontend_new` `composer-triggers.test.mjs` "every built-in path ... exists" fails before and after this change (MEMORY_DESIGN.md is not in the tree); it is unrelated.
